`shiftNotFinishedSql()` (`> NOW()`) replaces every `event_date >= CURRENT_DATE` in the family: `STAFF_OPEN_SHIFTS_SQL`, `GET /shifts/unstaffed-upcoming`, `badge-counts.unstaffed_events`, the scoped admin feed `adminScopedShiftsSql` (twice, and deliberately: once for the Upcoming bucket, and again inside its own `needs_staff` flag, so the flag still means "staffing is still possible" when it rides a Past row where nothing else supplies the guard), all five shift queries in `GET /api/me/staff-home` (next shift, pending, cover broadcasts, open-shifts teaser, "All (N)" count), `GET /api/messages/shifts`, `outstandingDocuments.listUncertifiedStaffable`, and `smsShiftCommands.findNearestApprovedShift` (re-exported by `smsInbound.js`). **A COUNT and the LIST it counts must import the same fragment** - the badge/modal pair and the teaser/count/Available-tab trio drifted apart twice, each side carrying a comment claiming it mirrored the other, so the pairing is asserted by test (`shifts.visibility.endInstant.test.js`, `staffPortal.staffHome.endInstant.test.js`). Two things stay OUT: `autoAssignScheduler.js` keeps `CURRENT_DATE` because it is an ACTION trigger, not a visibility predicate (converting it moved staff auto-approval SMS to just after midnight Chicago with no quiet-hours gate), and marketing/payroll/calendar windows measured in months or ±days, where a one-day shift is immaterial - the payroll member being `payrollAccrual.js`'s 21-day proposal-recency guard (`maybeReaccrueForDuty`), which is a lookback window and not a "which period is now" question.

**The pay-period LOOKUP was a separate case, and it cost a real defect (2026-08-19).** A pay-period lookup is a DATE boundary, not a shift-visibility one, so no end-instant applies - but it is not immaterial: `GET /api/me/staff-home` selected the current period with `CURRENT_DATE`, the UTC day, so from 19:00 Chicago on a period's LAST evening it resolved the NEXT period while accrual was still writing the current one, and - because `pay_periods` rows are minted lazily by accrual 0 to 4 days INTO their own week - tomorrow's row did not exist, so it matched **zero rows** and the card fell back to the new-hire empty state (*"No payouts yet. Your first payout will appear here after your first shift."*). A months-tenured contractor was told they had never been paid, five hours every period (six in winter). It now passes `chicagoTodayYmd()` as a parameter, matching the rest of the payroll "which period is now" family (`payrollProcessing.findOpenPeriodForDate` via `dutyLines.js`, `admin/payroll.js`). Pinned by `staffPortal.payPeriod.boundary.test.js`, which feeds the shipped predicate the Chicago day and the UTC day and requires them to select different periods - a clock-derived test cannot discriminate here, because the two agree for ~19 hours a day. The Staff hub's subtitle joins that family from the READ side (2026-08-19): `server/utils/staffHubSummary.js` (`summarizeOpenPeriod`, pure) derives the window from `chicagoTodayYmd()` through `payPeriodForDate` and only then LEFT JOINs the `pay_periods` row, so a mid-week request whose row has not been minted yet still names the right dates instead of going blank or falling back to last week.

**The rest of the family: the remaining bare `CURRENT_DATE` reads (2026-08-25).** The end instant answered every SHIFT-visibility question, and the pay-period lookup answered the payroll one, but the sweep stopped there - nine live sites still compared a date column to bare `CURRENT_DATE`, which resolves in the GMT session zone. Each now binds `chicagoTodayYmd()` as a parameter, the same shape the payroll paths use:

- **`contractor_profiles.hire_date`**, stamped in three places (`utils/contractorSeed.js` twice, `routes/admin/users.js` once for the rare no-application direct hire). This one WRITES, and nothing recomputes it: a contractor hired on a weekday evening carried a hire date one day in the future forever, and `GET /users/:id/seniority` derives tenure from it, which orders the roster.
- **The client portal's upcoming/archive split** (`routes/clientPortal.js`, three compares). A client opening their portal on the evening OF their event saw it gone from the focus card and sitting in the archive. The three now share ONE bound value, because they are complements and independent evaluation can drop or duplicate a row. Note the deliberate divergence from the shift rule above: a calendar day IS right here. The end-instant argument is about a write path - a CANT text re-opening a shift somebody already worked - and a client's own portal has none.
- **The autopay claim** (`utils/balanceScheduler.js`). `balance_due_date <= CURRENT_DATE` claimed a balance due TOMORROW during the evening window and charged the card, up to five hours before the date the client agreed to, unattended.
- **`utils/autoAssignScheduler.js`** (the window opened a day early), **`utils/autoAssign.js` + `GET /users/:id/seniority`** (tonight's shift counted as already worked, inflating a ranking - the two are documented mirrors), **`utils/payrollAccrual.js`** (the 21-day re-accrual guard), **`utils/retentionEligibility.js`** (a client booked TONIGHT read as having no upcoming event, so the retention nudge could fire at them), and **`routes/calcom.js`** (tomorrow's date in an auto-created client's note text).

Three sites are deliberately LEFT on `CURRENT_DATE`, and this is a judgement, not an oversight: `routes/calendar.js` (a ±30 and ±365 day window), `utils/marketingAudience.js` (11-to-13-month lapsed windows), and `scripts/staffPaymentImport/generateSeniorityMapping.js` (a one-off import already run). A one-day shift is immaterial at those scales.

Pinned by `contractorSeed.chicagoDay.test.js`, `clientPortal.chicagoDay.test.js` and `balanceScheduler.chicagoDay.test.js`. All three inject the day through a `_deps.today` seam rather than deriving it from the clock, for the reason the pay-period suite already documents: the UTC and Chicago days agree for ~19 hours out of 24, so a clock-derived fixture is green for most of the day against the very bug it exists to catch. The autopay suite seeds `amount_paid == total_price` so `balanceCents <= 0` returns before any Stripe call - this box talks to live Stripe - and its first test is an explicit non-vacuity guard, because `processAutopayCharges` returns early with no Stripe client and every "not charged" assertion would otherwise pass without the SQL running.

**`shifts.status = 'cancelled'` means the EVENT was cancelled - closure must never write it.** The value is overloaded: `client/src/components/adminos/shifts.js` `isCancelledEvent` renders a Cancelled chip off it on the admin `GET /shifts` feed, and `server/routes/calendar.js` turns it into `STATUS:CANCELLED` with a bumped `SEQUENCE`, which strikes the event off the owner's subscribed Google Calendar. Its intended producers are the genuine event-cancel paths (P6 cancel, `shiftReap.js`, `cancel-or-unassign` mode='cancel'). There **was** a fourth, unintended writer: `PUT /shifts/:id` (`server/routes/shifts.handlers.js`) destructured `status` off `req.body` with no allowlist - unlike `equipment_required` / `supply_run` validated a few lines below - and wrote `status = COALESCE($9, status)`. **CLOSED 2026-08-19: the handler now rejects any `status` in the body with a 400.** Two corrections to how this was recorded. (1) "an arbitrary status" overstated it once `shifts_status_check` was re-armed: an out-of-list value raises 23514 and 500s. The real exposure was narrower and worse - a PUT could stamp a **legal** `'cancelled'` on a delivered event, which is exactly the overload described above, so the Events chip and the iCal `STATUS:CANCELLED` would both fire on a shift nobody cancelled. (2) It was rejected outright rather than allowlisted to the four legal values, because nothing sends it: the only two client callers of this route send `equipment_required` and `supply_run`, and every legitimate transition already has an owner (`shiftReap.js` and cancel-or-unassign for `cancelled`, `staffShiftActions.js`/`smsShiftCommands.js` (through `smsInbound.js`) for `open`, the closure sweep for `completed`). Pinned by `shifts.statusGuard.test.js`, whose fourth test asserts cancel-or-unassign still works - a guard that also disarmed the real cancel path would be worse than the bug.

**Closure is a SWEEP, not a completion hook.** `server/utils/shiftClosureSweep.js` runs hourly (`RUN_SHIFT_CLOSURE_SWEEP_SCHEDULER`) and closes a shift when the SHIFT has finished **and** its proposal is `completed`. It is deliberately not hung off the two proposal-completion doors (`balanceScheduler.processEventCompletions`, `PATCH /api/proposals/:id/status`): `shifts.event_date` is independent of `proposals.event_date`, so such a hook needs a date gate, and those doors never re-select an already-completed proposal - a shift the gate skipped was skipped forever. The sweep has no such hole. Terminal status is **always `completed`**, worked or not: a shift nobody was rostered on is an unrecorded ROSTER, a data gap, not a cancellation, and prod holds real paid-in-full events where the owner worked the bar himself. `server/scripts/backfillShiftClosures.js` runs the same query as a dry-run-default report and names those as ROSTER GAPs. `shiftClosureSweep.test.js` pins all of it.

**Losing quotes close out too.** `server/utils/staleProposalSweep.js` runs hourly and archives a proposal whose event date passed more than 48 hours ago while it was still in `draft`/`sent`/`viewed`/`modified`, writing `archive_reason = 'event_passed'` (a value the client portal deliberately does NOT surface, unlike `event_completed`). It reuses `voidUnpaidProposalInvoice`, `reapShiftsForProposal`, the pending-message delete and the post-commit tail from `POST /proposals/:id/archive` so the two archive doors cannot drift. Its date expression uses `AT TIME ZONE event_timezone` and never reads the free-text `event_start_time`.

**`accepted` is exempt, and that exemption is the money guard.** `reconcileProposalPaymentStatus` demotes a fully refunded proposal to `accepted`, so such a row has `amount_paid = 0` and a real payment history; sweeping it would stamp a refunded booking as a lead that never booked. Past-dated `accepted` proposals are emailed to an admin instead, once each, sending before marking so a Resend quota failure cannot silence the notice permanently.

Unlike every sibling, the scheduler is **opt-in** (`RUN_STALE_PROPOSAL_SWEEP_SCHEDULER=true` exactly, not the default-on `enabled()` helper): its first run voids about 105 invoices and makes about 220 live Stripe calls, and `RUN_SCHEDULERS=true` is a documented local pattern. `STALE_PROPOSAL_SWEEP_DRY_RUN=true` logs both candidate lists and writes nothing. A runaway bound (`MAX_ARCHIVES_PER_RUN = 200`) archives nothing and emails an admin if a tick ever selects an implausible number, and the run rethrows on any per-row failure so `wrapScheduler` records `failed` rather than reporting green through a systemic break.

### Admin - `/api/admin` (continued)

- **GET /api/admin/users/:id/seniority** (Admin): Get seniority score, events worked (total, plus the `events_worked_live` / `historical_events_worked` split), tenure
- **PUT /api/admin/users/:id/seniority** (Admin): Update seniority_adjustment, hire_date and historical_events_worked
- **GET /api/admin/payroll/...** (Admin): Payroll surface - contractor payouts, pay periods, and paystub data (`server/routes/admin/payroll.js`, mounted under `/api/admin/payroll`; see the route file for exact paths).
- **POST /api/admin/payroll/payouts/:id/toggle-draw** (Admin): Flip a payout `pending` ⇄ `no_draw` (owner rows: tracked, never owed - spec 2026-08-07). Row-locked like mark-paid; refuses paid payouts, paid periods, and PARKING anyone whose `contractor_profiles.takes_draw` is true (the flag is the server-side authority; un-parking is never gated). Parking the LAST pending payout of a `processing` period is an API-irreversible close, so it 409s unless the body carries `{ confirm_finalize: true }`; with it, the park finalizes the period (same check as mark-paid). Audit-logged as `payout_toggle_draw`; audit runs after client release (one-connection rule). Returns `{ payout, period_status }`.
- **POST /api/admin/payroll/duty-lines** (Admin): Create a manual duty line `{ payout_id, kind, amount_cents, shift_id?, note? }` (`origin='admin'`, admin-owned; EVENT kinds only - review money flows exclusively from the reviews surface so its idempotency keys exist; nonzero integer cents within +/-100000; shift must sit in the payout's period; NULL-shift duplicates explicitly refused; frozen when payout/period paid or processing). Every duty mutation audit-logs and recomputes the payout total via the single writer. (`server/routes/admin/payrollDuty.js`)
- **PATCH /api/admin/payroll/duty-lines/:id** (Admin): Edit amount/note; an AMOUNT edit stamps `admin_owned` (derivation never overwrites) and may set $0 (distinct from removal); any PATCH confirms a held line; note-only edits leave `admin_owned` alone.
- **POST /api/admin `/payroll/duty-lines/:id/remove` / `.../restore`** (Admin): The pay-time checkbox. An admin removal is FINAL to derivation (never resurrected); restore returns the stored amount.
- **PUT /api/admin/payroll/duty-attributions** (Admin): Set/move who gets an attributed duty (`bar_rental` / `equipment_supplies` / `hosted_supplies` / `menu_print`). Validates against the current approved roster, then re-derives via accrual so a first-time attribution materializes its line and a move re-homes the money with both totals recomputed.
- **GET /api/admin/payroll/periods/:id/unattributed-duties** (Admin): What blocks Process: missing attributions plus stale ones (attributed user off the roster), with display names + proposal labels for the attribution modal. The process endpoint 409s (plain message) while this list is non-empty.
- **GET/POST/PATCH /api/admin `/staff-reviews`, `/staff-reviews/:id`, `/:id/confirm`, `/:id/dismiss`** (Admin): Review log (spec 2026-08-06 §7): TT-webhook-ingested + manual google rows, credit tagging, confirm materializes the $10 `review_bounty` per credited 5-star staffer (and clears system removals so dismiss-then-reconfirm re-pays), dismiss refuses while a bounty is paid. All mutations audit-log. The LIST envelope also carries `bounty_cents` (the exported `REVIEW_BOUNTY_CENTS`, so no client holds a money literal), `bounties_paid_cents` (all-time sum of `review_bounty` duty lines with `removed_at IS NULL`, the payable filter) and `total_logged` (all-time row count; the list itself is capped). (`server/routes/admin/staffReviews.js`)
- **GET/POST /api/admin `/staff-reviews/leaderboard?quarter=` / `/staff-reviews/contest-award`** (Admin): Quarterly contest: rate = confirmed named 5-stars over events worked (approved + not dropped, event date in quarter, bounded at today), floor 4 events + 2 reviews, server-computed winners/shares; award splits the $100 pot, idempotent via `UNIQUE(contest_quarter, contractor_id)`, refuses an in-progress quarter without `force`. (`server/routes/admin/staffReviewsContest.js`, mounted before the log router)
- **GET /api/admin/payroll/contractors/:userId/payment-history** (Admin): Imported pre-OS payment history for one contractor (`staff_payment_history` only, never `legacy_cc_payouts`) + `blended_total_cents` = ledger sum + PAID payouts. (`server/routes/admin/payrollTax.js`)
- **GET /api/admin/payroll/tax-totals** (Admin): Per-person 1099 calendar-year totals: ledger by `paid_on` year + payouts by `paid_at` year (constructive receipt, never `payday`), plus per-platform breakdown and `exclude_from_1099`. Year defaults to current; validated 2024–2100.
- **PATCH /api/admin/payroll/tax-totals/:userId/exclude** (Admin): Toggle `users.exclude_from_1099` (foreign contractors on W-8BEN); body `{ exclude: boolean }`.
- **GET /api/me/payment-history** (Staff (self)): The logged-in staffer's imported pre-OS history + blended all-time total, IDOR-scoped to `req.user.id`. Platform ONLY - no memo/source/handle (PII). (`server/routes/staffPortal/payouts.js`)
- **(cover-swaps) /api/admin various** (Admin): Admin cover-swap approval endpoints (`server/routes/adminCoverSwaps.js`, mounted under `/api/admin`).
- **GET /api/admin/lead-call-attention** (Admin+Manager): FAULT rows from BOTH call bridges, `UNION ALL`ed and tagged `kind` (`lead` | `consult`), joined to a customer name + proposal/client ids, newest first (`created_at DESC, id DESC`), with `detail` riding through raw so the client can tell cap causes apart. Lead half unchanged: `lead_call_attempts` in `failed`/`skipped_unconfigured`/`skipped_invalid_phone`, younger than 7 days, lead still `new`; missed and after-hours chains stay deliberate non-items (2026-07-20). Consult half (2026-08-25, spec 5.3): `consult_call_attempts` in those three plus `skipped_missed_window` and `skipped_cap`, younger than 7 days, consult still `scheduled`. Three things a reader is likely to get backwards, each argued in full in the header comment of `server/routes/admin/leadCalls.js`: there is deliberately **NO `scheduled_at > NOW()` filter** on the consult half, because a failed consult is a client who was promised a call, so the slot passing CREATES the callback obligation rather than retiring it; `c.status = 'scheduled'` is the clearing mechanism instead; and the **limits are PER HALF**, lead 200 and consult 100 under an outer 300, because the consult half is fillable from a PUBLIC booking page and one shared limit let it starve the revenue-critical lead half off the feed. `skipped_cap` carries THREE causes: `cap_tripped` (`openChain`'s chain-open daily cap, and the dominant one), `dial_cap_tripped` (rings to Dallas), `va_leg_cap_tripped` (international legs to Zul).

### Inbox (`/api/admin`, its routes under `/inbox`, spec 2026-10-06)

- **GET /api/admin/inbox** (Admin+Manager): `{ generated_at, ai: { status }, waiting, snoozed, handled, feeds }`. Waiting rows: `person_key, name, kind, need, waiting_since, hot, channels` (ordered by last use, the newest last) and `state: { type, by_user_id, by_name, mine, promised_by, since }` (`since` is the claim time on a claim chip). Snoozed rows: `until, by_name, mine`. Handled rows (7 days): `need, channel, closed_at, reason_code, reason_text, by`. Waiting rows also carry `personal` (the "dallas@" chip). `feeds` holds `thumbtack`, `888`, `1922`, `0082`, `gmail` (its `last_at` is the last good sync, plus `status`: `on`, `off` with no key or with the sync switched off (`RUN_SCHEDULERS` or `RUN_GMAIL_SYNC_SCHEDULER`, read by `gmailSchedulerEnabled` in `server/utils/inbox/feeds.js`, so a box that runs no schedulers reads off), `failing` after 3 failed ticks in a row, `catching_up` while the backfill pages) and `zola` (the newest stored Zola notice: the newest `gmail_messages` row that carries a lead, which only a Zola notice does, read through `idx_gmail_messages_zola_at`), each with `last_at` and `quiet` (48 hours)
- **GET /api/admin/inbox/:personKey** (Admin+Manager): `{ person, status, waiting, waiting_since, need, state, snooze, closed, legal_hold, context, thread, reply }`. `closed` is `{ reason_text, closed_at, by }` while handled, else null. `reply` is `{ mode, lines, default_line, last_line, their_line, recipient_display, opted_out, bad_number, proxy, staff, first_text_prefix, app }`: `last_line` feeds the FROM default (for a client, read over their whole history beside their events, the rows the Messages reply reads, `server/utils/inbox/lastHumanRows.js`), `their_line` is the line of their latest inbound text, and `first_text_prefix` is a map by line (`{ "1922": "Dr. Bartender: " }`, `{}` when none). `app` is `{ name, url, caption }` for the person's latest app channel (Thumbtack, Gmail or Zola), and `mode` is `open` when their newest message came that way; the page shows `caption` as sent. Thread rows from mail carry `attachments` (a count), an inbound mail its `subject` (a Zola inquiry reads "Zola inquiry"; our replies carry none and show our words), and, on our Gmail replies, `by_email` with `author_name` null. Mail adds the system lines "Auto-reply received" (theirs), "Auto-reply sent" (our own, the reader's `meta.sent`), "Email bounced" and "Closed their inquiry on Zola." (with their reason when the notice gave one). `context` adds `{ kind: 'zola_lead', couple_label, wedding_date, client_id }` and, on the unknown card, `address` beside `phone_display`. A key that no longer resolves is 404 `{ code: 'INBOX_MOVED', moved_to }`, written by the route because the global handler carries no extra fields; an unknown key is a plain 404

Every route is `auth`, `requireAdminOrManager` and `asyncHandler` (`server/routes/admin/inbox.js`). `:personKey` is `c-<clients.id>`, `s-<users.id>`, `p-<last 10 digits>`, `t-<Thumbtack negotiation id>`, `e-<16 lowercase hex>` (an unknown email sender: a hash of the address, never the address) or `z-<zola_leads.id>` (`parsePersonKey`: canonical integers, at most int4, for `c-`, `s-` and `z-`); anything else is 400 before any read. A personal person (spec 2026-10-14 decision 6, as the owner decided it on 2026-10-15: the personal mail of one sender, judged once per mail by the sync and stored, under its own `e-` key, `people.personalEmailKey`, apart from that sender's business key) exists only for the owner's account (`PERSONAL_MAILBOX_OWNER_USER_ID`, user 1): every other viewer's list and badge leave them out, and the routes answer them the plain 404 a key that names no one gets. A personal key never moves and never answers `moved_to`: it is never in `keyByEmailHash`, so a sender who later becomes a client leaves it where it is. The item route's `getItem` checks `hiddenFrom` before anything is loaded or resolved. The seen, actions and text routes ask `engine.isHiddenKey` (`visibleKey` in the route file), which also refuses any `e-` key with no mail in the snapshot (an address that never wrote, or one whose key moved to a client), to every viewer, with the same 404: an `e-` key is the hash of an address anyone can guess, so these routes never tell a personal person from an unused address. Every 400 carries its real copy as `error`, never the generic "Please fix the errors below".

- **The text route** (`server/utils/inbox/textSend.js`). `body` is trimmed, 1 to 1600 characters; `line` a line key; `send_id` a UUID (400 otherwise, nothing reserved). The `send_id` is reserved in `inbox_sends` before anything else: a repeat with a stored answer gets that answer and sends nothing; a repeat while the first is running is 409 `INBOX_SEND_IN_PROGRESS`; a reservation still unanswered after 2 minutes is 409 `INBOX_SEND_UNRECORDED`; another user's id is 409 `INBOX_SEND_ID_REUSED`. Then, from a fresh engine read: who may be texted (`c-` with a phone, `s-` in the send-eligible set, `p-` and `t-` only when their number has texted a DRB line; else 422 `INBOX_NOT_TEXTABLE`); the line (`INBOX_TEXT_LINES`, else 422 `INBOX_LINE_NOT_ENABLED`; staff and Thumbtack proxy numbers the 888 only, else 422 `INBOX_LINE_NOT_ALLOWED`); `textability()` (409 `INBOX_OPTED_OUT`; 422 `INBOX_BAD_NUMBER`, where the Messages reply answers the same copy with 400, its route's existing validation status); and the "Dr. Bartender: " prefix on a first text from a 224 line. The recipient is the number of their latest inbound text (decision 29), else the phone on file. Clients, `p-` and `t-` go through `sendSMS` (an explicit `from`, the status callback, and the ledger meta `sentBy` with type `inbox_text`; `logClientMessage` writes the `message_log` row only for a client who has a proposal, as for every SMS); staff through `staffText.sendToStaffRecipient` with a fresh `group_id`. Either way the row carries `sender_id`, `metadata.line` (from the number Twilio reports) and `metadata.send_id`, and a failed send's `error_message` is `twilioErrorText(err)` (`Twilio <code> (failed)`, or `Twilio send failed` with no code; the admin group send keeps Twilio's raw message). A failure callback that beat the row (kept in `sms_status_orphans`) is folded into it after the insert, best effort as in the Messages reply, so the 201 shows the row failed; such a send never counts as a first text. A Twilio failure keeps the failed row and is 502 `EXTERNAL_SERVICE_ERROR`; a 21610 is 409 `INBOX_OPTED_OUT` (`sendSMS` has written `sms_optouts`). Every answer is stored as `inbox_sends.result`; the reservation is deleted only when an unexpected error lands before Twilio is called, and an unexpected error after the send is 500 `INBOX_SEND_UNRECORDED`, never released.
- **The engine** (`server/utils/inbox/engine.js`). Two passes behind one 30-second in-process cache that the list, the item and lane inbox-ai's read job share (`badge-counts` accepts one up to 2 minutes old): light headers from every source since `INBOX_HISTORY_START` and the pure rules over them (`rules.js`, spec 5.5 and 5.6), then full rows only for the people waiting, snoozed or closed in the last 7 days. Pass 1 is a header scan, so it grows with all traffic since the floor; past 10,000 pass-1 rows (the headers, the AI reads and the taps) it warns once per process, in the log and to Sentry (counts only). Taps stored under a person's old `p-`, `s-`, `t-`, `e-` or `z-` key fold onto their current key (`aliases.js`: an `e-` key moves through the index's `keyByEmailHash` once its address names a client or staffer, a `z-` key follows its Zola lead to the lead's client) unless that key still has events of its own, reads match by `subject_ref` alone, and a key with taps but no events is never a person. Every write clears the cache: seen, an action, Undo, an Inbox text, the Messages reply, and a status callback that wrote. A source query that fails fails the whole GET (500): never a partial list that looks complete. `badge-counts.inbox_waiting` is `getWaitingCount({ timeoutMs: 2000, viewerId })`, counted per viewer (personal mail counts only for its owner), null on failure or timeout, and never delays the other counts. `getReadSubjects()` reads the cached snapshot (only `{ fresh: true }` reloads, and `maxAgeMs` lets the read job accept an older one, as the badge does), carries each person's real name or null, and never returns the legal-hold client, a personal person, a subject under any key in the held key set, or a subject whose own event or any line of its slice belongs to a mail that also reached the held client, so the words written to the held client are never read under another key. Each snapshot loads the held set with its other pass-1 reads (`loadLegalHold`: the held keys and the held addresses in canonical form, one more query per snapshot; a failure fails the load). `markHeldSiblings` then marks every event of a Sent mail that reached a person under any held key `meta.legalHold` (one `readRef`, every recipient's event), and `markHeldAddresses` marks every event of a mail whose From, To or Cc (the pass-1 row's `from_address` and `to_addresses`) names a held address in any form Gmail delivers to it (`canonicalAddress`: lowercased and trimmed, a +tag dropped, and for gmail.com and googlemail.com the local part's dots dropped and the domain read as gmail.com), so the held client writing from a variant of their address, or a planner or counsel copying them, is held; that mail only, never the rest of its thread, by plain string compares.
- **Errors**: the 422s are `AppError` built directly (`server/utils/errors.js` has no 422 class).
- **The AI read** (lane inbox-ai; spec sections 6 and 17). The only server module that requires `@anthropic-ai/sdk` (pinned 0.131.0) is `server/utils/anthropicClient.js`: one memoized client per key with a 30 s timeout, `maxRetries` 2, `baseURL` pinned to `https://api.anthropic.com` (an `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` cannot redirect message text), `authToken: null` (a stray `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` would add a second credential) and `logLevel: 'warn'` (debug logging would print request bodies). It returns null with no key, and throws under `NODE_ENV=test` unless a test passes a stub.
- **The call** (`server/utils/inbox/aiRead.js`): `client.beta.messages.create()` (only the beta namespace moves `betas` into the `anthropic-beta` header), the one text block then parsed through `output_config.format.parse()` inside its own try, so an answer the schema rejects is stored as `ParseError` instead of throwing before `stop_reason` can be read with `output_config: { format: zodOutputFormat(schema), effort: 'low' }`, `max_tokens: 4096` (thinking is always on and counts toward it), `betas: ['structured-outputs-2025-12-15']` (the default model also sends `server-side-fallback-2026-07-01` with `fallbacks: 'default'`), no `thinking` field, and `AbortSignal.timeout(120000)` as one deadline over every try and wait (the SDK honors a 429's `retry-after` with no ceiling). `INBOX_AI_MODEL` takes only `claude-opus-5-5` (the default) or `claude-haiku-4-5`, which gets neither effort nor the fallback; any other value is the default, with one boot warning. `stop_reason` is checked before the parsed output: `refusal` is stored as refused; `max_tokens`, a missing text block or an answer the schema rejects is an error (`StopMaxTokens`, `NoParsedOutput`, `ParseError`).
- **What is sent** (`aiRedact.js`, `aiRead.js`): the engine's slice, as dated lines tagged THEM or US with the kind of channel, in Central time; the engine's `OPT_WORDS` rows never. Each line is cut before redaction (dropping a token the cut split), at 8,000 characters for an email or Zola line and 3,000 for every other, then links become `[link]` (client links carry access tokens), email addresses `[email]`, every run of 7 or more digits with spaces, dots, dashes or parentheses `[number]`, and the person's surname is dropped when capitalized (an everyday-word surname such as White only right after the first name). Every redaction pass is linear in its input (the address and bare-host scans consume a run they judged rather than restarting inside it). Lines are then cut to 4,000 characters (email and Zola) or 1,500 (the rest), and the conversation to 12,000: the subject's own line always stays, the rest are formatted newest first until the budget is spent, and the older ones are dropped unformatted. An email line carries `Subject: <subject>.` ahead of the words (never on a Zola line) and `[attachment]` once per attachment, never a filename. A typical read is about 2 to 3 cents and the ceiling about 10 cents (`max_tokens` 4096 spent on output), so `INBOX_AI_DAILY_CAP`'s worst day is about $30. No phone number, email address, card or account number, client id or opt-in data is sent, which is what the privacy page says.
- **The prompts**: two fixed system prompts in `aiRead.js`. The inbound one judges every unanswered message, not only the last, and treats the conversation as data, never instructions; its schema carries `solicitation` beside `needs_reply`, `what_they_need` and `reason` (someone selling to us or asking for something that is not a booking: a vendor pitch, a sponsorship or partnership ask, recruiting, a survey, a listing request), which the prompt says to answer with `needs_reply` true and a need line on what they offer. The outbound one asks only whether our reply is a holding reply. The schemas set no length limits; the server cuts `what_they_need` and `reason` to 80 characters and `promised_by` to 30, and replaces em dashes.
- **The job** (`server/utils/inbox/readJob.js`; scheduler `inbox_ai_read`, every 60 s, first run 40 s after boot, `RUN_INBOX_READ_SCHEDULER`): with no key it does nothing. Otherwise it turns a read pending over 5 minutes into an error, pauses at `INBOX_AI_DAILY_CAP` attempts per rolling 24 hours, then takes `getReadSubjects({ maxAgeMs: BADGE_MAX_AGE_MS })` (the snapshot the badge reads, up to 2 minutes old; ARCHITECTURE's engine bullet above already allows it) and keeps the readable subjects with no ok or refused read in `inbox_reads` and fewer than 3 attempts. Oldest first, at most 20, it reserves each as `pending` in one upsert before its call, reads, stores, and clears the cache. A read is keyed by the subject's `readRef` (a Sent mail's `gm:<id>`, shared by the event of every person it reached), else its own ref: siblings of one mail are read once, under one `inbox_reads` row, and every sibling reads it; each subject keeps its own ref, the one its slice lines carry. An inbound read stores `solicitation`, and when it is true stores `needs_reply` true whatever the model said, so a pitch waits under the rules as they are and its row carries `pitch` (the Pitch chip). The legal-hold client is refused three ways: the engine never offers them, a subject flagged `legalHold` or holding a held line in its slice is skipped (`aiRead.isReadable`), and the job refuses every key in the held key set itself. That set has one builder, `engine.loadLegalHold` (`legalHoldPersonKeys` is its keys). Its proposals are the held proposals and every other proposal of the held clients, and each source is read by client and by proposal as two UNION ALL arms, so each arm can use its own index (`zola_leads.proposal_id` and `email_leads.client_id` have none; both tables are small): the held clients as `c-` keys; every number on record as a `p-` key (the client row, their texts by client, their Thumbtack leads, consults and SMS ledger rows by client or proposal, so a send to a number that matches no client counts, the shift contacts by proposal, their SMS consent log by client); their Thumbtack leads as `t-` and their Zola leads as `z-` keys, by client or proposal; and every address on record (the client row, the email ledger and consult bookings by client or proposal, the shift contacts and drink plans by proposal, their marketing lead rows and legacy Check Cherry proposals by client) as its business `e-` key and its personal-mail `e-` key, hashed in JavaScript by the people index's own functions, and in canonical form for the address hold flag. A recorded address is dropped by its provenance, never because a `users` row matches it (anyone can register an address): from every source, an address in the people index's own set (the mailbox, its aliases, `INBOX_OWN_EMAILS`, `FROM_EMAIL`, `ADMIN_EMAIL` and Zola's vendor address) or an admin's or manager's `users.email`; and from a staff notice only (an email ledger row with no client of type `gratuity_removed_staff_notice`, `service_extension_approved` or `service_extension_declined`), a real staffer's address (an admin or manager, or onboarding status submitted, reviewed, approved, suspended or deactivated). Every other recorded address stays held, a staffer's included, and the held client's own address on the client row is never dropped. Addresses compare in canonical form. `makeInboxReadTick` holds the in-flight guard.
- **The email feed** (spec 2026-10-14; scheduler `gmail_sync`, every 60 s, first run 20 s after boot, `RUN_GMAIL_SYNC_SCHEDULER`; `server/utils/inbox/gmailSync.js`). With `GMAIL_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY` unset every tick does nothing. Otherwise the tick inserts the one `gmail_sync_state` row if missing and claims its lease (180 s) with one statement, so two instances at a deploy never overlap. The first sync reads the mailbox's history id before listing, then backfills from the history floor, Sent first and then the rest, each page oldest first; after that it follows `history.list` (an id added as a draft or into Spam or Trash is never read; one rescued later arrives as a label gained, INBOX or Primary, and is read then), and a history id Gmail no longer keeps falls back to a dated listing from the last good sync minus a day. Each message is fetched, judged by the pure counting rules (`gmailRules.js`: drafts, spam and trash dropped before any of their body is decoded, then our own addresses, the system domains, bulk and no-reply senders dropped; the Zola branch before the own-address rule; auto-replies classified, where the subject fallback after the headers counts "Automatic reply", "Auto-reply" or "Auto response" at the start (after an optional "Re:") whatever follows, but "Out of office" only alone, right before punctuation (":", "(", a hyphen or a dash, ",", ".", "|", "/" or "!"), or before "until", "reply", "autoreply" or "notice", so "Out of office - back Monday" is an auto-reply and an "Out of Office party" subject is a message; and Google's bounces, where a delivery report is a bounce only when a recipient's Action in its delivery-status part is `failed` (with no status part, when its subject is no "(Delay)" notice), any other report from Google (a Delay) is dropped and never stored, and the verdict names the failed recipients, `bouncedTo`, at most 20) and stored in its own transaction, every side effect (a bounce marking the Sent row it names failed and merging the recipients its report says failed into that row's `bounced_to`, while a report that names none, or a row already failed whole, leaves it failed whole; a Zola notice's lead row) gated on the insert returning a row. A bounce row's `in_reply_to` is the Sent Message-ID it marked (In-Reply-To's, else the last stored References entry), never the raw header, so the reader places the bounce with the same Sent mail the sync marked. The reply and bounce rules read stored Sent mail of kind `message` only, never our auto-replies. The Zola sender check runs in `gmailRules.js`'s `classifyZola`, in three checks: the strict From (`isStrictZolaFrom`, in `gmailRules.js`: the value trimmed and refused past 400 characters, then exactly `weddingvendors@zola.com`, bare or after one display name), the authentication gate, whose one home is `server/utils/inbox/zolaMail.js` (`zolaAuthPasses`: in the topmost Authentication-Results header, the one mx.google.com wrote, refused outright when it holds a double quote or runs past 4,096 characters, DKIM passes for a zola.com signature and the single DMARC result passes for the zola.com From; its properties are read in one linear pass, a name starting only where a run of name characters starts), and the notice being addressed to us (`addressedToUs`: one of the business's own mailboxes, as written or without its +tag, in To or Cc, never Bcc and never Zola's own address; those mailboxes are `index.mailboxEmails` from `people.buildEmailIndex`, the mailbox, its personal alias and the `INBOX_OWN_EMAILS` list, never an admin's or manager's own address, `FROM_EMAIL` or `ADMIN_EMAIL`; a notice with more than one To or Cc header fails, because we read the top copy and DKIM checked the bottom one). A notice that fails any of the three is dropped, counted as `gateFailed` on the tick's log line, and three such drops in 24 hours raise one Sentry warning tagged `zola_gate` (at most one a day), whose message says the notices failed the gate (authentication, or not addressed to us) and names no address and no content. Ids already stored, and ids skipped for good, are passed over before any fetch; at most 50 messages or 40 s a tick. A message that fails 3 times (anything after its fetch, or a request Gmail refuses for that one id) is skipped for good (`gmail_sync_skips.attempts`) with one Sentry capture. Of the database's errors only a data error (SQLSTATE class 22 or 23, a value the row refuses, which would fail the same way every time) costs the message an attempt; any other (no SQLSTATE, a Node errno such as EPIPE, a missing column after a failed migration, Neon's proxy errors as XX000, a deadlock or serialization failure, a lock or statement timeout: `isDbOutage`) fails the tick instead and spends no attempt. A message Gmail leaves unavailable at the API while it answers another fetch that tick (or the probe, the mailbox's profile from `getProfile`, which needs nothing stored, asked at the end of each listing page or history window, or after two unanswered fetches in a row) is held and counted in `gmail_sync_skips.unavailable`, and skipped for good with the same Sentry capture after 20 such ticks (`GMAIL_UNAVAILABLE_SKIP_TICKS`), the window moving past it on the tick of that twentieth strike; no answer anywhere is an outage, which ends the tick and counts against no message, recorded under the held message's class, or the auth class when the probe itself was refused for auth. A token that will not mint is the tick failing, never the message being fetched. A cursor write that changes no row (another instance took the lease at a deploy) stops the tick, which then records neither a good sync nor a failure. The sync resolves addresses through `people.loadEmailIndex` (clients with an address, the send-eligible staff and the operators: three reads, built by the same `buildEmailIndex`, so `ownEmails` and `resolveEmail` answer as the full index does), loaded once a tick and only when a message needs it. Three failed ticks in a row read `failing`, with a Sentry warning at most once an hour. Log lines and `last_error` carry counts and an error class, never a body or an address, and the summary line is logged only by a tick that counted something.
- **What is stored** (`mailText.js`): the person's own words (plain over HTML, quoted history and signatures cut, capped at 20,000 characters), the subject and an attachment count. Never the raw mail, an attachment or a filename. Mail is hostile input read inside the Express process, so every pass is linear in its input: a base64 body is never decoded past what the 1,000,000-character source bound can hold, HTML is converted from its first 256 K characters only, comments and style, script, head and title blocks go in one forward scan (one that never closes cuts the rest once; a head with no end tag ends at the body, and with no body either only its tag goes, so the words after it stay), blockquotes pair on a stack, and a tag pattern never scans past the next `<` (`[^<>]*`). The cuts keep the person's words: a dashed date is no phone number, so a dated detail block stays; a trailing contact block is cut only when a sign-off (on its first line or the line above) or the sender's own name line opens it, and never under a line that ends in ":" (an address block they introduce is what they sent); plain text cuts at the standard `-- ` signature delimiter, and at a bare `--` line only when what follows it to the end is a signature (at most six lines with words, each under 60 characters, none a sentence, opened by a sign-off or the sender's own name or holding an address, a link or a phone, and not introduced by a line that ends in ":"), so a bare `--` over a list or a question is their separator (text converted from HTML, whose lines are trimmed, takes `--` as the delimiter outright); a bottom-posted or inline reply (no words above its "On ... wrote:" header) keeps its unquoted lines when its quote is marked with `>`, cut at the next header below; and Outlook's plain-text rule (a line of underscores) above a From:/Sent: block is the quote boundary.
- **Personal mail** (spec 2026-10-14 decision 6, as the owner decided it on 2026-10-15; `server/utils/inbox/personalMail.js`). Each mail is judged on its own, once, by the sync, when it stores the row, and the answer is stored (`gmail_messages.personal`, `personal_to`); nothing later changes it, so a sender who becomes a client, a lead or a staffer, or who writes to the business mailbox, never moves a stored personal mail to business, nor a business mail to personal. An inbound mail (not a Zola notice, not a bounce) is personal when its To and Cc name none of the business's addresses (`people.businessEmails`: the mailbox, the `INBOX_OWN_EMAILS` list, the no-reply address `FROM_EMAIL` sends from, `ADMIN_EMAIL` and the own `users.email` of every operator but the owner, never the personal alias or Zola's address, compared as written and without a +tag; the owner's call of 2026-10-15 is that an operator in copy is business, since the sender copied our team, while the owner's own account (`PERSONAL_MAILBOX_OWNER_USER_ID`) is not the team, so a friend copying it keeps the mail personal; the Zola gate still reads `mailboxEmails` only), which covers both private shapes: the owner alias alone among ours, and none of ours at all (we were Bcc'd, a list, a forward), since an unclear shape stays private; and when its sender was nobody we knew when it arrived. "Known" is one parameterized lookup (`SQL.knownAt`) through the message's own transaction connection: a client, a marketing lead, an admin or manager, or a send-eligible staffer (role staff, onboarding submitted, reviewed or approved, a profile and an agreement) by the address trimmed and lowercased (`lower(btrim(email))`, the exact expression of `idx_clients_email_norm`, `idx_email_leads_email_norm` and `idx_users_email_norm`, so each address is an index probe), each by a record with `created_at` at or before the mail's `at` (a record with none counts as known). For a Sent mail, answering decides first, whichever of our addresses it went from: one that answers an inbound (In-Reply-To, the lowest-id inbound with that Message-ID dated at or before the reply, so a later mail reusing a Message-ID never takes it over; else the newest inbound in its thread before it, the readers' rule; never a forward) takes that inbound's side, so a reply to a personal inbound is personal for that inbound's sender and for each recipient nobody knew when it was sent, and a reply to a business inbound is business, which lets the business item get its reply and close (a reply from the owner alias included). Only a mail that answers nothing (a fresh send, or a forward) reads its From: from the owner alias it is personal for each recipient nobody knew, from anywhere else it is business. A client or staffer copied on it keeps the business event either way. Only a mail that could be personal is looked up. A database error rejects the judgment inside the store's transaction, so the message follows the sync's own rule (`isDbOutage`: only SQLSTATE class 22 or 23 counts against it) and is retried, never stored as business. One residual is accepted: a reply sent from contact@ to a personal mail that the first sync stored before that mail (so the sync judged it a fresh send and stored it business) keeps a copied stranger on their personal person only while the index knows them as nobody, so if that stranger later becomes a client their copy of the reply moves to that client's business item.
- **The readers** (`readGmail.js`, `readZola.js`) map `gmail_messages` into the engine's events at read time, so business mail stored before its sender became a client joins that client (personal mail never moves: below). Both take their rows from one scan of `gmail_messages` per snapshot (`readGmail.MAIL_HEADERS_SQL`, split by `isGmRow` and `readZola.isZolaRow`; `readZola.mapMailHeaders` runs both). An inbound mail is about its From address (a client, then a staffer, else an `e-` key); a Sent mail answers the person of the stored inbound it replies to, then each recipient, one event per person sharing `meta.readRef` so the mail is read once. A forward (its subject starts Fwd: or Fw:) answers its recipients only, never the inbound it forwards nor the Zola notice its In-Reply-To names. A bounce fails only the events of the people its failed addresses (`bounced_to`) reached, and its system line sits with them; a report that names no recipient of the mail fails the whole mail. An auto-reply, either way, is a system line; our own carries `meta.sent`. A row stored personal is its sender's personal person (`people.personalEmailKey`), whoever the index says the sender is now; a Sent row follows the same answering-first rule at read time (the same In-Reply-To pick, made deterministic by lowest id): one that answers a personal inbound puts that inbound's sender, each recipient in its `personal_to` and each recipient the index resolves to an `e-` key (nobody we know) on their personal people, one that answers a business inbound is business whatever its `personal_to` holds, and one that answers nothing reads `personal_to` alone. Asking again here covers the first sync, which stores Sent mail before the inbound it answers and so judged such a reply as a fresh send. Every event of a personal person carries `meta.personal`, which is what `classify.isPersonal` reads, and a bounce of a personal Sent mail sits with that person. Zola rows are about their lead (its client when linked, else `z-`): until lane zola-leads adds the body parser, every gated notice is stored as `zola_unparsed` and reads "Zola sent something the OS couldn't read. Open Zola."; a closed notice closes by rule. A Gmail reply joins its Zola couple by a read-time join, because the backfill often stores the reply before the notice; the reply's event is `gm:<id>:z` (its `readRef` `gm:<id>`), so it never shares a ref with the mail's email events, and a Zola reply on a person the email side already answers (a linked couple's own address among the recipients) is dropped as a twin, kept as the email event. A reply from Gmail closes with "Replied by email" ("Replied to Zola by email" for a couple); a bounced one does not, for the people its failed addresses reached.
- **What it records**: an `inbox_reads` row holds the answer and the model that served (a fallback model included). An `error` row, the failed-read and cap Sentry warnings (each at most one an hour) and every log line carry only the error class, HTTP status, request id or error code, never message text or model output.
- **The status** (`server/utils/inbox/aiStatus.js`) is `ai.status` on every `GET /inbox`, read beside the snapshot (`Promise.all` in `getInbox`): off (no key, or the scheduler disabled), paused (at the cap), failing (3 or more error reads in the last hour, or the count unreadable), else on. It never throws. The read job gates on the same count.
- **Failure stance** (spec 6.5): a read can only move a waiting item into Recently handled with its reason and Reopen, or keep a replied item open as a promise. An unread, failed or refused inbound read leaves the item waiting; an unread, failed or refused outbound read leaves the reply counted as real.

### Messages - `/api/messages`

- **POST /api/messages/send** (Admin): Send SMS to one or more staff (supports batch sends with group_id). One send and one `sms_messages` row per eligible recipient through `server/utils/staffText.js` (`loadEligibleStaffRecipients` + `sendToStaffRecipient`, shared with the Inbox text route); the group send passes no line, no status callback and no send id, so it sends from the 888 with `metadata` `{}`. It is a human send that does not consult `textability` or `sms_optouts`: it checks `agreements.sms_consent` and a usable phone only. A `shift_id`, when present, must be a positive integer naming an existing shift, checked before the first text goes out (otherwise a 400 whose message is the sentence, and nothing is sent). A row that fails to save is logged (never the text or the number) and the group carries on, so every staffer is still texted (spec amendment 29). Answers 200 with `sent: 0` when nobody qualifies

### Two-Way SMS - `/api/sms`

- **POST /api/sms/opt-in** (**public, none** (`publicLimiter`)): Submit for the standalone `/sms` opt-in page (`server/routes/smsOptIn.js`, mounted ahead of `sms.js`). Requires name + email. `sms_consent` is OPTIONAL and the phone is required only when it is ticked (Twilio forced-consent rule, 2026-08-03). Ticked: records via `recordSmsConsent` with `source_form='sms_page'`, and rolls the whole submit back rather than leave a created client SMS-on without a recorded opt-in. Ticked also 503s `SMS_CONSENT_VERSION_SKEW` on an unknown consent version. Un-ticked: upserts `email_leads` only, never touches `clients`, ignores any number given, and can never 503 (it resolves no version). Same generic 200 for every consent outcome (no client/opt-out enumeration).
- **POST /api/sms/inbound** (Twilio signature): Twilio inbound-SMS webhook (signature-verified, no JWT). Passes `To` and the picture media (`NumMedia`, `MediaUrlN`, `MediaContentTypeN`; Twilio-hosted URLs only, at most 10) to `processInboundSms`, which stores them as `metadata.to` and `metadata.media` and stamps `metadata.outcome` when the row settles. STOP-set words (`stop`, `stopall`, `unsubscribe`, `end`, `cancel`, `quit`, `optout`, `revoke`) write `sms_optouts` and START or UNSTOP clears it (a YES runs the preference opt-in only, spec amendment 32), for every sender on every line, Thumbtack proxies included (a proxy never flips the client's preference). HELP/INFO is answered on every line. Staff CONFIRM/CANT and the automated staff replies run only on the 888 and only when DRB's latest outbound text to that staffer was automated (decision 17; admin alerts, dead-letter alerts and automated texts on a client's thread do not count as DRB's latest text to them); any other staff text is stored as `conversation`, with no TwiML reply and the routine admin email, whose subject says no shift was changed when the text was CONFIRM or CANT
- **POST /api/sms/status** (Twilio signature (the shared `twilioSignature.js` check; production 403, dev warn-and-allow, as `/inbound`)): Twilio message status callback, registered by Messages-page replies and Inbox sends (`smsStatusCallbackUrl()`). `failed`/`undelivered` flips the outbound row (by `twilio_sid`) to `status='failed'` with `Twilio <code> (<status>)` in `error_message`; the code is stored only when Twilio sends a numeric one (anything else stores `Twilio error (<status>)`). `ErrorCode` 21610 writes `sms_optouts` (`source='twilio_21610'`) before the row is touched, so a failure in the row bookkeeping never skips it. A failure whose sid matches no row yet (the callback beat the sender's insert) is kept in `sms_status_orphans`; a sid that belongs to an inbound row never is. After storing an orphan it re-checks the row (runs the flip once more), so a reply row inserted in between still ends up failed. Every other status is ignored and never stored (`accepted` violates prod's CHECK); `clients.phone_status` is not touched. 300/min per IP. A failed or undelivered callback, or a 21610, clears the Inbox cache (a duplicate failure callback clears it again). 204 when handled, 500 on a DB error
- **GET /api/sms/conversations** (Admin/Manager): List SMS conversations, one row per client with an unread inbound count
- **GET /api/sms/conversations/:clientId** (Admin/Manager): Full SMS thread for a client
- **POST /api/sms/conversations/:clientId/reply** (Admin/Manager (`adminWriteLimiter`)): Send an outbound SMS reply to the client's phone. `textability` refuses an opted-out person (`sms_enabled` false or an active `sms_optouts` row) with 409 `INBOX_OPTED_OUT` and a number marked bad with 400. Sends from the default line (decision 9: the line of the latest human-involved text by `lastHumanLineFromRows` over the client's whole history (`server/utils/inbox/lastHumanRows.js`), the rule and rows Inbox shares, where a relay text counts as their 888 text; else the sender's own line; limited to `INBOX_TEXT_LINES`, and the 888 only for a Thumbtack proxy number) with a status callback; stores `metadata.line` from the number Twilio reports and `sent_by` on the `message_log` row. A failure callback that beat the row (kept in `sms_status_orphans` by `/status`) is folded into it right after the insert, so the thread shows the failure and the default line agrees with Inbox; the orphan stays, because the Inbox reader left-joins the orphan table. A failed send stores `twilioErrorText` (`Twilio <code> (failed)`). A Twilio 21610 saves the failed row, writes `sms_optouts` and answers 409. A body over 1600 characters is refused with 400 before anything is read or sent, and every refusal carries its sentence as `error`. Once Twilio has taken the text nothing answers "try again": a row that fails to save answers 500 `SMS_REPLY_UNRECORDED` ("Do not send it again"), and the orphan fold is best effort (logged, the inserted row returned). The first text from a 224 line to a number starts "Dr. Bartender: " (`withFirstTextPrefix`, the Inbox rule), and every reply clears the Inbox cache
- **PUT /api/sms/conversations/:clientId/read** (Admin/Manager): Mark a client's unread inbound SMS as read

### VA Calling - Twilio Voice - `/api/voice`

- **POST /api/voice/inbound** (Twilio signature, **fail-closed in every env, signature BEFORE the limiter**): Client dials the 0082 → returns TwiML `<Dial timeout="20" action="…/inbound/missed?line=zul" callerId="<caller>"><Number>VA_CELL</Number></Dial>` forwarding to Zul's cell (caller = `req.body.From`, constrained to bare +digits or `VOICE_CALLER_ID`).
- **POST /api/voice/inbound/primary** (Twilio signature, **fail-closed in every env, signature BEFORE the limiter**): Client dials the 1922 (the primary business number) → `<Dial timeout="VM_PRIMARY_RING_SEC" action="…/inbound/missed?line=primary">` to `VM_PRIMARY_DIAL_TARGET` (env-only, strict E.164; unset → apology TwiML + throttled Sentry page, never an empty `<Dial>`). Own limiter bucket so a storm on one line cannot silence the other.
- **POST /api/voice/inbound/missed** (Twilio signature, **fail-closed in every env**): The shared `<Dial>` action callback, keyed on the HMAC-covered `?line=`. On a missed `DialCallStatus` it checks `VM_DAILY_CAP`, claims a `voicemail_delivery` row (the ping dedup, stamping `line`), returns the PER-LINE greeting (wrapped in a press-1 `<Gather>` whenever a digit could arrive, which is a DAY-ONLY question: `!night && (escalation is on OR the day greeting itself says "press one")`, and both 2026-08-19 day recordings do. NIGHT never gathers, switch on or off, and nothing validates a night-greeting override - a `VM_NIGHT_GREETING_URL*` pointed at a recording that mentions press 1 would reproduce the trap this closed for day - the `<Gather>` follows the GREETING, not the switch, so flipping `VM_ESCALATION_ENABLED` off can never leave callers pressing a key nobody catches) + `<Record>`, then pings Zul AFTER the response on ZUL-LINE misses only (a primary miss shows natively on the GV dial target). Any other status, or `VOICEMAIL_ENABLED` off, returns a bare `<Hangup/>`. An instant-answer on the primary line is logged by the interception canary (LOG-ONLY since 2026-08-14 - the per-call `dialSec` console line; the Sentry page was removed as inverted, see voice.js's canary block).
- **POST /api/voice/inbound/voicemail** (Twilio signature, **fail-closed in every env**): The `<Record>` `recordingStatusCallback` (not `action`: Twilio skips that when the caller hangs up). Claims delivery (reading back `line`), then answers 204 and works detached: zul → fetch mp3 (constructed URL, never the body's `RecordingUrl`) + Telegram upload + delete on `ok === true`; primary → SMS the caller's number/duration **plus a listen link** (`/api/voice/vm/<listen_token>`, omitted when `VM_LISTEN_LINK_ENABLED=false`) to `VM_TEXT_DESTINATION`. The recording is RETAINED: the link streams from Twilio, so deleting would break every link already sent as well as destroying the only copy until 1b's R2 mirror. Failure alerts go to the LINE'S owner via `alertOperator`.
- **POST /api/voice/bridge** (Twilio signature): Fetched when Zul answers her leg. Looks up the target by `CallSid` from `pending_call` (never from a request param); returns `<Dial answerOnBridge="true" callerId="+12242220082" timeLimit="…"><Number>+1TARGET</Number></Dial>`, or a `<Say>…</Say><Hangup/>` when no target is found.
- **POST /api/voice/status** (Twilio signature): Twilio call-status callback. On a failed/unanswered leg (`no-answer`/`busy`/`failed`/`canceled`) messages Zul via Telegram ("That call didn't connect, resend the number to retry.") + records `call_audit`. Always empty 200/204.
- **GET /api/voice/audio/:name** (**public, none**): Static voicemail audio for Twilio `<Play>` (`voiceAssets.js`, mounted before `/api/voice`). `:name` is a KEY into a frozen allowlist, never a path, so no value can traverse out of `server/assets/`; an unknown key is a 404 that tells a prober nothing. Serves Dallas's four 2026-08-19 recordings plus Zul's 2026-07-24 greeting (also reachable at its original `/greeting.mp3` path). Rate limited 300/min per IP, because Twilio fetches these mid-call and a saturated origin means a live caller hears silence.
- **GET /api/voice/greeting.mp3** (**public, none**): Serves the recorded voicemail greeting mp3 (bundled `server/assets/voicemail-greeting.mp3`) that `/inbound/missed` `<Play>`s. Unauthenticated by necessity - Twilio fetches it with no credentials or signature. Static asset, no DB, no caller input. Overridable / kill-switchable via `VM_GREETING_URL`.

### Voicemail listen link - `/api/voice/vm` (mounted before `/api/voice`)

- **GET /api/voice/vm/:token** (**public, token-gated**): Streams the retained primary-line recording as `audio/mpeg` so the alert SMS link plays on a phone. The UUID token is the auth; the RECORDING SID is read from the row and never accepted from the request, so this cannot become an open proxy into the Twilio account. `requireUuidToken` runs first (a non-UUID 404s before the DB, no 22P02). Supports `Range` with `206`/`416` because iOS Safari will not play without it. One byte-identical `text/plain` 404 for every miss (unknown token, non-primary line, recording gone, switch off) so nothing can be enumerated; `502` for a DB or Twilio failure, never a 404. `no-store`, `noindex`, `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`. Links expire at 30 days via the route's OWN SQL predicate, deliberately not borrowed from the row prune (which retains `recorded`/`failed`/`skipped` rows past retention, because their audio is still undelivered at Twilio, so a token on one of those rows would otherwise never expire). Kill switch `VM_LISTEN_LINK_ENABLED` (default ON) is TWO-SIDED: it closes this route and drops the link from the SMS. Revoke ONE link by rotating `listen_token`.

### Press-1 Escalation - Twilio Voice - `/api/voice/escalate` (mounted before `/api/voice`)

- **POST /api/voice/escalate** (Twilio signature (fail-closed in EVERY env)): The missed-greeting `<Gather action>`, reached only on a real digit. `1` + `VM_ESCALATION_ENABLED` + a shape-valid `CallSid` → env-only target for the OTHER person → quiet-window check → `claimEscalationUnderCap` (advisory-lock serialized cap+claim, a HARD `VM_ESCALATION_DAILY_CAP` bound; a capped/lost claim records and never dials) → billed `<Dial>` with a `<Number url=…/whisper>` keypress screen. Anything else → straight to `<Record>`. Skip outcomes (`skipped_no_target`/`skipped_quiet`/`skipped_cap`) are written AFTER the response.
- **POST /api/voice/escalate/whisper** (Twilio signature (fail-closed in EVERY env)): TwiML played to the ANSWERING party only: `<Gather numDigits="1" finishOnKey="" action=…/accept?sid=PARENT>` + `<Hangup/>`. The keypress screen is what stops an unattended carrier voicemail from ever bridging; a malformed parent sid hangs up outright.
- **POST /api/voice/escalate/accept** (Twilio signature (fail-closed in EVERY env)): The whisper's action: fires only on a real keypress, marks the PARENT call accepted (`escalation_accepted_at`, first-writer-wins, requires a claimed row), returns an empty `<Response>` which bridges the legs. Runs on the CHILD leg, hence the `?sid=` round-trip (HMAC-covered, `isCallSid`-gated both ways).
- **POST /api/voice/escalate/done** (Twilio signature (fail-closed in EVERY env)): The outer `<Dial action>`. Branches on ACCEPTANCE STATE, never `DialCallStatus` (a screened-out machine still reports `completed`); not-accepted → back to `<Record>`, the outcome write runs after the response. Fails closed toward the caller: any doubt offers voicemail.

### Lead Call Bridge - Twilio Voice - `/api/voice/lead`

- **POST /api/voice/lead/answer** (Twilio signature (fail-closed in EVERY env)): Agent leg answered (Dallas/Zul). Returns `<Gather numDigits="1" timeout="10">` wrapping the spoken lead briefing (`buildLeadBriefing`, xml-escaped) AND its one automatic repeat, then `<Hangup/>`. Missing/terminal/malformed attempt → apology TwiML, never 500. Query: `attempt`, `leg` (admin|va), `play`.
- **POST /api/voice/lead/digit** (Twilio signature (fail-closed in EVERY env)): Gather action. `1`: guarded claim to `connected` then `<Dial answerOnBridge="true" callerId="VOICE_CALLER_ID"><Number>toUsE164(lead phone)</Number></Dial>` (the 224 shown to the lead; stale/duplicate press → apology). `9`: replay via `<Redirect>` (max 3 plays). Other: `<Hangup/>`.
- **POST /api/voice/lead/status** (Twilio signature (fail-closed in EVERY env)): Status callback for all legs, at-least-once tolerant. Admin-leg terminal → record disposition + `advanceChain` (claims `calling_va`, winner dials Zul). VA-leg terminal → guarded `missed` (log state, NO alert since 2026-07-20; only chain failures email, via advanceChain/reaper). Lead-leg terminal → `bridge_duration_sec` (defensive parse) and flips `thumbtack_leads.status` to `contacted` only at ≥20s bridge (relay-refusal floor). Non-terminal/unknown statuses ignored.

Respond-then-ring (spec 2026-07-21): with `TT_AUTOREPLY_ENABLED='true'` the webhook tail queues the TT quick reply INSTEAD of dialing; the chain then starts from `first-reply-sent` (or the 60s fallback sweep at +3 min) via `triggerLeadCall({ skipWindowCheck: true })`, bounded by `FIRST_REPLY_CALL_MAX_AGE_MINUTES`. Flag off = the tail dials directly, exactly the pre-feature flow.

### Consult Call Bridge - Twilio Voice - `/api/voice/consult`

- **POST /api/voice/consult/answer** (Twilio signature (fail-closed in EVERY env)): Agent leg answered (Dallas/Zul). Returns `<Gather numDigits="1" timeout="10">` wrapping the spoken consult briefing (`buildConsultBriefing`, xml-escaped: booker name, slot time, ring wording, and the linked proposal's event date + guest count) AND its one automatic repeat, a short pause between them, then `<Hangup/>`. With `CONSULT_CALL_ENABLED=false` it speaks the off message and reads no briefing. A row that is not waiting on THIS leg and ring gets the apology, never a 500 (`legMatches`, ruling R5). Query: `attempt`, `leg` (admin|va), `ring` (0 for the Zul leg), `play`.
- **POST /api/voice/consult/digit** (Twilio signature (fail-closed in EVERY env)): Gather action. `1`: the kill switch first (off speaks the off message and claims nothing), then validate the dial target, re-check that the consult is still scheduled (a cancelled one speaks the cancellation and does NOT claim), then the guarded claim to `connected` (binding `admin_ring` on the admin leg, ruling R9) and `<Dial answerOnBridge="true" callerId="CONSULT_CALLER_ID">` (the 1922 for Dallas; the 0082 for Zul) `<Number statusCallback=…>toUsE164(booker_phone)</Number></Dial>`. Validate-before-claim is the point: a claim that then failed to dial would strand the row `connected`, reported only when the reaper's bridge arm flips it, 40 minutes later. `9`: replay via `<Redirect>` (max 3 plays). Other: `<Hangup/>`, and the leg's status callback moves the chain on.
- **POST /api/voice/consult/dialend** (Twilio signature (fail-closed in EVERY env)): The `<Dial>` action: how the bridged call to the booker ended. `no-answer`/`busy`/`failed`/`canceled` (an allowlist, not a denylist) latch `client_no_answer_at = NOW()` once `WHERE client_no_answer_at IS NULL`, and either way the TwiML reads the booker's number back to the agent still on the line, re-derived through `toUsE164` so it speaks a number we would actually dial. Only AFTER that answer does the latch winner notify (`notifyClientNoAnswer`: text Dallas, or send the one admin email when the text cannot go out), so a slow SMS or Resend call never delays Twilio's response. Everything else, `completed` included, is a bare `<Hangup/>`. The latch is its own column rather than a value in `detail` (ruling R14): `placeLeg`'s catch writes a Twilio error code into `detail` earlier in the same chain.
- **POST /api/voice/consult/status** (Twilio signature (fail-closed in EVERY env)): Status callback for every leg, at-least-once tolerant. A status outside the five terminal Twilio values is a complete no-op BEFORE any write (ruling R25 gate 1; `onLegTerminal` carries its own copy as gate 2, those five plus the internal `create_failed` that `placeLeg` reports, so the billed-effect boundary is self-defending rather than trusting its only caller). Client leg: records `bridge_duration_sec`, digits-only parsed and `COALESCE`d so a redelivery cannot erase a good value. Agent legs: hand off to `onLegTerminal`, which owns the ring guard, the claim, the kill-switch and too-late re-checks, and every non-initial transition. Answers 200 with TwiML on any error, because Twilio retries a 5xx.

### VA Calling - Telegram Trigger - `/api/telegram`

- **POST /api/telegram/:secret** (Secret path + `X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token` header + `user_id` allowlist): Zul's outbound-call trigger. Verifies the secret path + header (constant-time; 403 on mismatch), dedupes on `update_id`. Bootstrap (allowlist unset): replies with the sender's id, dials nothing. Non-allowlisted sender: silent 200 no-op. `YES` (`/^y(es)?$/i`): per-min + daily cap check (DB-backed via `call_audit`), `claimForDial`, `placeBridgedCall`, reply "Calling <last4>…". Any other text: `toUsE164`-validate the target (US NANP only, 900/976 rejected), upsert the pending record, reply "Reply YES to call <pretty>". Always 200 to Telegram on a handled outcome; **never dials on a dev signature skip**.

### Blog - `/api/blog`

Blog post bodies are stored as sanitized HTML (via DOMPurify). The admin editor uses TipTap (WYSIWYG) and the public view renders HTML directly.

### Calendar - `/api/calendar`

- **GET /api/calendar/feed/:token** (Token): iCal feed of shifts (rate-limited per token)
- **GET /api/calendar/event/:shiftId.ics** (Yes): Download single shift as .ics file
- **GET /api/calendar/token** (Yes): Get user's calendar subscription token
- **POST /api/calendar/token/regenerate** (Yes): Regenerate calendar subscription token

### Email Marketing - `/api/email-marketing`

- **GET /api/email-marketing/leads** (Admin): List leads with search, filter by source/status, pagination
- **POST /api/email-marketing/leads** (Admin): Create single lead
- **POST /api/email-marketing/leads/import** (Admin): CSV bulk import
- **GET /api/email-marketing/leads/:id** (Admin): Lead detail with send history and conversations
- **PUT /api/email-marketing/leads/:id** (Admin): Update lead
- **DELETE /api/email-marketing/leads/:id** (Admin): Soft-delete (set status to unsubscribed)
- **GET /api/email-marketing/campaigns** (Admin): List campaigns with type filter
- **POST /api/email-marketing/campaigns** (Admin): Create campaign (blast or sequence)
- **GET /api/email-marketing/campaigns/:id** (Admin): Campaign detail with stats, sends, steps, enrollments
- **PUT /api/email-marketing/campaigns/:id** (Admin): Update campaign
- **DELETE /api/email-marketing/campaigns/:id** (Admin): Archive campaign
- **POST /api/email-marketing/campaigns/:id/send** (Admin): **RETIRED (409).** Its audience read `email_leads` only, so it could not honor `clients.marketing_excluded` and would mail someone you excluded. Replaced by lane mkt-g's send
- **POST /api/email-marketing/campaigns/:id/schedule** (Admin): **RETIRED (409).** Same defect as `/send`; hiding one without the other would leave the hole reachable on a timer
- **POST /api/email-marketing/campaigns/:id/test** (Admin): Send the campaign to a single address (defaults to the requester). Mints a REAL unsubscribe token, and refuses an address that is opted out
- **POST /api/email-marketing/upload-image** (Admin): Designed-email image upload (magic-byte validated, R2; returns `/api/blog/images/...` URL)
- **POST /api/email-marketing/preview** (Admin): Render a design (or raw HTML) into the branded email shell for an exactly-as-sent preview
- **GET /api/email-marketing/campaigns/:id/steps** (Admin): List sequence steps
- **POST /api/email-marketing/campaigns/:id/steps** (Admin): Add sequence step
- **PUT /api/email-marketing/campaigns/:id/steps/:stepId** (Admin): Update sequence step
- **DELETE /api/email-marketing/campaigns/:id/steps/:stepId** (Admin): Delete step and reorder
- **POST /api/email-marketing/campaigns/:id/activate** (Admin): Activate drip sequence
- **POST /api/email-marketing/campaigns/:id/pause** (Admin): Pause drip sequence
- **POST /api/email-marketing/campaigns/:id/enroll** (Admin): Enroll leads in sequence
- **GET /api/email-marketing/campaigns/:id/enrollments** (Admin): List enrollments with progress
- **GET /api/email-marketing/analytics/overview** (Admin): Aggregate stats (leads, campaigns, sends, rates)
- **GET /api/email-marketing/conversations** (Admin): List conversations grouped by lead
- **GET /api/email-marketing/conversations/:leadId** (Admin): Conversation thread for a lead
- **POST /api/email-marketing/conversations/:leadId/reply** (Admin): Admin sends reply email
- **PUT /api/email-marketing/conversations/:conversationId/read** (Admin): Mark conversation as read
- **POST /api/email-marketing/conversations/:leadId/mark-replied** (Admin): Manual mark reply received
- **GET /api/email-marketing/unsubscribe** (Public): JWT-verified. Renders a confirmation form and CHANGES NOTHING: mail gateways and scanners GET every link in an email
- **POST /api/email-marketing/unsubscribe** (Public): The acting verb. Writes BOTH identity rows (clients + email_leads) in one transaction, so one click silences the whole person
- **POST /api/email-marketing/webhook/resend** (Resend): Webhook receiver (tracking events, svix-verified)

### Marketing Contacts - `/api/marketing`

- **PUT /api/marketing/contacts/:id/tags** (Admin): Replace a contact's marketing tag set (full set, not a delta)
- **PUT /api/marketing/contacts/:id/do-not-contact** (Admin): Set or clear the house do-not-contact rule; setting requires a reason
- **GET /api/marketing/overview** (Admin): Moments (derived, never stored), the year-honestly numbers, the Needs-You queue, the reachable base, today's send budget
- **PUT /api/marketing/moments/:id** (Admin): Rewrite a moment's WORDS. The rule, audience and window are code and are rejected here. Stores only the fields sent; null clears one back to the authored default
- **POST/DELETE /api/marketing /moments/:id/dismiss** (Admin): Dismiss THIS OCCURRENCE only (year for an annual moment, month for a rolling one), so a recurring prompt returns rather than vanishing
- **GET /api/marketing/sent** (Admin): Campaigns with sent/opened/clicked and BOOKED: a recipient created a proposal within 30 days of receiving it, counted once per client
- **POST /api/marketing/campaigns/:id/send** (Admin): The campaign send. Body `{client_ids}`. Re-checks mailability, dedupes by lowercased address, claims each recipient before calling Resend, paces serially, stops resumably on a quota 429
- **PUT /api/marketing/contacts/:id/email-status** (Admin): Clear a bad `email_status` back to `ok` (one-way; marking bad stays with the webhook). No UI yet, wire it in lane mkt-d
- **GET /api/marketing/contacts** (Admin): Paginated contacts with tags, derived state, mailability, held-back buckets, quick-filter counts, and suggestions
- **GET /api/marketing/contacts/:id** (Admin): Contact drawer: identity, event history, and message history with automated/human per row
- **GET /api/marketing/audiences** (Admin): The seven audience definitions with live emailable counts

Separate router from Email Marketing above, which is at 987 of the 1000-line
hard cap and cannot grow. **Admin only**, deliberately tighter than that file's
`requireAdminOrManager` convention: these write the marketing classification and
(from lane mkt-c) expose names, emails, and lifetime spend across the whole
client base.

**The audience resolver.** `server/utils/marketingAudience.js` is the single definition of who may receive marketing. It exports the suppression conditions as reusable expressions: `MAILABLE_SQL` for filtering, `HELD_BACK_SQL` (a CASE, so buckets are mutually exclusive and sum to `total - mailable`) for the held-back panel, and `isMailable`/`heldBackReason` for a send-time re-check. A test runs all three over identical database rows, including precedence cases, and requires the same answer from each; `isMailable` is defined as `heldBackReason() === null` so those two cannot drift.

- `CONTACT_AGGREGATES` carries the traps: `proposals.amount_paid` is DOLLARS while `legacy_cc_proposals.total_cost_cents` is CENTS; the Check Cherry join is on lowercased email because `client_id` is populated on only 197 of its 1,230 rows; `cc.last_event` is filtered to the past because that ledger runs to 2027; event-type casing is normalized because the import left both `corporate-event` and `Corporate Event`; and Thumbtack inbound resolves through `thumbtack_leads.client_id` to `negotiation_id` to `thumbtack_messages` where `from_type = 'Customer'`, since `thumbtack_messages` has no client id.
- "Has paid us" accepts a Check Cherry ledger booking, not only a native proposal. Gating on proposals alone silently drops the 184-person imported cohort.
- Corporate is **never inferred**. Measured across every client with a proposal: 14 of the 30 who booked corporate work used a personal address, and 10 of the 26 on company domains were booking their own weddings. `marketingSuggestions.js` therefore surfaces a suggestion with its reasoning for a human to accept, and never writes a tag.
- `GET /contacts` computes counts over **two scopes**: the rows and the held-back panel honor every filter, while the quick-filter chips exclude the active filter so they can answer "what would each filter give". `total` comes from the aggregate, not a window function, which would report 0 on a page past the end.

### Client Auth - `/api/client-auth`

- **POST /api/client-auth/request** (No): Request OTP code (rate-limited)
- **POST /api/client-auth/verify** (No): Verify OTP and get client JWT
- **GET /api/client-auth/me** (Client): Get current client profile

### Client Portal - `/api/client-portal`

- **GET /api/client-portal/home** (Client): Portal landing read: focus event (soonest upcoming), archive list, upcoming_count, has_quote_draft flag. Scoped by `client_id` (no IDOR). Money reads `total_price` only (the focus total and balance, the archive totals; spec 2026-10-16 section 4.4), never `total_price_override`, which left an override booking's gratuity out of the Total and balance
- **GET /api/client-portal/proposals/:token** (Client): Get single proposal by token (includes venue_name/city/state + drink_plan_token/submitted_at). Pricing leaves as `price_rows` (`[{ key, label, amount }]`, the proposal page's service rows built by `clientPriceLines.js` with hidden adjustments folded on the server, against `total_price` less the client gratuity) and `gratuity_amount` (the snapshot's client gratuity, dollars), so rows plus gratuity equal `total_price`, the Total the Prescription tab shows; no rows (an unpriced snapshot or a NULL `total_price`) means the tab shows its Total alone. Never `pricing_snapshot`, `adjustments` or `total_price_override`: the snapshot is selected in the same statement as `total_price` and the package (two reads could straddle a write that moves the total and the snapshot together) and taken off the row before the response spreads it (spec 2026-10-16 section 4.4; `clientPortal.test.js` asserts every portal route's raw body). A fallback to a "Pricing adjustment" row, a package-less snapshot, or a gap the snapshot does not explain (a `total_price` that drifted off its snapshot) reports through `reportPriceRowsFold` tagged `client-portal`, by proposal id only. The change-request approved email (`changeRequestNotifications.js`) quotes `total_price` too
