- `status`: pending | draft | submitted | reviewed
- `planner_version` INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 2 (planner v2 rollout): new tokens render the v2 wizard; every pre-existing row was backfilled to 1 at column add, so in-flight drafts finish on the legacy wizard untouched
- `lab_cta_clicked_at` TIMESTAMPTZ — write-once celebration-screen Enhancement Lab CTA click (attach-rate funnel)
- `shopping_list` (JSONB) — auto-generated at submission, admin-editable, persisted across modal opens
- `shopping_list_status` VARCHAR(20) CHECK (NULL | `'pending_review'` | `'approved'`) — gates the public `/t/:token/shopping-list` endpoint
- `shopping_list_approved_at` TIMESTAMPTZ — set when admin approves and sends the list
- `shopping_list_approved_snapshot` (JSONB) — copy of `shopping_list` captured at approve time, written in the same UPDATE as the approval. The client-facing public token route will serve this last-approved snapshot while later admin edits sit in `pending_review`, so clients never see unreviewed quantities (wired once the approved-snapshot lane lands).
- `consult_selections` (JSONB) — admin-side consult-form input (parallel to client-side `selections`)
- `shopping_list_source` VARCHAR(20) CHECK (NULL | `'planner'` | `'consult'`) — flags which input source currently feeds the generator
- `consult_filled_by_user_id` INTEGER FK → users (admin who saved the consult; SET NULL on user delete)
- `consult_filled_at` TIMESTAMPTZ
- `finalized_at` TIMESTAMPTZ — BEO finalize stamp. Set by the derived finalize (list approved) from the shopping-list approve, or by the manual Finalize button (the hosted click, unpaid-extras override, post-Unfinalize); the finalize UPDATE stamps `status='reviewed'` alongside it and is the only writer of that status since 2026-09-22; non-NULL locks every mutation route on the plan (status, notes, shopping list, logo, consult, source flip, delete; the list PUT and the approve flip carry `finalized_at IS NULL` inside their UPDATEs too) and gates the staff-side acknowledge + the T-3 nudge. One deliberate edge: re-confirming an already-approved list on a finalized plan is an idempotent no-op rather than a 409, so a send Retry still works. Cleared by Unfinalize.
- `finalized_by` INTEGER FK → users (admin who finalized; SET NULL on user delete)
- Partial index `idx_drink_plans_shopping_list_pending` covers the admin badge-count "pending review" filter
- Partial index `idx_drink_plans_finalized_at(finalized_at) WHERE finalized_at IS NOT NULL` covers the BEO scheduler + nudge lookups
- On submit: addOns flow into proposal_addons, pricing is recalculated, admin notified, `shopping_list` auto-generated server-side as `pending_review`
- Auto-emails the drink plan link to client on creation

### Proposals & Pricing

**service_packages** (12 rows) — Service tiers
- `slug`, `name`, `description`, `category` (byob | hosted)
- `pricing_type` (flat | per_guest)
- Rate columns: `base_rate_3hr`, `base_rate_4hr`, `extra_hour_rate` (standard + small-event variants)
- Staffing: `bartenders_included`, `guests_per_bartender`, `extra_bartender_hourly`
- Bar fees: `first_bar_fee`, `additional_bar_fee`
- Slots (planner v2): `slot_count` INTEGER NULL + `slot_kind` CHECK (`hard` | `featured` | NULL) — hard = the picks ARE the bar (Base Compound 2); featured = picks headline the menu, the bar improvises (Clear Reaction 4)

**package_items** (planner v2) — structured package contents; one row per stocked category
- `package_id` FK -> service_packages (CASCADE), `category` ("Tequila"), `par_per_100` NUMERIC, `unit`, `sort_order`
- `eligible_item_ids` TEXT[] — SPLIT PARS: the listed par_items share the category volume, so extra "for show" labels never multiply cost
- Coverage: a drink is in-tier when every recipe ingredient resolves (via potionCatalog aliases) to an item in the package's eligible union — computed by `server/utils/coverageEngine.js`, served by `/api/admin/packages/:id/makeability`
- Lineup CONTENT (spec 2026-07-18 §5) is applied by the one-time `server/scripts/applyPackageLineup2026.js` (lane pp2-lineup), never by boot-path schema UPDATEs — after seed, admin/DB is canonical

**ingredient_class_addons** (planner v2) — gap pricing map: `class_key` PK (snake_case ingredient class, e.g. `coffee_liqueur`) -> `addon_slug` (the service_addons row that prices the gap per guest). Pricing itself stays in service_addons; an uncovered ingredient with no class mapping makes the drink `unmakeable` rather than silently free
- `includes` (JSONB array of what's included)
- `service_packages.covered_addon_slugs TEXT[]` — which add-on slugs the hosted
  package's base price already includes. Used by the Potion Planning Lab to
  suppress redundant add-on offers and compute cocktail ingredient gaps.
- Hosted minimums: `min_guests` (rate-tier threshold, default 50 — under it the
  small-event rate columns apply), `min_billed_guests` (INTEGER, 25 on non-class
  per_guest packages, NULL on classes/BYOB), `min_total` (NUMERIC, $550 backstop
  on those same rows). P4 (fix #8): the base price bills at `max(actualGuests,
  min_billed_guests)` heads, then clamps to `min_total`. `schema.sql` sets both on
  every `pricing_type='per_guest' AND bar_type<>'class'` row (the class filter is
  load-bearing — classes are `category='hosted'` too). The engine
  (`pricingEngine.js`) keeps staffing / the 1:100 bartender ratio / gratuity
  surcharges on ACTUAL guests; the snapshot exposes `billed_guests` and
  `floor_reason` (`'guest_min'|'dollar_min'|null`, with `floor_applied` = reason
  is non-null) which flow through `POST /api/proposals/public/calculate`.

**service_addons** — Add-on services
- `slug`, `name`, `description`
- `billing_type`: per_guest | per_hour | flat | per_guest_timed
- `rate`, `extra_hour_rate`
- `applies_to`: byob | hosted | all | class
- `linked_package_id` FK → service_packages (nullable, ON DELETE SET NULL) — ties supply add-ons to a specific class package; NULL addons are universal (e.g., class equipment kits)
- `requires_provisioning` (BOOLEAN, default false) — true for every consumable/gear add-on (mixers, glassware, kits, specialty spirits, the BYOB bundles, the class supply packs, etc.); false for staffing/fee add-ons. Drives the shift `supply_run_required` default (a non-hosted event carrying any provisioning add-on needs a supply run) and the `equipment_supplies` / `hosted_supplies` duty triggers in `dutyLines.js`. Seeded in `schema.sql`; `server/db/provisioningSeed.test.js` fails any seeded add-on that is neither in the seed nor on its short non-provisioning list.

**proposals** — Generated service proposals
- `token` UUID (public access), `client_id` FK → clients
- `source` (VARCHAR(30), nullable) — intake origin: `'thumbtack'` for Thumbtack auto-drafts, NULL = manual/direct (the permanent contract, never "unknown"). CHECK-constrained to `NULL | 'thumbtack'` (widen as new sources land). Drives the Proposals dashboard source filter and badge.
- Event details: type, date, start time, duration, location, guest count
- Structured venue address: `venue_name` (optional), `venue_street`, `venue_city`, `venue_state`, `venue_zip` (ZIP optional). Venue name is captured optionally in the quote wizard; street is **required and persisted server-side at the `/sign` route** (the client also disables Pay until it's filled; `PaymentForm` calls `/sign` before `stripe.confirmPayment`, so a card cannot be charged without a persisted venue). `event_location` is a **derived display string** composed from these via `server/utils/venueAddress.js` (city/state-only output stays byte-identical to the legacy `"City, State"` format); `shifts.location` is composed the same way in `createEventShifts` and `syncShiftsFromProposal`.
- `package_id` FK → service_packages, `num_bars`, `num_bartenders`
- `group_id` (nullable INTEGER FK → proposal_groups, ON DELETE SET NULL) — set when this proposal is one "option" in a side-by-side comparison; NULL = a solo proposal (every existing row). Surfaced in the `GET /` list and `GET /clients/:id` payloads for the dashboard rollup.
- `setup_minutes_before` (nullable INTEGER) — crew arrival/setup lead time before service start. **Back-of-house only — never exposed on public token/proposal/invoice surfaces** (the `/t/:token` allowlist deliberately omits it). NULL ⇒ derive the default at read time: **90 for hosted (per-guest) packages, 60 otherwise** (`server/utils/setupTime.js`, mirrored client-side). Admin-adjustable via `PATCH /:id` (undefined/null sentinel like `total_price_override` — explicit `null` resets to the package default; never COALESCEd). Synced into `shifts.setup_minutes_before` by `createEventShifts` / `syncShiftsFromProposal`. A manual override **persists across package changes**; set it to NULL to fall back to the new package's default (90 hosted / 60 else). `GET /:id` also returns a server-derived `setup_time_display` (service start − effective minutes, 24-hour "HH:MM" — admin event detail is 24h throughout; staff-facing SMS/email keep 12-hour via the default `subtractMinutesFromTime`). Validation: integer 0–600 inclusive.
- `transferred_from_cc_id` (nullable TEXT, partial-unique) + `external_paid` (NUMERIC(10,2) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, whole DOLLARS per the proposals money exception) — CC event transfer (2026-07-07, `scripts/cc-transfer-events.js`): events moved from CheckCherry before its shutdown. `external_paid` is money collected in CC, folded into `amount_paid` at transfer with ZERO `proposal_payments` rows (the CC-era ledger holds those payments; native rows would double-count blended metrics), so balance math (`total_price - amount_paid`) self-corrects dunning and invoice derivation. Transferred proposals are born `confirmed` + `accepted_at` set with `total_price_override` = the CC contracted total, drink plan created nudge-suppressed, balance/pre-event reminders enrolled (past-dated rungs purged). The payment panel shows an "Off-platform" line when `external_paid > 0`. `proposals.cc_id` stays a v1 relic (NULL) so the ledger loader's guard and the metrics tri-state keep their meaning.
- `pricing_snapshot` (JSONB — full pricing breakdown at time of creation; also carries `gratuity {rate, tip_jar, staff_count, hours, staff_noun, total, floor_rate}`, `staff_noun`, `display_labels`, and `_version` — stamped by `calculateProposal` (`PRICING_SNAPSHOT_VERSION`); server readers route through `server/utils/pricingSnapshot.js` `readSnapshot()`, tolerating legacy unversioned snapshots as v1 and hard-failing only on unknown future versions)
- **Checkout gratuity** (spec 2026-06-05; election-at-payment 2026-08-03; admin mandate 2026-08-10): `tip_jar` (BOOLEAN, default true), `gratuity_rate` (NUMERIC(10,4), default 0 — per-staff-per-hour pre-paid gratuity), `gratuity_rate_change_origin` (TEXT: `'staffing' | 'admin' | null`), `gratuity_floor_rate` (NUMERIC(10,4), nullable — admin-mandated floor rate; a mandate exists iff > 0, enforced by `proposals_gratuity_floor_positive_check`; mirrored into `pricing_snapshot.gratuity.floor_rate` by every persisting snapshot writer). CHECK `(tip_jar = true OR gratuity_rate >= 50 OR (gratuity_floor_rate IS NOT NULL AND gratuity_rate >= gratuity_floor_rate))` — the third disjunct lets a sub-$50 mandate coexist with a no-jar answer; the mandate REPLACES the no-jar floor at every enforcement layer, never stacks. Note the disjuncts are OR'd, so the CHECK is not itself a mandate-floor backstop (a jar-yes row can hold rate < floor as far as the DB cares); mandate-floor enforcement is application-level (`deriveGratuityRate` + the webhook's post-`FOR UPDATE` check), which every current writer satisfies. The dollar gratuity is computed (`rate × staffCount × hours`), appended to the breakdown as a `"Gratuity"` line, and folded into `total_price` on top of any override. **The CLIENT election is written by the `payment_intent.succeeded` webhook, not at checkout**: `create-intent` computes it in memory and stamps `tip_jar`/`gratuity_rate` into the PaymentIntent metadata, and the webhook applies it (before the Balance invoice, `FOR UPDATE`, payable-status guard) so an unpaid proposal never carries a client-elected gratuity (an admin MANDATE is the one pre-payment gratuity); that apply sets `origin` to NULL (a client election). The other writers: `server/utils/lineItemCancel.js` — the cancel-line-item gratuity removal/lowering, admin's only POST-PAYMENT path, stamps `origin = 'admin'` and clears `gratuity_floor_rate` — and the admin PATCH's mandate field `gratuity_mandate_total` (`server/utils/gratuityMandate.js`, spec 2026-08-10), the only PRE-payment/PRE-signature path, which writes `gratuity_rate` + `gratuity_floor_rate` together and never touches `origin`. That `'admin'` stamp is live logic, not an archival label: `PATCH /:id` (`crud.js`) reads it and skips the `'staffing'` relabel, so an admin-set gratuity keeps its `'admin'` provenance and fires no staffing-gratuity client notice on a later staffing change (the dollar still rescales at the same rate, as it does for every origin). `'staffing'` is stamped by that PATCH rescale path. One-off cleanup of pre-election rows: `scripts/reset-unpaid-gratuity.js` (skips mandated rows — `gratuity_floor_rate IS NULL` guard in both the SELECT and the under-lock re-check).
- `class_options` (JSONB — nullable) — set for class-category bookings; shape: `{ spirit_category: 'whiskey_bourbon' | 'tequila_mezcal' | null, top_shelf_requested: bool }`. Written only by `POST /api/proposals/public/submit` via a whitelist; other writers must preserve the same shape.
- `client_provides_glassware` (BOOLEAN, default `false`) — client is supplying their own glassware. What makes a Flavor Blaster add-on line item valid; surfaced on the admin proposal-detail Event card.
- **Bar menu print file** (staff event-details spec 2026-07-22): `menu_print_key` (TEXT, nullable — R2 object key under `menu-print/<proposalId>/`; replacing or removing a file orphans the old object, matching the drink-plan logo pattern since `storage.js` has no delete) and `menu_not_required` (BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false — admin's explicit "no printed menu for this event"). The staff-facing tri-state is derived, never stored: key present = `ready`, else `menu_not_required` = `not_required`, else `pending`. Set only through `setMenuPrintKey` in `server/utils/menuPrintFile.js`, by `/api/proposals/:id/menu-print` (admin/manager, the manual upload; its DELETE clears the key) and by the menu art Approve (`POST /api/proposals/:id/menu-draft/approve`, inside its transaction); every writer fires `reaccrueDuty` after its write is committed. Read by the staff event-details payload and the two download proxies, `/api/shifts/:shiftId/menu-print` (staff) and `/api/proposals/:id/menu-print` (admin), both through `server/utils/menuPrintFile.js`.
- `total_price`, `status`: draft | sent | viewed | modified | accepted | deposit_paid | balance_paid | confirmed | completed | archived
- `archive_reason` (TEXT, nullable) — only set when `status = 'archived'`. CHECK-constrained to `no_hire | client_cancelled | we_cancelled | event_completed | other | option_not_chosen`. The legacy `cancelled` status was migrated to `archived` with `archive_reason = 'client_cancelled'` in Task 2 of the automated-communication rollout; the enum no longer accepts `cancelled`. A cancelled BOOKED event (P6/fix #7) archives with `client_cancelled` (client cancelled) or `we_cancelled` (Dr. Bartender cancelled).
- `cancelled_at` (TIMESTAMPTZ, nullable), `cancelled_by` (TEXT, CHECK IN `client` | `admin`, mirroring `proposal_change_requests.cancelled_by`), `cancellation_note` (TEXT) — set by `POST /api/proposals/:id/cancel` when a booked event is cancelled. Distinct from generic archival: these are only populated on the cancel-event path.
- `event_timezone` (TEXT, NOT NULL, default `'America/Chicago'`) — IANA zone used for scheduling reminder communications and any future local-time renders.
- Client signature: `client_signed_name`, `client_signature_data`, `client_signed_at`

**Proposal Service Agreement.** The client-facing master agreement lives as a
versioned, bundled module at `client/src/data/eventServicesAgreement.js` and is
rendered in full by `client/src/pages/proposal/proposalView/AgreementText.js` (a
dependency-free markdown-lite renderer). At signing, the client sends the
`document_version` it rendered; `POST /api/proposals/t/:token/sign` validates it
against the allowlist in `server/utils/agreementVersions.js` and records it as
`proposals.client_signature_document_version`. Missing version → recorded as the
legacy `event-services-agreement-v2` (the pre-feature abridged block, kept in the
allowlist permanently); unknown version → rejected. No backfill of existing rows.
Every shipped version's text stays in the module (`AGREEMENT_VERSIONS`, a new
version never edits an old one), and the proposal page renders
`agreementForVersion(client_signature_document_version)`: a signed proposal shows
the text its client signed, an unsigned one shows the current version (legacy v2
text lives only in git history, so v2 rows fall back to current).
`server/utils/agreementVersions.test.js` pins the client/server lockstep.
Because client and server deploy independently (Vercel vs Render), the server
must ship before the client so it knows a new version before the client sends it:
a version lands in the allowlist one push ahead of the client bump that makes it
current. A rollback reverts the client before the server, for the same reason.
Versions: v3 (2026-06-04) the full master agreement; v4 (2026-09-27) amends
Section 8.1 only (on-site Additional Time bills every increment even inside a
package's base hours, hosted packages bill their per-guest extra-hour rate, and
on-site time is its own invoice accepted and paid before the added service
begins; time arranged in advance is priced in its addendum). Both texts are
pinned by sha256 in `agreementVersions.test.js`, so a shipped version cannot be
edited in place.
v4's hosted rate is "as stated in the Event-Specific Agreement", so the public
proposal payload carries `additional_time` (`{ hourly, per_guest_rate,
billed_guests }`, or null for a class or a proposal with no package), computed
by `server/utils/additionalTimeRate.js` from the live catalog row through
`pricingEngine.extraHourCharge`, the function the on-site extension bills with.
`ProposalPricingBreakdown` prints it under the Total, except under an agreement
version that states its own rate (`additionalTimeOnProposal` on each entry of
`AGREEMENT_VERSIONS`; false for v3). The rate follows the catalog and is not
locked at signing.

- Payment: `payment_type` (deposit | full), `autopay_enrolled`, `deposit_amount`, `amount_paid`, `balance_due_date`
- Stripe: `stripe_customer_id`, `stripe_payment_method_id` (for autopay off-session charges)
- Autopay claim: `autopay_status` (NULL | 'in_progress' | 'failed') and `autopay_attempted_at` — atomic row-claim used by both the scheduler and the admin manual charge so concurrent runs can't double-charge. Cleared to NULL when `payment_intent.succeeded` flips status to `balance_paid`. Stuck `'in_progress'` claims expire after 72h (F1: 24h equalled Stripe's idempotency-key lifetime, so a >24h webhook outage re-fired with an expired key and double-charged; a re-charge is now additionally guarded against Stripe's actual intent status via `server/utils/autopayDurableCharge.js`).
- Tracking: `view_count`, `last_viewed_at`

Event identity: proposals/shifts/drink_plans carry `event_type` (id) + optional `event_type_custom` (for "Other"). No free-text title. Display via `getEventTypeLabel({ event_type, event_type_custom })` helper, mirrored in `client/src/utils/eventTypes.js` and `server/utils/eventTypes.js`. Falls back to the literal string `'event'` when type is unset.

**proposal_addons** — Line items linking proposals to add-ons
- `proposal_id` FK, `addon_id` FK
- `addon_name`, `billing_type`, `rate`, `line_total`
- `quantity` (NUMERIC(10,2)), the pricing engine's OUTPUT display quantity for
  the add-on (`calculateAddonCost(...).quantity`), NOT the admin's unit count.
  Per billing_type: `per_guest`/`per_guest_timed` store guestCount, `per_hour`
  stores effectiveHours x count, `per_staff` stores the staff count,
  `per_100_guests` stores 100-guest blocks, `flat` stores the count. The
  `additional-bartender` slug is the exception inside `per_hour`: its bespoke
  engine branch stores RAW durationHours x count and never consults
  `minimum_hours`. Read back by `eventCreation.addonHeadcount` (divides by
  duration for headcount), `invoiceLineItems` (renders `quantity x rate`), and
  the admin editor's `recoverAddonQuantities`. Any code converting between this
  and a unit count MUST go through `server/utils/addonQuantity.js` on the server
  or `client/src/pages/admin/proposalEditor/formState.js` on the client, and the
  two must agree; re-feeding the stored figure to the engine as an input
  multiplied per_hour add-ons by their own hours (a $450 line repriced to
  $2,700, 2026-07-26) and inflated the gratuity staff basis with it. pg returns
  NUMERIC as a string, so readers coerce with `::float8` / `Number()`.
- `variant` (nullable) — optional addon-specific variant tag (e.g., `'non-alcoholic-bubbles'` swaps the Champagne Toast label without changing price)

**proposal_activity_log** — Audit trail
- `proposal_id` FK, `action` (created, sent, viewed, signed, deposit_paid, etc.)
- `actor_type` (system | admin | client), `actor_id`, `details` (JSONB)

**stripe_sessions** — Payment intent tracking
- `proposal_id` FK
- `stripe_payment_intent_id`, `stripe_payment_link_id`
- `amount` (cents), `status`: pending | succeeded | failed | canceled | processing
- `processing_at` (TIMESTAMPTZ, bank debit in flight, spec 2026-09-14): stamped by the `payment_intent.processing` webhook; "started" on every surface
- `invoice_id` FK→invoices (nullable, ON DELETE SET NULL): the invoice a processing payment is for; set by the invoice checkout rail at insert and by the processing webhook from intent metadata after an ownership check
- Partial index `idx_stripe_sessions_proposal_processing` on `(proposal_id, processing_at DESC) WHERE status = 'processing'` backs every in-flight lookup; the older `idx_stripe_sessions_proposal_pending` covers only pending rows; the plain `idx_stripe_sessions_proposal_id` backs the combined guard and the autopay scan, which read pending, processing and failed rows together

**proposal_payments** — Individual payment records
- `proposal_id` FK, `stripe_payment_intent_id`
- `payment_type`: deposit | balance | full | invoice | drink_plan_extras | drink_plan_with_balance (column VARCHAR(30))
- `amount` (cents), `status` — CHECK `proposal_payments_amount_nonneg` (`amount >= 0`); refund reversals live on `invoice_payments`, which is deliberately left unconstrained

**proposal_refunds** — Audit ledger for partial refunds
- `id` PK; `proposal_id` FK→proposals (ON DELETE RESTRICT, NOT NULL); `payment_id` FK→proposal_payments (ON DELETE RESTRICT, nullable); `stripe_payment_intent_id`; `stripe_refund_id`
- `amount` (INTEGER cents); `reason` (TEXT); `total_price_before` / `total_price_after` (NUMERIC dollars)
- `issued_by` FK→users (nullable — NULL = dashboard refund); `status`: pending | succeeded | failed; `created_at`
- `gratuity_cents` (INTEGER, nullable) — the gratuity portion attributed to this refund (P6/cancel). Label-based attribution has no gratuity concept, so the cancellation-refund path records how much of each refund row is the returned gratuity, keeping it auditable and out of contract-revenue adjustments. NULL for admin partial refunds that don't attribute gratuity.
- Approach A reconciliation: a refund drops `proposals.amount_paid` by the full refund amount and adjusts `total_price` only for contract-scope invoices (classified by linked invoice `label`: Deposit / Balance / Full Payment); extra-scope invoices leave `total_price` intact
- Excess derivation: ATTEMPTED AND PULLED (2026-07-26, revert 29316b10). The idea was that the portion of a refund exceeding the contract never lowers `total_price`. It was reverted because deriving the excess as `amount_paid - total_price` counts off-contract invoice money (syrups, manual fees) that lives in `amount_paid` but never in `total_price`, so it under-refunded. Do NOT re-attempt without netting that out first; see the do-not-reattempt note in `refundHelpers.js` and the invoice-provenance entry in the fix list. The `total_scope` column below is what actually ships this behavior, per refund row.
- `total_scope` (TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'contract', CHECK contract|overpayment) — the total_price rule for THIS refund. 'contract' = Approach A (refund lowers the total); 'overpayment' = the refund returns money held beyond the contract, so reconciliation leaves total_price alone. THREE writers set it (2026-09-15): cancel-line, where the fold already corrected total_price before the refund fired; the admin payment panel, where `total_scope` on `POST /api/stripe/refund/:id` carries the admin's choice, capped at the netted overpayment under the proposals row lock; and the `refund.created` webhook, which derives it for a dashboard refund from Stripe's `duplicate` reason or the netted overpayment. Under overpayment scope reconciliation first consumes the payment's UNCREDITED headroom (an invoice credit is capped at that invoice's remaining due while the whole intent rolls into `amount_paid`), because those cents are on no invoice and refunding them must reverse none. Durable on the row because webhook/sweeper adoption runs later with no caller memory; `refundExecute` adopts its own row BY ID so a stranded same-amount pending row can never rewrite the rule. Invoice `amount_due` is a separate question: it is left alone ONLY for invoices whose demand something else already rebuilt, i.e. UNLOCKED invoices with a total-tracking label (`proposalMoneyShared.TOTAL_TRACKING_INVOICE_LABELS` = Balance / Full Payment). Every other invoice — locked, or unlocked Deposit / Additional Services / Enhancement Lab / manual, none of which `refreshUnlockedInvoices` recomputes from the total — drops both figures, or a settled invoice flips to a client-visible `partially_paid` phantom balance on a still-live pay link.
- Partial unique index on `stripe_refund_id` (WHERE NOT NULL) — idempotency anchor shared by the synchronous route, the `refund.created` webhook backstop and the stale-pending sweeper

**proposal_groups** — Option groups behind the "compare your options" feature. One row bundles two or three sibling proposals (each an "option") behind one public `/compare/:token` link. `token` UUID (the public compare link), `client_id` FK, `chosen_proposal_id` FK → proposals (set first-writer-wins on the winning option's first settled payment; NULL until decided). A proposal joins a group via `proposals.group_id`. Created / dissolved by `server/utils/proposalGroups.js`.

**proposal_change_requests** — Client-portal change requests (the consent contract behind every booked-event change a client requests through the portal). One row per request, kept for history. Authoritative source for the admin pending-queue and per-proposal review card; created by the client-portal router, decided by the admin router or by `PATCH /api/proposals/:id` with `change_request_id`.
- `id` SERIAL PK; `proposal_id` FK → proposals (ON DELETE CASCADE — matches proposal_payments / activity_log so admin hard-delete cascades cleanly); `client_id` FK → clients (ON DELETE SET NULL)
- `status` VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL DEFAULT `'pending'` CHECK (`pending`, `approved`, `declined`, `cancelled`)
- `edit_window` VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL CHECK (`pre_booking`, `before_t14`, `inside_t14`) — computed at create time from the booking-window helper; freezes the policy that applied when the request was made even if T-14 crosses while the request sits pending
- `requested_changes` JSONB — only the allowlisted fields the client touched (guest count, hours, package, add-ons, event date, venue); the field allowlist is enforced server-side in `server/utils/changeRequests.js`
- `baseline` JSONB — snapshot of the same fields' current values on the proposal at request time, so the admin diff is stable even if the proposal mutates while the request sits pending
- `note` TEXT — optional client note ("we'd like to add a second bar")
- `price_preview` JSONB — full price preview computed at create time via the same path the admin editor uses (`pricingEngine`); persisted so the admin reviews the price the client saw
- `acknowledged_total` NUMERIC(10,2) **dollars** (matches `proposals.total_price`'s unit) — the total the client saw and acknowledged when submitting; persists the consent record
- `request_ip` VARCHAR(45); `request_user_agent` TEXT — audit trail for the consent contract
- `decided_by` FK → users (ON DELETE SET NULL); `decided_at` TIMESTAMPTZ; `decision_note` TEXT — admin decision metadata (decline reason or approve note)
- `cancelled_by` VARCHAR(10) CHECK (`client`, `admin`, `system`) — set when status is `cancelled` (client withdraws / admin clears stale / reaper auto-cancels on archive/complete)
- `created_at`, `updated_at` TIMESTAMPTZ (auto-updated by `update_updated_at_column` trigger)
- Partial unique index `idx_pcr_one_open(proposal_id) WHERE status = 'pending'` — enforces one open request per proposal so the client cannot stack pending requests and the admin queue stays clean
- Supporting indexes: `idx_pcr_status(status)` for the admin queue scan; `idx_pcr_proposal(proposal_id)` for per-proposal lookups

### Invoices

**invoices** — Invoice records (sit on top of proposals)
- `proposal_id` FK, `token` (UUID for shareable links)
- `invoice_number` (INV-0001), `label` (Deposit, Balance, etc.)
- `amount_due` (cents), `amount_paid` (cents) — CHECK `invoices_amounts_nonneg` (`amount_due >= 0 AND amount_paid >= 0`)
- `status`: draft | sent | paid | partially_paid | void
- `locked` (boolean), `locked_at` — freezes line items on payment
- `due_date`, `notes`
- A proposal's first invoice is created when the proposal enters the `sent` state. `createInvoiceOnSend` (via the `server/utils/invoiceHelpers.js` facade; implementation in `invoiceLifecycle.js` — the facade re-exports the invoice sibling modules `invoiceShared/LineItems/Lifecycle/Linking/Extras.js` with an unchanged public interface; idempotent on `proposal_id`) runs inside the same DB transaction as the status change on every →sent path: admin `POST /proposals` with `send_now`, admin `PATCH /:id` and `PATCH /:id/status`, and the public `POST /api/proposals/public/submit` quote-wizard submission. Client notification (`sendProposalSentEmail`) fires post-commit and is best-effort.
- A full payment on a deposit-terms proposal UPGRADES that first invoice rather than stranding it. `createInvoiceOnSend` picks the label from `payment_type` at SEND time (column default `deposit`), so a client who then chooses pay-in-full at checkout arrives with a $100 Deposit invoice open while a full-total capture lands, and the label-blind link credited $100 and dropped the rest against `linkPaymentToInvoice`'s cap. `upgradeDepositInvoiceToFull` (spec 2026-08-28; implementation in `invoiceLifecycle.js`, exported through the facade) re-derives that row FROM the proposal, inside the caller's transaction, after the proposal-side credit and BEFORE the invoice link: label `Full Payment`, `amount_due = total_price − external_paid`, `due_date = balance_due_date`, line items regenerated, invoice number unchanged. It locks the proposal row first (the global order is proposals then invoices, and on the webhook rails the status-guarded credit takes no lock on a confirmed or completed proposal) and carries its own guards: a confirmed, completed, archived or cancelled proposal is never relabelled, nothing owed means nothing to relabel, and money the proposal already held before this payment (which never reached the Deposit invoice) makes it decline so the link's overflow email says so. It is then guarded to an unlocked, unpaid, `sent` Deposit, so an invoice with money on it, locked, void, or differently labelled is never touched and null comes back. Lines regenerate only when the generated lines sum to the contract, which the generator guarantees whenever the snapshot has a package line (see `invoice_line_items` below); a package-less legacy or empty snapshot keeps its old lines, and the breadcrumb records `generated_sum_mismatch`. Both webhook rails wrap the call in a `SAVEPOINT deposit_upgrade`, so a deterministic failure inside it degrades to the old link path plus a Sentry warning, never to a payment that was not recorded. `refreshUnlockedInvoices` writes `amount_due` only where the row is still unlocked and still the label it decided on, so the admin re-price (the one caller that runs it outside the proposals lock) cannot put the deposit figure back onto a just-upgraded, locked Full Payment invoice. All three payment entrances call it on a full payment: `payment_intent.succeeded` and `checkout.session.completed` under the same archived/conflict guard as the Balance mint (a stale full intent on a cancelled event or a non-chosen option never relabels and reprices an invoice), and admin record-payment on its fully-paid gate (a balance record on deposit terms leaves `payment_type` alone, and the helper returns null on the paid, locked Deposit row). The `checkout.session.completed` rail now mints a Balance invoice only on a deposit link (commit `a0b8c1f0`), in parity with `payment_intent.succeeded`, so a full link that captures short never has a Balance minted beside the upgraded Full Payment invoice that already carries the whole remainder: a full-link capture is no longer double-billed. The lookup-and-link itself is one shared function, `linkOpenContractInvoice` (`invoiceLinking.js`): oldest open invoice, off-ledger labels excluded, `id` tiebreak, then `linkPaymentToInvoice`, returning the overflow payload. An overflow that still occurs is no longer silent, and neither is a refused credit (a `not_found`, `not_payable` or `no_remaining_due` refusal carries the whole payment as overflow): each entrance emails the `payment_failure` lane from its POST-COMMIT tail via `notifyLinkOverflow`, post-commit because `notifyAdminCategory` takes its own pooled connection. Rows already stranded in the old shape are repaired by the one-off `server/scripts/backfillFullPaymentInvoices.js`.

**invoice_line_items** — Line items per invoice
- `invoice_id` FK, `description`, `quantity` (NUMERIC — mirrors proposal_addons.quantity so a fractional add-on quantity flows into the auto-generated invoice without a 500), `unit_price` (cents), `line_total` (cents)
- `source_type`: package | addon | fee | manual
- `source_id` — the service_packages id on a package line, the service_addons id on an add-on line, else null
- Deposit / Balance / Full Payment lines come from `generateLineItemsFromProposal` (`server/utils/invoiceLineItems.js`, spec 2026-10-08) and add up to `proposals.total_price` whenever the snapshot has a package line: a discount prints negative and a surcharge positive, only adjustments marked "Client sees" get a line (the proposal page's own test), and whatever the itemized lines leave short of the contract (a hidden adjustment, an override, snapshot drift) is folded into the package line. A gap larger than the package lands on one "Pricing adjustment" line instead. A NULL `total_price` or a package-less snapshot folds nothing. Every fold is logged; a gap the snapshot does not explain sends Sentry `invoice_lines_unexplained_fold`, and the "Pricing adjustment" branch sends `invoice_lines_fold_fallback`. No money is ever computed from lines: `amount_due` comes from `total_price` and the invoice amounts, and the only line readers that compute anything (`extrasLinesAreFolded` and cancel-line) read Drink Plan Extras invoices, which have their own writer. One display-only reader sums contract lines: the public invoice route's `contract_step` (`server/utils/invoiceStep.js`, spec 2026-10-16 section 7) shows Contract Total, the step and the Invoice Total only when a Deposit, Balance or Full Payment invoice's lines sum to `total_price` exactly and the invoice lists no refund, and nothing it computes is stored or charged.

**invoice_payments** — Junction linking invoices to proposal_payments
- `invoice_id` FK, `payment_id` FK → proposal_payments
- `amount` (cents); negative rows are refund reversals written by reconciliation
- `refund_id` FK → proposal_refunds (ON DELETE SET NULL) — stamped on reversal rows only, attributes a refund to the exact invoice(s) it walked onto; NULL on positive rows and pre-upgrade reversals (display falls back to the gross-applied clamp)

*Off-ledger invoice labels:* `OFF_LEDGER_INVOICE_LABELS` (`server/utils/proposalMoneyShared.js`) holds labels whose invoice money lives entirely OUTSIDE `proposals.total_price` (genuinely additive, invoice-only upsells). Non-empty again since 2026-07-26: exactly `['Service Extension']`. (History: `Enhancement Lab` left the set on 2026-07-20 when lab additions switched to folding into `total_price`; the consumer machinery then sat wired as a no-op until the extension label joined.) Membership flips four behaviors: the Stripe webhook skips the `amount_paid` roll-up for a payment linked to such an invoice (`paymentIntentSucceeded.js`), `refreshUnlockedInvoices` and the Balance `lockedTotal` never count it (`invoiceLifecycle.js`), refund reconciliation leaves the contract alone (`refundHelpers.js`; extension payments are also excluded from the refund planner's candidate list by an anti-join, so the admin refund cannot land on them), and the open-invoice lookup that picks which invoice a contract payment credits excludes it at all three payment entrances. That fourth one matters because the lookup is otherwise label-blind (oldest `sent`/`partially_paid` row wins), so an open `Service Extension` invoice would absorb a contract payment and leave the contract invoice unpaid: each of the three now carries `AND NOT (label = ANY($2::text[]))` against this set, in `stripeWebhookHandlers/paymentIntentSucceeded.js` (since 2026-08-03), `routes/proposals/actions.js` (admin record-payment), and `stripeWebhookHandlers/checkoutSessionCompleted.js` (the payment-link rail), the last two brought to parity 2026-08-28. The label is reserved: admin invoice create and edit refuse to mint an invoice into it or rename one out of it (`routes/invoices.js`). Extension money is deliberately NOT in `CONTRACT_LABELS`: it never enters `total_price` or `amount_paid`, so treating it as contract money would either forgive the contract by the extension amount or count side money as contract revenue.

### Service Extensions (on-site added bar time)

**service_extensions** — One row per staff request for more bar time (spec 2026-07-25). Money is SIDE money: the paid extension lives as its own invoice + payment under the off-ledger `Service Extension` label (see above), and `proposals.total_price` / `amount_paid` / `pricing_snapshot` never move; the only contract mutation is `proposals.event_duration_hours` plus the single linked shift's denormalized duration and end_time. Owned by `server/utils/serviceExtension{Pricing,Settle,Payroll,Notify,Sweep}.js` and the routes in `server/routes/serviceExtensions/`.
- `proposal_id` FK → proposals (ON DELETE CASCADE); `shift_id` FK → shifts, `requested_by_user_id` FK → users, `invoice_id` FK → invoices (all ON DELETE SET NULL)
- `contracted_end_time` / `requested_end_time` VARCHAR(20): display strings ("8:00 PM"); `contracted_duration_hours` / `requested_duration_hours` NUMERIC(4,1)
- `amount_cents`, `gratuity_cents`: INTEGER CENTS; CHECK `service_extensions_money_nonnegative_check` keeps both >= 0 (the pricing module already refuses a negative delta; this is the DB backstop). The gratuity delta joins the payroll pool: accrual adds `SUM(gratuity_cents)` over the proposal's `paid` rows (`payrollAccrual.js`)
- `hosted_product_confirmed` BOOLEAN (hosted per-guest packages only, else NULL); `terms_version` TEXT looks up the versioned client-facing copy in `server/data/extensionTermsCopy.js`, which THROWS on an unknown version rather than showing text the client never agreed to
- `client_accepted_at` TIMESTAMPTZ, `client_accept_ip` VARCHAR(64), `client_accept_ua` TEXT: the acceptance artifact, stamped once by the public accept route (COALESCE keeps the FIRST acceptance; a double-tap cannot rewrite it)
- `status` CHECK: pending | paid | expired | cancelled | overridden. Every exit from `pending` goes through the claim UPDATE in `serviceExtensionSettle.js` (`... WHERE status = 'pending'`), so a settle/expire race or a replayed webhook resolves to exactly one winner
- `finalized_at` TIMESTAMPTZ: stamped only after the post-settle side effects (payroll hours + staff greenlight) have run. A `paid`/`overridden` row with `finalized_at` NULL is a crash casualty: the settle committed but payroll still holds the old hours and no bartender was told; the sweep's heal finds and finishes it
- `override_by_user_id` FK → users (SET NULL), `override_reason` TEXT, `expires_at` TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL, `created_at`, `updated_at`
- Indexes: partial UNIQUE `idx_service_extensions_one_pending (proposal_id) WHERE status = 'pending'` (one live request per event; this is what turns a concurrent second request into a clean 409 instead of a double charge). Partial `idx_service_extensions_pending_expiry (expires_at) WHERE status = 'pending'` (sweep driver); `idx_service_extensions_invoice (invoice_id)` (the webhook's extension discriminator); `idx_service_extensions_proposal_status (proposal_id, status)` (payroll gratuity addend); partial `idx_service_extensions_unfinalized (updated_at) WHERE finalized_at IS NULL AND status IN ('paid','overridden')` (crash-recovery heal driver)

*Contract hours vs worked hours (spec 2026-09-30):* `settleExtension` moves `proposals.event_duration_hours` (the hours the bar WORKS) and deliberately nothing else; the hours the contract was PRICED at, which the snapshot, `total_price` and every `proposal_addons.quantity` were computed from, do not move. `server/utils/contractDuration.js` owns the one rule that recovers them: row hours minus the hours settled extensions added (`SETTLE_OUTCOMES` in `serviceExtensionOutcomes.js`: `paid`, `overridden`), never below the hours the first settled extension found contracted (the clamp covers a hand-reverted row after a refund; a clamp or a sub-0.5h result pages Sentry, and the corrupt case falls back to the row, never to an under-bill). Nothing is stored. Readers: the admin PATCH (`patchContractHours.js`: `contractHours` for `calculateProposal` and the gratuity resolve, `workedHours` for the UPDATE, the curfew gate and the shift sync), `foldExtrasIntoProposal` and `REPRICE_ADDON_SQL` (which selects the two aggregates and inverts at the contract's hours, so a second extension prices its add-on counts right), `lineItemCancel` (every inversion and the partial-removal write), `deriveStaffingRoster` (divides by the hours the quantity was priced at: the snapshot's `inputs.durationHours`, else the rule), the drink-plan lab and submit add-on pricing, `changeRequests.buildPreview` (the public options and switch routes pass no rows and stay byte-identical), `POST /proposals/calculate` (optional `proposal_id`), and `GET /proposals/:id` (`settled_extension_hours`, always a number, which the editor and `RemoteStaffingFeePrompt` subtract before inverting stepper counts). The PATCH refuses a duration CHANGE (value compare) while a request is `pending`, keyed `event_duration_hours`. Recovery for a paid extension the webhook failed to settle goes THROUGH the request (the admin override), never through a duration edit: a hand-moved hour is contract time and bills again on the next save. Payroll, shift end times, the curfew, the extension's own delta legs and the pre-signing pricers read the worked hours, correctly.

*Expiry sweep + heal:* `server/utils/serviceExtensionSweep.js` registers the `service_extension_sweep` scheduler (every 60 seconds, gated by `RUN_SERVICE_EXTENSION_SWEEP_SCHEDULER`, schedulerHealth heartbeat, in-process reentrancy skip so a slow tick never overlaps). Each tick first expires pending rows past `expires_at` (claim via `closeExtension`, void the invoice and cancel open intents, staff decline notice carrying the insurance warning), then `healUnfinalizedExtensions` finishes settled-but-unfinalized rows. A pending row whose invoice is already PAID is never expired: that shape means the client paid but the settle tail died, so the sweep leaves it pending and alerts admins to settle or refund by hand (throttled to once per 24h per extension). Load-bearing, not housekeeping: nothing else enforces the hard service stop at the contracted end.

### Stripe Payout Mirror (read-side; spec 2026-07-01)

Two new tables mirror Stripe payouts and their balance-transaction lines for a bank-level reconciliation view. Read-side only: no existing table is written. The `stripe_` prefix is load-bearing (plain "payouts" = staff payroll). All money is integer cents. Owned by `server/utils/stripePayoutSync.js`.

**stripe_payouts** — One row per Stripe payout (`po_...`)
- `stripe_payout_id` UNIQUE (`po_...`), `amount_cents` (net to bank), `currency`, `status` (paid | in_transit | pending | canceled | failed)
- `created_at_stripe`, `arrival_date`, `automatic`, `method`, `description`
- `livemode` — ingest skips non-live objects; column is the tripwire
- `failure_code`, `failure_message`
- `alerted_at` — atomic-claim gate so a failed-payout email fires exactly once across webhook/sweep/tab-open races
- `lines_synced_at` — NULL until the payout's balance txns are fetched; the sweep heals NULLs. A manual payout (`automatic` false: an instant payout or one sent by hand) keeps NULL for good, because Stripe lists balance txns by payout only for automatic payouts; its row has no line breakdown. Its debit (instant fee included) is listed inside the next automatic payout and stored there as a line, so an automatic payout's lines sum to its `amount_cents`; the sweep fetches again any automatic payout whose lines do not.

**stripe_payout_lines** — One row per balance transaction (`txn_...`)
- `stripe_balance_txn_id` UNIQUE (`txn_...`), `payout_id` FK → stripe_payouts (ON DELETE CASCADE); **NULL = in transit** (settled charge not yet paid out, or a manual payout's debit not yet claimed by the next automatic payout, so In transit nets out money already sent). Only `syncPayout` ever sets `payout_id` (ownership rule); the pending path is insert-only.
- `txn_type`, `reporting_category`, `amount_cents` (signed gross), `fee_cents`, `net_cents`, `available_on`, `description`
- `stripe_charge_id`, `stripe_payment_intent_id`, `stripe_refund_id` — Stripe-side keys used by the matcher
- `matched_kind` CHECK (payment | tip | refund | dispute | adjustment | unmatched) — the reconciliation verdict. `adjustment` also covers an instant payout's `advance` / `advance_funding` pair, which nets to zero, and a manual payout's debit listed inside an automatic payout (`payout`).
- `acknowledged_at` — read-side "known dead line" marker. Set once by the schema.sql backfill on the 119 pre-cutover baseline lines (mirrored by the 2026-07-02 backfill with `available_on` before the cutover; they predate payout tracking and can never match). Acknowledged lines are excluded from the stuck-line Sentry alert, the sweep's re-match loop, and the admin unmatched counts/badge, but stay visible in the payout detail (rendered with a "pre-cutover" chip). Matching semantics untouched: `matched_kind` keeps its value and `matchLine` never reads this column.
- Link FKs (all ON DELETE SET NULL): `proposal_payment_id`, `tip_id`, `proposal_refund_id`, `proposal_id`, `invoice_id`
- Indexes: `payout_id`, `stripe_payment_intent_id`, partial index on `matched_kind='unmatched'`

### Staff Payroll

Weekly Tue–Mon payroll: `pay_periods` (status open → processing → paid, plus `reopened`: the admin "I fucked up" state reached only from `processing`, editable like `open` for pending payouts but refused by every money writer because they all test `status = 'open'`; re-processing returns it to `processing`) → `payouts` (one per contractor per period; status `pending` | `no_draw` | `paid` — `no_draw` is the owner shape (spec 2026-08-07): born via `ensurePayout` when `contractor_profiles.takes_draw = false`, tracked and line-editable like pending, but excluded from owed rollups, period-close blocking, mark-paid, paystubs, and 1099 totals; the staff portal presents it as-if-paid ("Not drawn" + blended total); ALL payout creation goes through `ensurePayout` in `payrollProcessing.js` — an inline `INSERT INTO payouts` anywhere else is a defect; `total_cents` = clamped sum of its event lines PLUS payable duty lines, written ONLY by `recomputePayoutTotal(s)` in `server/utils/payrollProcessing.js` — the single-writer rule, spec 2026-08-06 §3.3: the clamp wraps the WHOLE sum so clawback debt nets against duty pay; any other `UPDATE payouts SET total_cents` is a defect; `payment_reference` TEXT holds the mark-paid trace: Zelle conf #, Venmo note, check number; admin-only, never in staff projections) → `payout_events` (per-event line items) + `payout_duty_lines` (typed duty pay: bar_rental / parking / equipment_supplies / hosted_supplies / menu_print / out_of_area / review_bounty / review_contest; derive-never-increment in accrual, removed rows kept forever so derivation cannot resurrect an admin removal, `held_state` mirrors the payout_events hold chip, review kinds may carry NULL `shift_id`; `duty_attributions` stages who-gets-it for the attributed kinds, and the period Process endpoint 409s while any attributed duty is missing or stale). Owned by `server/utils/payrollAccrual.js` (accrual + roster sweeps) + `server/utils/dutyLines.js` (triggers/reconcile/materializers) and `server/routes/admin/payroll.js` / `server/routes/admin/payrollDuty.js` (admin worklist + edits). All money integer cents; DB backstops `payout_duty_lines_amount_cap_check` (±$1,000) and `shifts_out_of_area_bonus_cap_check` (0..$250). Ship/period-open backfill: `scripts/backfill-duty-lines.js`.

Duty-pay satellite tables and columns (spec 2026-08-06): `staff_reviews` (review_date, stars 1..5, source google|thumbtack, `tt_review_id` UNIQUE bridging the existing `thumbtack_reviews` webhook ingest, status pending|confirmed|dismissed) + `staff_review_credits` (which staffers a review names; UNIQUE(review, user)) feed the $10 `review_bounty` / quarterly `review_contest` materializers. `shifts.out_of_area_bonus_cents/_attached_by/_attached_at/_locked_at/_locked_user_id` carry the discretionary Out-of-Area Bonus (locked once an approved staffer holds the shift; re-arms on drop). `proposals.remote_fee_prompted_at` (the Send popup asks once) and `proposals.venue_lat/venue_lng` (on-demand geocode cache for the remote-staffing check) support the client-side Remote Staffing Fee surfaces built in the out-of-area lane.

**payout_events** — one line per (payout, shift); `UNIQUE (payout_id, shift_id)`
- `contracted_hours`, `hours`, `rate_cents`, `wage_cents` (= hours × rate), `late`
- `gratuity_share_cents`, `card_tip_gross_cents`, `card_tip_fee_cents`, `card_tip_net_cents`
- `adjustment_cents` (admin-entered, may be negative for clawback debt — no line-level floor, the payout total clamps at 0), `adjustment_note`
- `line_total_cents` = wage + gratuity + card-tip net + adjustment
- `held_state` TEXT CHECK (`held` | `confirmed`), nullable — held-reimbursement lifecycle (fix #4, 2026-07-13). When a roster sweep finds an off-roster worker's line with a POSITIVE `adjustment_cents`, the line is HELD, not deleted: `held_state='held'`, hours + all payable components zeroed, `line_total_cents = 0` (tracked but non-payable; the reimbursement must be hand-confirmed at payroll, never auto-paid). Semantics live in this column, never in the free-text note. Sweeps never re-touch `held` rows. Any admin PATCH on a `held` line recomputes `line_total` (re-arms the money) and flips it to `confirmed`; `confirmed` lines are NEVER re-held (sticky through note edits and re-accruals). If the worker rejoins the roster, accrual re-seeds `hours` from `contracted_hours` (the held 0 is not admin-owned), restores wage, clears `held_state` to NULL, and the adjustment rides along. Held lines are excluded from paystub adjustment aggregates (`paystubData.js`, this-period + YTD) so the stub foots against net.

### Clients

**clients** — Client records
- `name`, `email`, `phone`; `idx_clients_email_norm` on `lower(btrim(email))` serves the Inbox personal-mail lookup (`personalMail.SQL.knownAt`)
- `source`: direct | referral | thumbtack | zola | website | calcom | instagram | checkcherry | other
- `notes`
- `communication_preferences` JSONB — `{sms_enabled, marketing_enabled}` (defaults true). Drives the Automated Communication system's send gating: `sms_enabled` is the SMS kill switch (STOP/START), `marketing_enabled` is the campaign unsubscribe. There is no email kill switch — email suppression comes from `email_status = 'bad'` alone. Rows also carry an inert `email_enabled` key (no writer ever set it; nothing reads it as of 2026-08-14; deliberately left in place rather than rewritten out). NOTE the column DEFAULT in `schema.sql` still includes it, so NEW rows get it too; dropping it from the default and backfilling existing rows is a separate migration decision.
- `email_status` (`ok` | `bad`), `phone_status` (`ok` | `bad`) — channel deliverability flags flipped on bounce/blocked-list signals.
- `email_harvest_status` (`not_needed` | `pending` | `harvested` | `failed`), `email_harvest_attempted_at` (lease + cooldown timestamp), `email_harvest_attempts` (failure counter for the retry cap) — track the Thumbtack email-harvest flow for email-less leads. Partial index `idx_clients_email_harvest_pending` powers the agent's `pending-harvest` queue.
- **OTP login** (`/api/client-auth`): `auth_token` (bcrypt hash of the 6-digit code), `auth_token_expires_at`, `auth_token_attempts` (per-account brute-force ceiling). `token_version` — manual session-revocation lever embedded in the OTP-issued JWT and checked in `clientAuth` middleware (mirrors `users.token_version`); bumping it kills a client's outstanding 7-day sessions without rotating `JWT_SECRET`. No automatic trigger today (login is OTP-only, no password to reset).
- **Intake de-duplication:** every intake path that de-duplicates (Thumbtack webhook, admin `POST /proposals`, public quote wizard, public SMS opt-in) resolves clients through `findOrCreateClient` (`server/utils/clientDedup.js`), which matches on email OR normalized phone (name-guarded on phone-only matches) and backfills NULL fields only — never overwriting an existing identity, so the unauthenticated wizard is safe. A phone + name match resolves only an email-less row, except for a caller passing `trustedPhoneMatch` (the Thumbtack webhook alone, and only on a request that passed its secret check), where it may also resolve an email-bearing row whose source is `'thumbtack'` (the webhook's own, or a client an admin entered as a Thumbtack customer, which the proposal form defaults to), so a harvested repeat customer is not re-created as a second client; no public form writes that source (the wizard and SMS opt-in hard-code `'website'`), so a self-entered identity is never reachable that way. The Cal.com webhook and admin `POST /clients` insert directly.

**sms_consent_log** — Append-only client SMS consent audit (A2P 10DLC, 2026-07-22)
- `client_id` (FK, ON DELETE SET NULL), `phone`, `consented`, `copy_version`, `copy_text`, `source_form`, `ip`, `user_agent`, `created_at`
- **Ownership rule (load-bearing).** The endpoint is unauthenticated and `findOrCreateClient` resolves an existing row by email ALONE, so knowing a stranger's email is enough to be handed their row. `recordSmsConsent` therefore writes only when the same submit CREATED the client (`findOrCreateClientDetailed` returns `{id, created}`). Without that, anyone could flip a real client's SMS on or off and forge a row in the log we would hand a carrier. Two further predicates on the UPDATE close the gap between owning a ROW and owning a NUMBER (creating a fresh row with someone else's phone passes the `created` gate): it refuses the row if it carries `sms_opt_out_at`, and it refuses if ANY client row holding the same normalized phone carries one. So no web form lifts an inbound STOP, per row or per number, and existing clients stay grandfathered on the preference they already had.
- Written by TWO callers, both via `recordSmsConsent` (`server/utils/smsConsent.js`) inside their own transaction: `POST /api/proposals/public/submit` when the quote wizard's consent checkbox is present in the body (`source_form='quote_wizard'`), and `POST /api/sms/opt-in`, the standalone `/sms` page (`source_form='sms_page'`). Keep this list current — it is the only place the writers are enumerated. `communication_preferences.sms_enabled` + an `sms_opt_in_at` / `sms_opt_out_at` stamp are written in the same call, reusing the exact jsonb shape `smsInbound.js` writes on STOP/START.
- `copy_text` is always resolved server-side from `server/data/smsConsentCopy.js` by version — never taken from the request body. The client posts only `sms_consent` and `sms_consent_version`. The consent sentence itself is defined once per side (`client/src/constants/smsConsent.js` for display, the server map for the record) and `server/utils/smsConsent.test.js` fails if they drift.
- The log records consent *changes*, not page submits: a repeat submit with the same answer and version appends nothing.
- Read by nothing at runtime. It exists to answer a carrier or a claimant, which is why `phone` survives client deletion and why the phone index is the primary one.
- **Clients only.** Staff SMS consent is `agreements.sms_consent`, the gate `server/routes/messages.js` enforces on staff sends, and is separately approved with Twilio. It deliberately does not write here; two consent records for one person would be able to disagree.

### Menu

**cocktail_categories** (5 rows) + **cocktails** (25 rows) — Cocktail menu
- Categories: Crowd Favorites, Light & Refreshing, Classic, Bold, Bartender's Picks
- Each cocktail: name, emoji, base_spirit, description, sort_order, is_active, ingredients (JSONB array of strings — used by the Shopping List Generator)
- `cocktails.upgrade_addon_slugs TEXT[]` — add-on slugs that must be purchased
  when the client's hosted package doesn't already cover them. Auto-added when
  the client selects such a cocktail on the planner.

**mocktail_categories** (4 rows) + **mocktails** (16 rows) — Mocktail menu
- Categories: Fruity & Refreshing, Creamy & Sweet, Sparkling & Light, Bold & Complex

### Menu art generator (spec 2026-10-01)

Three tables, written by the menu art routes and job (`/api/proposals/:id/menu-draft*`, described under its route table). The full `inputs` and `content` shapes are in spec section 6.

**menu_drafts**: one per event.
- `proposal_id` INTEGER NOT NULL UNIQUE FK → proposals (ON DELETE CASCADE); `drink_plan_id` FK → drink_plans (SET NULL); `run_id` UUID NOT NULL, the current run, which every job write is conditional on
- `inputs` JSONB NOT NULL: `planner_image_role` (`logo` | `reference` | `ignore`), `reference_key` (written only by Generate from its own upload, never accepted from PATCH), `reference_source` (`none` | `planner` | `upload`), `art_direction`, `drb_mark`, `image_model` (a record of the toggle at the last Generate)
- `content` JSONB NOT NULL: the words step's `words_status`, `words_error` and `words_heartbeat_at`; `title`; `headings`; `drinks[]` (each keyed, with `has_art`, `hidden`, `added_by_sync` and the AI flags); `also_at_bar[]`; `palette` (display only); `font_pairing`, the ink and accent colors, `text_backing`, the logo and mark settings, `background_label`. The job writes exactly one fill (the words step), conditional on `run_id`; every other write is a request under the `version` check
- `plan_fingerprint` JSONB NOT NULL; `dismissed_plan_hash` TEXT, the plan hash the plan-changed banner was dismissed for. CHECK `menu_drafts_json_shape_check` holds all three JSONB columns to objects (a JSON null or array would break every later PATCH)
- `version` (default 1), bumped by every content or inputs write (the requests' optimistic check); `art_version` (default 0), bumped by the job when a piece reaches `done`
- `approved_version`, `approved_art_version`, `approved_print_key`, `approved_planner_image_key`, `approved_at`, `approved_by` FK → users (no ON DELETE action); Approve writes them
- `created_at`, `updated_at` (trigger `update_menu_drafts_updated_at`)

**menu_draft_pieces**: one per image.
- `draft_id` FK → menu_drafts (CASCADE); `piece_key` (`background` or a drink key), UNIQUE (`draft_id`, `piece_key`); `run_id` UUID NOT NULL (a re-roll gives the piece its own)
- `status` CHECK `pending` | `working` | `done` | `failed`; `r2_key` (art under `menu-art/<proposalId>/<runId>/`); `error` (sanitized); `reroll_note`; `heartbeat_at` NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(), older than `MENU_ART_STALE_MS` on a pending or working piece reads as failed ("interrupted"); `started_at`, `finished_at`
- `tone` (`light` | `dark` | NULL) and `ground_rgb`, set on the background piece only
- `provider` NOT NULL CHECK (`openai` | `gemini`) and `model` NOT NULL, stamped when the piece is asked for; the job draws with them
- `drawn_count` (default 0), bumped each time the piece reaches `done`

**menu_art_calls**: the cap's ledger, the cost record and the audit trail (`server/utils/menuArtCap.js`).
- `proposal_id` FK → proposals (SET NULL, so a deleted event's spend still counts against the cap); `piece_key`; `run_id`
- `kind` CHECK `image` | `text`; `status` CHECK `reserved` | `sent` | `ok` | `failed` | `released`
- `provider` NOT NULL CHECK (`openai` | `gemini`) with no default, so a call can never be mislabeled; `model`, `size`, `error` (the sanitized descriptor), `provider_request_id`
- `created_at` NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(), indexed (`idx_menu_art_calls_created_at`, the rolling 24-hour count); `updated_at`, stamped by trigger `update_menu_art_calls_updated_at` on every status change; partial index `idx_menu_art_calls_open_run (run_id) WHERE status IN ('reserved','sent')` for settling by run

### Staffing

**shifts** — Event shifts
- `event_type`, `event_type_custom`, `client_name`, `event_date`, `start_time`, `end_time`, `location`
- `setup_minutes_before` (INTEGER DEFAULT 60) — informational crew setup lead time. `start_time` is **always** equal to service start; setup is NOT a change to the billable/pay window. Editable directly via `PUT /shifts/:id` (COALESCE — omitting it preserves the row). Auto-synced from the proposal's effective value by `createEventShifts` / `syncShiftsFromProposal`, but **only for single-shift events** (the `count !== 1` guard skips hand-built multi-shift events by design — the admin sets those per shift). Staff surfaces always read the shift's own value, so multi-shift events stay consistent. Back-of-house only — never sent to clients.
- `positions_needed` (JSON text array of canonical role labels — the FULL paid roster: bartenders + banquet servers + barbacks, e.g. `["Bartender","Bartender","Banquet Server"]`), `status`, `created_by`. Derived by `deriveStaffingRoster` (`server/utils/eventCreation.js`) from `num_bartenders` + the staffing add-ons; re-derivable by `scripts/backfill-positions-needed.js`. Always parse via `parsePositionsNeeded` (handles both the flat-array and legacy `[{position,count}]` shapes).
- `proposal_id` FK (nullable) — links to the proposal that created this shift (auto-created on deposit payment)
- `lat`, `lng` — Geocoded event coordinates
- `equipment_required` (JSON text array of tokens `portable_bar` / `cooler` / `table_with_spandex`, e.g. `["portable_bar","cooler"]`). Together with `supply_run_required` it gates the staff transport acknowledgment on a shift request.
- `supply_run_required` (BOOLEAN, default false) — the shift needs a Pilsen pickup / shopping run (hosted event OR any `requires_provisioning` add-on). Surfaced to staff as a logistics tag ("Bar Kit Only" vs warning).
- `supply_run_overridden` (BOOLEAN, default false) — set true when an admin manually toggles `supply_run` via `PUT /shifts/:id`; while true, `syncShiftsFromProposal` stops recomputing the default so the manual decision sticks.
- `auto_assign_days_before` — Schedule auto-assign N days before event; `auto_assigned_at` — timestamp of last auto-assign run

**shift_requests** — Staff applying for shifts
- `shift_id` FK, `user_id` FK (unique together)
- `position`, `status` (pending/approved/rejected), `notes`. `position` is the resolved canonical role and the only money-sensitive field here (payroll's tip split keys on `LOWER(position)='bartender'`). It is NULL on a fresh request and written at approval from the staffer's `requested_positions` (or an admin override). The request / assign / approve handlers and the position-resolution logic live in `server/routes/shifts.approval.js` (extracted from `shifts.js`); a `shift_requests_position_canonical` CHECK enforces canonical values.
- `requested_positions` (TEXT, default `'[]'`) — the staffer's RANKED role preferences (JSON array of canonical labels). Drives the computed waitlist (`server/utils/staffingClassification.js`): a pending request whose ranked roles are all full is treated as waitlisted (no stored "waitlisted" status). Empty = "any open role" (legacy / back-filled rows).
- `transport_acknowledged_at` (TIMESTAMPTZ) — set when the staffer acknowledges the equipment/supply transport requirement when requesting a transport-required shift; cleared when the event is no longer transport-required, and re-required on each submit if the logistics escalate.
- `acknowledged_at` TIMESTAMPTZ — set when the assigned staff member texts CONFIRM for the shift (Comms Phase 2 two-way SMS). Lives on the per-(shift, staff) row, not on `shifts`. Index `idx_shift_requests_user_id` on `user_id` supports the inbound-SMS nearest-approved-shift lookup.
- `beo_acknowledged_at` TIMESTAMPTZ — BEO read-receipt stamp set by `POST /api/beo/:proposalId/acknowledge`. Independent from `acknowledged_at` (shift CONFIRM); a staffer who has CONFIRMed the shift still must open the BEO to acknowledge it. Cleared on Unfinalize, on re-assign (`POST /:id/assign`), on approve-after-deny re-request, on `PUT /requests/:requestId` deny/approve, and on auto-assign promotion — so a stale ack from a prior cycle never carries forward.

**app_settings** — Configurable settings (auto-assign weights, max distance, etc.). Planner v2 adds flat scalar keys (value is TEXT; objects would stringify wrong): `shopping_buffer_spirits/mixers/garnish/supplies`, `pour_pace_per_hour`, `pour_split_cocktails/beer/wine` (quantity-engine knobs) and `margin_labor_rate` / `margin_supplies_per_guest` (packages margin defaults)
- `key` VARCHAR PK, `value` TEXT, `updated_at`

### Messaging

**sms_messages** — SMS message log (inbound + outbound)
- `id` SERIAL PK
- `user_id` FK → users (recipient)
- `shift_id` FK → shifts (nullable, for shift invitations)
- `group_id` UUID — groups messages from the same batch send
- `message_type` TEXT — e.g. general, shift_invitation, plus the Comms Phase 3 client-SMS touch types. Widened to `TEXT` in Phase 3 (was `VARCHAR(20)` with a 4-value CHECK constraint, now dropped) so automated client-SMS touch identifiers fit.
- `direction` TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'outbound' CHECK (`inbound`, `outbound`) — added for two-way SMS via Automated Communication Foundation
- `to_phone`, `body`
- `twilio_sid`, `status` — delivery tracking. CHECK now allows `received` (the status for an inbound message) alongside `sent` / `failed` / `queued`.
- `sent_by` FK → users (admin who sent)
- Two-way SMS additions (Comms Phase 2):
  - `client_id` INTEGER FK → clients (ON DELETE SET NULL) — links an inbound text to its client row so the admin thread UI can group by client
  - `read_at` TIMESTAMPTZ — marks an inbound message as seen; drives the unread badge
  - `metadata` JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT `'{}'`. Inbound rows carry `from`; `to`, the DRB number texted (Twilio's `To` since 2026-10-06; older rows hold `TWILIO_PHONE_NUMBER` and read as the 888); `media`, `[{ url, content_type }]` for picture messages; `outcome`, stamped when the row settles and equal to what `processInboundSms` returned (`thumbtack_relay`, `opt_stop`, `opt_start`, `help`, `client_message`, `unknown_sender`, `staff_confirm`, `staff_cant`, `staff_confirm_no_shift`, `staff_cant_no_shift`, `staff_confirm_ambiguous`, `staff_cant_ambiguous`, `staff_cant_race`, `staff_freeform`, `conversation`; only `staff_confirm` and `staff_cant` changed a shift; rows with no Twilio SID and pre-2026-10-06 rows carry none); plus the `thumbtack_relay`, `opt_keyword` and `help_keyword` flags. Human outbound rows from the Messages reply and Inbox carry `line` (`888`, `1922` or `0082`, from the number Twilio reports); Inbox sends also carry `send_id`
  - Index `idx_sms_messages_client_id` on `client_id`; partial index `idx_sms_messages_unread` on `client_id WHERE direction = 'inbound' AND read_at IS NULL` for the unread count; `idx_sms_messages_twilio_sid` on `twilio_sid` for inbound-webhook idempotency (dedupe a repeated Twilio delivery by MessageSid)

**sms_optouts**: the per-phone SMS opt-out record (spec 2026-10-06, decision 10). Writers and readers live in `server/utils/smsOptOut.js`.
- `phone_last10` TEXT PK: the `last10()` key (`server/utils/phone.js`), the one the inbound matcher uses
- `opted_out_at` TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL: when this opt-out began (a repeat STOP keeps it; a STOP after a START starts a new one)
- `source` TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (`keyword`, `twilio_21610`, `backfill`)
- `line` TEXT: the line key it arrived on; NULL when unknown, including every backfilled row
- `cleared_at` TIMESTAMPTZ: set by START or UNSTOP (a YES never clears it, spec amendment 32); the row stays as history, and only a row with `cleared_at IS NULL` is active
- Written by a STOP-set word to any line from any sender (`processInboundSms`), by Twilio 21610 at send (`sendSMS`) and by the status callback; cleared by START or UNSTOP. The Messages reply and Inbox refuse an active row on every line. The admin group send (`POST /api/messages/send`) does not consult it (it checks agreement consent only), and automated sends do not consult it yet (fix list)
- Seeded once by the boot backfill from the BODY of every inbound opt word: any of the eight STOP words, START or UNSTOP, whitespace-trimmed and case-insensitive; a YES never counts as a clear (spec amendment 32). Only a phone whose latest opt word is a STOP gets a row, dated by that row. The `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` re-runs on every boot as a no-op, so a START recorded later is never undone. Never DELETE from `sms_optouts`: stamp `cleared_at`, or the next boot re-seeds the row

**sms_status_orphans**: a Twilio status callback that beat its row (review fix, spec 2026-10-06). A human send writes its `sms_messages` row after Twilio answers, so a fast `failed` or `undelivered` callback can arrive first; `POST /api/sms/status` keeps it here instead of dropping it.
- `twilio_sid` TEXT PK; `status` TEXT NOT NULL, always `'failed'`; `error_message` TEXT, the same `Twilio <code> (<status>)` text a row would get; `created_at` TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
- Written only when the callback's UPDATE matched no outbound row; never for a sid that belongs to an inbound row; first failure per sid wins (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`). The Messages reply folds an early failure into its own row right after its insert (`status='failed'` with this `error_message`), and the Inbox reader left-joins the orphan table by `twilio_sid`, so a text that never arrived never counts as a reply; the orphan row stays

**message_log** — Append-only ledger of client-facing outbound messages (email + SMS), one row per send attempt. Written fire-and-forget at the `sendEmail` / `sendSMS` choke points via `server/utils/messageLog.js` (so coverage is structural, not a per-callsite checklist), and read newest-first into the admin `GET /proposals/:id` response for the event-detail Messages card (`client/src/pages/admin/eventDetail/MessageLogCard.js`). Keyed by `proposal_id` so a row logged at proposal stage shows on the event page after conversion. Lead-marketing sends pass `meta: { skipLog: true }` and are excluded; recipients that resolve to no client row are skipped.
- `id` SERIAL PK
- `proposal_id` INTEGER NOT NULL FK → proposals (ON DELETE CASCADE)
- `client_id` INTEGER FK → clients (ON DELETE SET NULL — matches every other client FK; deleting a client degrades the ledger row instead of blocking the delete)
- `channel` TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (`email`, `sms`)
- `message_type` TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT `'other'` — machine label (e.g. `proposal_sent`, `drink_plan_ready`, `shopping_list_ready`, `signed_and_paid`, `payment_received`, and since 2026-10-06 `cancel_confirmation`, `gratuity_disclosure` and `change_request_decision`, which used to log as `'other'` against the client's newest proposal); friendly display labels live in `client/src/utils/messageTypes.js` (an unlabeled type falls back to its subject). Which types count as an Inbox reply is `server/utils/answeringMessageTypes.js` (`ANSWERING_MESSAGE_TYPES`, plus `PROPOSAL_SEND_MESSAGE_TYPES`, the deliveries a proposal-send activity row absorbs); its writer-scan test (on the push gate) pins each allowlisted type's write sites, so a rename, removal or new writer fails it
- `recipient` TEXT NOT NULL — email address or E.164 phone
- `subject` TEXT — email subject / SMS body preview; the UI label fallback for `'other'` rows
- `status` TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (`sent`, `failed`, `bounced`, `complained`) — `sent` means accepted by Resend/Twilio; `failed` means the send threw on our end (quota, bad config, malformed); `bounced` / `complained` are reserved for the Resend delivery webhook to stamp after the fact
- `error_message` TEXT — truncated provider/error text on a failed send
- `provider_id` TEXT — Resend id / Twilio SID, stored for delivery tracking (intentionally not returned by the read)
- `sent_by` INTEGER FK → users (ON DELETE SET NULL) — the admin who clicked send in the compose-and-confirm modal, and (Inbox spec 2026-10-06) on every human-click client send the spec's section 2 lists, handed over by the route as a value: the Messages-page reply (lane sms-lines), Send to client and the send_now create (email and SMS), the event-details notice (both halves), the refund notice from the admin refund, cancel refund and cancel-line routes, the line-item removal notice, the record-payment receipt, the cancel confirmation, the gratuity disclosure and the change-request decision (lane send-attribution). Shared helpers take it as an optional parameter and never read req.user; sendAndLogSms takes an optional sentBy too and carries it into the SMS ledger meta, so a human send through that automated primitive is attributed. NULL means an automated send (scheduler, the refund.created webhook, the stale-pending refund sweep, the website quote wizard). sendAndLogSms never writes sms_messages.sender_id, which stays the mark of a hand-typed text (the Messages page and Inbox)
- `body_edited` BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false — true when the admin hand-edited the template copy before sending
- `created_at` TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
- Index `idx_message_log_proposal (proposal_id, created_at DESC, id DESC)` serves the newest-first per-proposal read; the `id DESC` tiebreaker keeps ordering deterministic for rows sharing a `created_at`.
- Index `idx_message_log_provider_id (provider_id)` backs the Resend delivery webhook matching a bounce/complaint event to its ledger row (otherwise a sequential scan on a growing table).
- Index `idx_message_log_client_created (client_id, created_at DESC) WHERE client_id IS NOT NULL` backs the per-CONTACT read described below, which filters on `client_id` rather than `proposal_id` and so cannot use the index above.

**Per-contact message history** — `server/utils/contactMessageHistory.js` answers "everything this contact has actually received", newest first.

- **`message_log` is the primary and near-complete source.** It is written at the `sendEmail` / `sendSMS` choke points, so it captures scheduler sends and human sends alike (2,200 sent rows in prod as of 2026-08-11).
- **`automated` is derived from `message_log.sent_by`, never from which table a row came from.** NULL means the scheduler sent it, a set value means a human did; 2,165 of those 2,200 rows are NULL. Assuming the source table instead would label the system's own drip as human-sent, inverting the one fact the view exists to convey.
- **`scheduled_messages` is a safety net, not a peer, and is anti-joined.** The dispatcher's handlers send through the same choke points *without* `skipLog`, so 1,154 of its 1,196 sent rows already have a `message_log` twin milliseconds apart. A dispatcher row is emitted only when no ledger twin exists within a two-minute window, i.e. only when a ledger write failed. Without that anti-join the drawer lists every drip twice, once tagged automated and once human.
- **`email_sends` joined in lane mkt-g**, which added its `client_id`; the leg still sits behind a once-per-process `information_schema` probe so the module works before and after. `idx_email_sends_client_sent_partial (client_id, sent_at DESC) WHERE client_id IS NOT NULL` backs it, because the table grows by one row per recipient per blast.
- **Scope limit, phase 1:** a contact with no proposal has nothing in *either* current source. `logClientMessage` returns early without a `proposal_id`, and every client-directed `scheduled_messages` row is proposal-anchored too, so the ~254 proposal-less clients have an empty history until they are actually sent a campaign, which the mkt-g leg then shows.
- Only rows that went out count. Pending is a plan; suppressed, failed, and bounced are non-events. `'complained'` is deliberately kept on the campaign leg: a spam complaint proves receipt, and it is the most important row in a do-not-double-tap view.
- The `email_sends` leg filters `sent_at IS NOT NULL AND status NOT IN ('failed','bounced','queued')`, **not** `status = 'sent'`: `emailMarketingWebhook.js` monotonically advances a delivered send past `'sent'` to `delivered`/`opened`/`clicked`, so an equality filter would hide most real campaign sends.
- Rows carry `subject` alongside `kind`, because `message_type` is the literal `'other'` on 1,007 of 2,200 rows while `subject` is populated on all of them. The UI humanizes via the existing `client/src/utils/messageTypes.js` `messageTypeLabel(kind, subject)`.

**inbox_actions**: the Inbox taps (spec 2026-10-06, section 7), append-only; Undo stamps `undone_at` and nothing is deleted.
- `id` SERIAL PK; `person_key` TEXT NOT NULL (`c-`, `s-`, `p-` or `t-`: the rules' identity as resolved when the tap was made, never a phone number; the engine folds an old key onto the person's current one); `action` TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (`claim`, `release`, `done`, `snooze`, `wake`, `reopen`); `until_at` TIMESTAMPTZ, set exactly when the action is a snooze (CHECK); `user_id` FK users (ON DELETE SET NULL); `created_at`; `undone_at`
- Index `idx_inbox_actions_person (person_key, created_at DESC)`. The enumerated CHECK is also a DO-block DROP + ADD, the file's pattern, so a widening reaches existing databases
