A person's true all-time total is `sum(ledger) + sum(paid payouts)` with **zero overlap by construction**. `boundary_exception=true` is the escape hatch for a post-boundary payment covering pre-boundary work that matches NO payout (verification asserts each exception row matches no payout, so it can never double-count).
- Index `idx_sph_contractor_paid_on(contractor_id, paid_on)`. Read by the payment-history + 1099 tax-totals endpoints (`server/routes/admin/payrollTax.js`, `server/routes/staffPortal/payouts.js`). **Supersedes `legacy_cc_payouts`** for earnings/tax display — read this table ONLY.

**Columns added to `users` (staff payment import):**
- **`users.exclude_from_1099` BOOLEAN DEFAULT false** — persisted per-person 1099 exclusion (foreign contractors e.g. Zul on W-8BEN, not 1099-NEC). Admin-toggleable post-import via `PATCH /api/admin/payroll/tax-totals/:userId/exclude`; drives the 1099 tax-totals include/exclude toggle (excluded rows render muted with a struck total and are dropped from the 1099 count).
- **`users.import_source` TEXT** (`'payment_history_import'`) — provenance marker for accounts created by the import: drives the admin staff-list "imported" chip, the audit trail, and the undo path. Distinct from the `cc_id LIKE 'legacy_cc:%'` stub marker, so imported placeholders and legacy CC stubs stay distinguishable.

**cc_import_phase0_failures** — Durable retry queue for Phase 0 attachment fetches
- `id` SERIAL PK; `source_url`, `source_entity`, `source_row_hash`
- `attempt_count` INTEGER DEFAULT 0, `last_error`, `last_attempted_at`, `resolved_at`, `resolved_r2_key`
- `given_up_at`, `given_up_reason` — operator marked the loss accepted (counts as 'resolved' for the sunset gate)
- UNIQUE `(source_url, source_entity)`; partial index `idx_cc_import_phase0_failures_active(attempt_count) WHERE resolved_at IS NULL AND given_up_at IS NULL` keeps the retry sweep cheap

**cc_import_runs** — Per-run telemetry log
- `id` SERIAL PK; `phase` INTEGER, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `status` CHECK ('running' | 'succeeded' | 'failed' | 'partial')
- `rows_processed`, `rows_inserted`, `rows_skipped`, `rows_errored`, `error_summary`, `notes` JSONB (audit trail per row decision when relevant)

### Columns added to existing tables (CC import)

- **`clients.cc_id` TEXT**, **`proposals.cc_id` TEXT**, **`users.cc_id` TEXT** — partial unique indexes `WHERE cc_id IS NOT NULL` on each. Real CC ids on `clients` / `proposals`; on `users` the value is a stub-format prefix `legacy_cc:<scope>:<id>` for imported bartenders we cannot pay through the modern payouts system. The `cc_id LIKE 'legacy_cc:%'` predicate is the canonical stub check used by `server/utils/payrollGuards.js`.
- **`proposal_payments.legacy_charge_id` TEXT** — Stripe charge id (`ch_…`) imported from Check Cherry. NEVER pass to Stripe API as `payment_intent:` — it MUST be `charge:`. Native (non-import) rows leave it NULL. Per-proposal partial unique index for re-run dedup; global partial unique index catches a misroute across proposals.
- **`proposal_payments.payment_method` TEXT** — free-form method label (`card` | `card_external` | `cash` | `check` | `paypal` | `other` | `unknown`). Populated by the importer; nullable on native rows.

## Pricing Engine

Located in `server/utils/pricingEngine.js`. Pure functions, no database dependencies.

**Inputs**: package data, guest count, duration, number of bars, number of bartenders, selected add-ons, selected syrups.

**Calculation flow**:
1. **Base cost**: Flat rate (BYOB) or per-guest rate (Hosted) with small-event tier pricing
2. **Bar rental**: First bar fee + additional bar fee per extra bar
3. **Staffing**: 1 bartender per 100 guests included; extras at $40/hr
4. **Add-ons**: Calculated per billing type (per_guest, per_guest_timed, per_hour, flat). `per_guest_timed` is the one billing type whose `rate` is NOT the whole per-guest price: it is the FOUR-HOUR price, and every hour past four adds `extra_hour_rate` per guest (`calculateAddonCost`). Four catalog rows carry it today (the-foundation, the-formula, the-full-compound, mocktail-bar) and more will follow, so no display code may key on those slugs. Every surface that prints such a rate, client-facing or admin, renders it through `client/src/utils/addonRateLabel.js` (`timedPerGuestRateLabel`), which produces the qualified string "$8/guest (4hr) + $2/guest/hr after" and degrades to the "(4hr)" floor when a column is too narrow or `extra_hour_rate` is absent. A bare per-guest-timed rate on a client surface is a quote the client can hold us to; do not hand-roll the qualifier.
5. **Syrups**: Three-pack bundles ($30) + singles ($12), optimized for best price
6. **Gratuity** (client-elected at sign-and-pay and persisted only at payment, §8.3 — OR admin-mandated pre-payment, spec 2026-08-10): `gratuity_rate × staffCount × hours` where staffCount = bartenders + additional-bartender addon (NOT barbacks/servers). Added on top of the total as staff pass-through money (never diluted by discounts/override). Layered on the forced `"Shared Gratuity"` over-ratio surcharge. Helpers: `getStaffNoun`, `computeGratuityBasis`, `gratuityLineAmount`, `deriveGratuityRate` (dollars-total → stored rate with floor/sanity validation, re-asserting the floor on the derived ROUNDED rate), `recomputeSnapshotGratuity` (drift-free checkout recompute). Labels live in the shared `server/utils/gratuityLabels.js` (client mirror `client/src/utils/gratuityLabels.js`). The admin proposal editor and `PATCH /:id` have exactly ONE gratuity write path — the mandate field `gratuity_mandate_total` (spec 2026-08-10, resolved in `server/utils/gratuityMandate.js`: dollars > 0 sets `gratuity_rate` + `gratuity_floor_rate` together, null clears a real mandate, absent carries forward, locked once signed or paid); the election keys `tip_jar`/`gratuity_total` stay refused, the stored rate/jar otherwise carry forward untouched (rescaled at the same rate on a staffing change, `origin='staffing'`), and post-payment removal goes through cancel-line-item (which also clears the mandate). A mandate floors the client's checkout election on BOTH jar answers, replacing the $50 no-jar floor. `POST /calculate` previews the STORED rate, or a DRAFT mandate when `gratuity_mandate_total` is sent.
7. **Total**: Sum of all components, plus gratuity

`server/utils/addonQuantity.js` is the one sanctioned server-side conversion between the engine's add-on OUTPUT (`proposal_addons.quantity`) and an add-on's INPUT unit count: `storedToInputCount`, `effectiveHoursFor`, `storedIsInputCount`, `countLabelFor`. Every reprice, cancel-line and fold path that needs a count out of the column goes through it rather than re-deriving the inversion, and it is the manual twin of the admin editor's `recoverAddonQuantities` (`client/src/pages/admin/proposalEditor/formState.js`). See the `proposal_addons` schema note for what the column actually stores.

**Adjustment lines** (`proposals.adjustments`, spec 2026-10-16 section 3) are `{ type: 'discount' | 'surcharge', label, amount, visible }` with `amount` in positive dollars, netted into the service total by the engine (`max(0, subtotal + net)`; the client gratuity rides on top and is never touched by a line). Two optional markers ride on a line the system writes, and the engine ignores both: `source` (`'agreed_price'` for a Set agreed price line or a converted override, `'refund'` for a refund line) and `refund_id` (`proposal_refunds.id`, only alongside `source: 'refund'`). `server/utils/adjustmentRules.js` is the one definition of a line: the engine's own coercions (`adjVisible`, where a missing `visible` means hidden; `adjAmount`, `Math.abs(Number(amount) || 0)`; `adjLabel` with the "Discount" / "Surcharge" fallback), `adjSignedCents` (a line's signed amount in integer cents, rounding the magnitude half away from zero: a per-line rounding the engine never does, since it nets raw dollars and rounds the total once, exact for every line the validator accepts), `isProtectedAdjustment` (true for either `source`, decided by the marker and never by a label an admin can edit), `isWholeCents` (no part finer than a cent, float noise tolerated, nothing rounded), and `validateAdjustments`, the server bound for lines that arrive in a request: at most 50 lines, each a discount or a surcharge, an amount of $0 or more and under $1,000,000 (the bound the override had) and in whole cents, where blank counts as $0 and a finer amount is refused, never rounded (so per-line cents equal the engine's net), a text label of at most 255 characters (`invoice_line_items.description`), `visible` coerced to a boolean, and the markers only in the shapes above, `refund_id` within int4 (`proposal_refunds.id` is SERIAL). `insertAdjustmentBreakdownRow` places a server-written line's row in `snapshot.breakdown` where the engine would have put it: the engine pushes adjustment rows after every other service row and the client `Gratuity` row last, only when the client gratuity is above zero, so the row goes just before a last `Gratuity` row while `snapshot.gratuity.total` is above zero and is appended otherwise (a last `Gratuity` row with no client gratuity is an admin's own line). Its client twin `client/src/utils/adjustmentRules.js` carries the constants, the five readers and `isWholeCents` (so a client input can refuse what the server would), never the validator or the placement; `server/utils/adjustmentRules.test.js` imports the twin and pins parity, with cases chosen so a one-token drift of either twin fails it. Each adjustment row the engine pushes into `snapshot.breakdown` carries the line's coerced `visible` (display only; no amount reads it). The admin views tag a hidden line "hidden from client" through `client/src/utils/hiddenRows.js` `hiddenRowFlags(snapshot)`, on the desktop (`PricingBreakdown`, the editor preview included) and in the phone Financials (`financialsOf`): a row that carries `visible` is read from it, and a row without it (every snapshot written before 2026-10-17, or a row a server writer placed without the key) is matched to `snapshot.adjustments` from the bottom of the breakdown, by label and signed cents, and read from its line, where a missing `visible` means hidden; the client `Gratuity` row (the placement rule above) and every service row are never tagged. `PricingBreakdown` draws no remove control on a cancel target marked `protected_line`.

**Client rows** (spec 2026-10-16 section 4): the rows a client sees are built on the server, so a hidden line's label never reaches a browser and its amount arrives only inside the rows: folded into the package row or, when it is larger than the package row, as one visible "Pricing adjustment" row (spec D3, the invoice rule). `buildClientPriceRows` (`server/utils/clientPriceLines.js`, pure) ports the proposal page's own row set row for row (the package row as `package.base_cost + staffing.total` under the package name the page shows, bar rental, every add-on including $0 ones, syrups, and the VISIBLE adjustments signed, through the `adjustmentRules` coercions), in whole cents, then folds the gap to a target into the package row, or into one "Pricing adjustment" row when the package row would go below $0. That decision is `decideFold` (`server/utils/foldDecision.js`), the one rule `invoiceLineItems.foldToContract` also asks (`skipped` when there is no package line: no fold), and the fallback's one name is `PRICING_ADJUSTMENT_LABEL` there, used by both. Rows are service rows only: the page adds the gratuity row from `snapshot.gratuity` itself (`client/src/pages/proposal/proposalView/pageRows.js`), because a create-intent quote re-prices the gratuity in client state. Row keys count visible adjustments on their own, so a key never reveals where a hidden line sat. The builder's fold report (gap, hidden net, explained, unexplained) never leaves the server; what the snapshot explains is read as `foldToContract` reads it (its own service total less its subtotal and visible lines), so hidden lines, an override and the engine's zero clamp are explained and only drift is not. `reportPriceRowsFold` (exported by `publicToken.js`, with a route tag so the client portal reports through the same rule) sends Sentry `public_rows_fold_fallback`, `public_rows_no_package` or `public_rows_unexplained_gap`, by proposal id only, once per proposal per route per process. `sanitizeSnapshotForPublic` (`server/utils/publicSnapshot.js`, pure) is the public copy of a snapshot, a denylist over the engine's shape: no `subtotal`, `breakdown`, `total_price_override` or hidden adjustment; a visible line keeps only `type`, `label`, `amount`, `visible`; `package.base_cost` is set so `base_cost + staffing.total` equals the folded package row (in a package fold, a browser on the old bundle mid-deploy then still draws rows that add up; a fallback's "Pricing adjustment" row it cannot draw); anything that is not a snapshot object comes back null. `publicPriceView` runs both from one builder call against the page target, the snapshot total less a positive client gratuity (`snapshotServiceCents`), for the three routes that hand a booking's snapshot to a token holder: `GET /api/proposals/t/:token`, the switch's response (both `buildPublicProposalPayload`), and `GET /api/drink-plans/t/:token`. Accepted, per the spec: the catalog calculator and the options drawer still let a token holder recover the hidden NET by arithmetic, never a label or a line.

**The extras fold** (`server/utils/proposalExtrasFold.js`, `foldExtrasIntoProposal`) is the client-triggered re-pricing writer: drink-plan submit (only when the submit carries extras on a booking with a package, a guest count and hours; an extras submit on a booking missing any of them skips the fold, and its price never moves), the Enhancement Lab and cancel-line call it inside their own transaction, with the proposal locked FOR UPDATE. Its first statement is the legal-hold guard (spec 2026-10-16 section 5.3): a held booking is refused before any read or write with 409 `NOT_CHANGEABLE`, "This booking can't be changed online right now." (a client-facing refusal never names the hold), judged on the id of the row the caller locked. A caller rolls back its own earlier writes on that throw (submit's plan update, add-on upserts and bar count), so a held booking saves nothing; `server/routes/drinkPlans.legalHold.test.js` pins it. Two callers refuse earlier, so the fold's guard is their backstop. Cancel-line, an admin surface, refuses a held booking right after its row lock with 409 `LEGAL_HOLD` and copy that names the hold, and its targets answer not eligible, so the admin breakdown draws no remove button. The Lab GET reports a held booking's open lab as `locked`, so the page offers no addition; a Lab PUT that arrives anyway is refused before any write with a 400 save error, never the window's 409, because the page turns any 409 into its locked bar, which would call the refused additions "locked in". The module also exports `computeCatalogSnapshot(client, proposal, { adjustments } = {})` (plan 2026-10-16-price-override-removal, Task F2; spec section 8.2): the engine output for a booking's current inputs at today's catalog with no override (the package row, the reprice-ready add-ons, the stored syrup selections as they are, the row's bars, bartenders, gratuity rate, tip jar and mandate floor, the contract's hours, and the row's adjustment lines unless `adjustments` is given). It reads only, on the transaction client that read the row (the pool is refused, so its reads see the row's state), and before any query it refuses a row missing any field it reads (a null id, guest count or hours counts as missing), naming the fields. It builds its engine inputs through the same internal `catalogEngineArgs` as the fold's catalog legs and final snapshot, so the override conversion prices a live booking exactly the way the fold does; `proposalExtrasFold.catalog.test.js` pins that argument for argument.

**Legal hold** (`server/utils/legalHold.js`, spec 2026-10-16 section 5.3, amended 2026-10-17): `isLegalHold(id)` answers whether a booking is on legal hold, against `LEGAL_HOLD_PROPOSAL_IDS` in `server/utils/staleProposalSweep.js`. It fails closed: an id is read only as a positive safe integer or a string of plain decimal digits, and anything else reads as held, because Postgres 17 reads `'6_00'` and `'+0x258'` as 600 where `Number()` gives NaN. A held booking keeps the price it has. It is the one function every guard this spec adds calls, each at the writer it protects (the spec names the editor PATCH, change-request approval, the client package switch, the extras fold and the refund line), refusing with no write; a client-facing refusal never names the hold. Older readers take the list directly (the stale sweep itself, the Inbox engine and read job), and `server/scripts/backfillFullPaymentInvoices.js` keeps its own copy. The list is read at call time, never at load: the sweep's load-time require graph already pulls in about 50 modules (the pricing engine among them), so a guard added to any of them would form a require cycle in which a load-time read keeps the sweep's half-built exports; a missing list throws rather than answering "not held". Suites ADD synthetic ids through `__setLegalHoldIdsForTest(ids | null)` (null clears them; refused under `NODE_ENV=production`): a test list never replaces the real one, so no process reads a real hold as unheld through the seam, and no suite reads or writes a real held booking.

**Editor price lines** (spec 2026-10-16 sections 5 and 5.1): the admin editor no longer edits the override; it writes lines. "Set agreed price" (`client/src/pages/admin/proposalEditor/AgreedPriceControl.js`, math in `agreedPrice.js`) takes the agreed SERVICE price (before the client gratuity) and writes one line for the difference against the live preview: its own `source: 'agreed_price'` line is backed out first and replaced in place (label reset, visibility kept), else a hidden line is appended, and an equal price removes it. Apply waits for a fresh preview, and at a $0 preview it refuses rather than guess past the engine's clamp (with the agreed-price line present, or a discount among the other lines). A booking that still carries an override shows it read-only and cannot set an agreed price until the conversion. The rows live in `AdjustmentsSection.js`, which asks inline before a `source: 'refund'` line is removed or lowered (that bills refunded money again). Every save carries `adjustments_token`, the md5 of the stored lines from the admin GET, and never the override (`patchBody.js`), so a tab that saw old lines cannot write over a server-written line: the PATCH answers 409 `STALE_EDIT` and the editor offers Reload, which refetches with the editor open. The phone edit sheet sends the same token and shows its stale notice with Reload on a 409; its own pre-save re-read refuses only when what the save rests on moved (`sheetBasis` in `editSheetView.js`: the payload it writes back, add-on rows included, the readout's money and the lock), never on `updated_at` alone, which every client page view moves. Both editors open read-only on the GET's `legal_hold`. The Remote Staffing Fee prompt seeds each try from its own fresh GET, token included, and answers a 409 with try again. A refused desktop save after a contact edit says the contact was saved. The breakdown's cancel-line targets on both editor parents come from `proposalEditor/useCancelTargets.js`: adjustment targets are positional (`adjustment:<index>`), so a set is shown only for the read it was fetched after, a new read or a save hides it at once, and a late answer is dropped; binding a target to its line on the server is on the fix list.

The result is stored as a `pricing_snapshot` JSONB on the proposal for historical accuracy. Payroll pools both gratuity labels (`payrollMath.extractGratuityCents`) and gates the gratuity portion of accrual on the proposal being funded (`amount_paid >= total_price`); the Stripe webhook records the amount actually charged (additive), never a flat `= total_price`, so a mid-flight total change can't misstate `amount_paid`. On first delivery that same webhook also APPLIES the client's tip-jar election from the intent metadata, before the credit and before `createBalanceInvoice` (so the Balance invoice sees the gratuity-inclusive total); the write is metadata-validated against the DB CHECK and bracketed in a `SAVEPOINT`, so a failed gratuity apply never rolls back the payment record. Payment-status reconciliation (demotion when a price move outruns `amount_paid`, promotion back to `balance_paid` once paid covers the corrected total) + overpayment detection is centralized in `server/utils/proposalStatus.js` (`reconcileProposalPaymentStatus`).

## Proposal Rules & Send Flow

Proposal business rules (BYOB bundle logic, add-on filtering, and selection guardrails) live in a pair of mirrored modules, following the same manual-twin discipline as `eventTypes.js`.

- **`client/src/utils/proposalRules.js`**: shared client rules consumed by both the public Quote Wizard and the admin proposal cockpit (`ProposalCreate.js`). Pure functions, no React or state. Provides bundle-slug resolution, `stripIncludedAddons` (drops bundle-covered add-ons), and the guardrail checks the UI enforces.
- **`server/utils/proposalRules.js`**: the CJS server twin. Exposes TWO gates, and the split matters. `validateProposalRules` is the authoritative INTERACTION gate: bundle mutex, mixer mutex, hosted-package guest minimum, glassware-dependent add-ons, parent-addon requirements, guest-count ceilings. It does NOT enforce the VISIBILITY rules that decide which add-ons a client may pick at all (`applies_to` lane gating, the hidden parking fee, the >100-guest glassware cap) — those lived only in the browser until `visibleAddonsFor` was added here as the CJS twin of the client's `filterAddons`. Any server path that prices or persists a client-chosen add-on set must gate on `visibleAddonsFor` FIRST and then `validateProposalRules`; the client copies are convenience, never the boundary. Together they stop a stale browser tab or a scripted POST from bypassing either class of rule. It throws `ValidationError` on a bad selection. The rule gate and `stripIncludedAddons` run on every proposal-write path: admin `POST /proposals`, admin `PATCH /:id` (edit), and the public `POST /api/proposals/public/submit`.
- **`server/utils/sendProposalSentEmail.js`**: post-commit, best-effort helper that emails the client when a proposal enters the `sent` state. It never throws (the proposal and its invoice are already committed, so an email failure is recoverable; the admin resends from the detail page) and reports failures to Sentry. Invoice creation is not here. It runs inside the caller's DB transaction via `createInvoiceOnSend`.
- **`server/utils/paymentInFlight.js`**: bank debit in flight (spec 2026-09-14). The ONE definition of "a payment on this proposal is still settling": a `stripe_sessions` row in `processing` less than 14 days old. `findInFlightPayments` feeds the public invoice and proposal payloads, the admin invoices payload and the balance reminder ladder (which defers a day instead of nagging); `IN_FLIGHT_LATERAL_SQL` rides the polled payment-state route in one round trip; `assertNoPaymentInFlight` is what the three checkout rails call before minting: a processing row refuses from the DB, then the newest recent pending or failed intents are read from Stripe (one retry, ten-second timeout) and refused when processing, succeeded, or awaiting microdeposit verification. Unknown fails closed (502). `findStaleProcessingPayments` feeds the hourly monitor's Sentry warning for a row older than 8 days.
- **`server/utils/bankPaymentProcessingNotify.js`**: post-`payment_intent.processing` helper. One client email ("We received your bank payment", email only, gated like the receipt) and one admin email (urgent_booking for a deposit or full payment, routine_finance otherwise), fired after the handler releases its connection. Best-effort, never throws.
- **`server/utils/paymentFailedClientNotify.js`**: post-`payment_intent.payment_failed`-webhook helper that emails the client their card was declined, sent once per proposal. The slot is claimed with an atomic `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` (partial unique index `idx_proposal_activity_payment_failed_client`) before the fetch and send, so concurrent Stripe retries cannot double-email; a failed send releases the claim. Extracted from `routes/stripe.js` to keep that file under the size cap. Best-effort — owns its try/catch, never throws into the webhook.

`adminWriteLimiter` (`server/middleware/rateLimiters.js`) caps the proposal-write endpoints that can fire client emails (`POST /proposals` and `PATCH /:id/status`) at 10 requests/minute, keyed by admin user id (not IP, so an office NAT does not share a bucket). That is far above any human admin workflow while capping the email-spam blast radius of a compromised admin token.

`clientPortalWriteLimiter` (same file) caps the client-portal write endpoints — `POST /api/client-portal/proposals/:token/calculate`, `POST /api/client-portal/proposals/:token/change-requests`, and `POST /api/client-portal/proposals/:token/change-requests/:id/cancel` — at 30 requests/minute, keyed by the authenticated `req.user.id` (falling back to `req.ip`) so one client cannot exhaust another's budget. The cap covers the live price-preview round-trips a portal session makes while keeping a runaway client from fan-firing admin notifications.

`publicReadLimiter` (same file, 100 requests/15min per IP) carries the read-only public pricing endpoints — `GET /api/proposals/public/packages`, `POST /api/proposals/public/calculate` (a POST in verb but a pure pricing preview, no write), and `GET /api/proposals/group/:token` — because they share a visitor's IP budget with proposal loads and the quote wizard; the 20/15min `publicLimiter` stays on the public write endpoints (capture-lead, submit, and `POST /api/sms/opt-in`).

## Potion Planning Lab (post-booking only)

The drink plan is created **only after the client books** — the Stripe deposit
webhook (`server/routes/stripe.js`) flips the proposal to `deposit_paid` and
calls `createEventShifts` → `createDrinkPlan` (idempotent). There is no
pre-deposit Exploration phase: the Lab is a single linear post-booking flow
(welcome → serving-style quick pick → per-module steps → confirmation). No
drink-plan token or link is generated before the deposit lands.

As a safety net for any `/plan/:token` link already sitting in an inbox from a
pre-deposit proposal, `GET /api/drink-plans/t/:token` returns a locked payload
`{ locked: true, proposalToken }` whenever the linked proposal has not reached
a post-booking status (`isDrinkPlanPreBooking` allowlist in
`server/utils/drinkPlanAccess.js`, fails safe on null/unknown). The client
renders a lock screen ("your drink plan unlocks after you book") with a link
back to the proposal instead of mounting the wizard — relevant for legacy
links only, since no new pre-deposit plans are created.

When a drink plan's linked proposal has a hosted package
(`service_packages.category = 'hosted'`), the Potion Planning Lab skips the
serving-style quick pick, the spirits selection, and the beer/wine selection
— these are already fixed by the package. A compact `HostedGuestPrefsStep`
replaces them, asking only how guests lean (mostly beer / cocktails / wine /
balanced). Cocktail cards show a "+$X/guest" badge when the drink needs
ingredients outside the package's stocked catalog; selecting such a drink
auto-adds the relevant specialty-ingredient add-on to the proposal with a
toast confirmation. Add-ons already covered by the package are suppressed
from every offer point. Logic lives in `client/src/pages/plan/data/packageGaps.js`
(pure helpers) + `server/utils/pricingEngine.js` (parity helpers); the data
model is `service_packages.covered_addon_slugs` and `cocktails.upgrade_addon_slugs`.

## Booking-Window Policy

Lead-time-based booking rules, computed by one pure helper so the client never
re-derives date math:

- `server/utils/bookingWindow.js` — pure lead-time tier (14-day full-payment /
  72-hour staffing-hold) computation; the single source of truth for
  booking-window policy. `getBookingWindow({ eventDate, eventStartTime, now }) →
  { hoursUntilEvent, fullPaymentRequired, lastMinuteHold }`. UTC math (consistent
  with the rest of the date code); null `event_start_time` ⇒ 00:00 of the event
  date (conservative — classifies more urgent).
- `server/utils/lastMinuteAlert.js` — admin + broad-net staff SMS blast for
  ≤72h "staffing hold" bookings (`notifyLastMinuteBooking(proposalId)`,
  self-guarding/non-blocking).
- `server/utils/lastMinuteStaffingConfirmation.js` — Touch 2.2: the moment a
  held proposal's shift becomes fully staffed, fire one client email + one
  client SMS naming the bartender(s) + phone. The trigger
  `confirmStaffingIfFullyStaffed(shiftId)` atomically flips
  `proposals.last_minute_hold` true→false via `RETURNING id`; only the caller
  that wins the flip fires the notify (one-shot guard, no double-sends under
  concurrent fills). Called from `server/routes/shifts.js` (manual assign +
  request approval) and `server/utils/autoAssign.js`.

**Behavior.** Bookings ≤14 days out require full payment — the deposit option is
**rejected at the `create-intent` payment-intent gate** (`FULL_PAYMENT_REQUIRED`;
never silently coerced) and hidden in the proposal UI via the server-computed
`payment_policy` block on `GET /t/:token`. Bookings ≤72h out additionally set
`proposals.last_minute_hold` (in the `payment_intent.succeeded` webhook, in-tx,
inside the `isFirstDelivery` idempotency guard), warn the client both
pre-payment (proposal page) and in the first-payment email that the booking is
subject to staff availability with a full refund if unstaffable, and trigger an
admin + staff SMS blast post-commit (once-per-payment via `isFirstDelivery`).
The hold clears automatically when the linked shift is fully staffed (approved
`shift_requests` ≥ `positions_needed` length — autoAssign's definition) via the
approve/assign handlers in `server/routes/shifts.js`. The Stripe charge path,
`balance_due_date` COALESCE, and the autopay scheduler are untouched (full
payment naturally sets `status='balance_paid'`, which the scheduler never
claims). Refunds on the rare unstaffable case are manual (Stripe dashboard) by
deliberate scope choice.

## CC-Import Behavior Changes

The Check Cherry import landed several skip gates and best-effort hooks across the money path, drink-plan scheduling, and reschedule code. They are documented here as a single load-bearing list because each one is invisible-by-design (the data flow looks identical on a normal proposal) and easy to lose on a refactor.

1. **`scheduleDrinkPlanNudge` early-return when no drink plan exists** (`server/utils/drinkPlanNudge.js:215-221`). Before scheduling the email + SMS nudge rows, the helper checks `SELECT 1 FROM drink_plans WHERE proposal_id = $1 LIMIT 1` and returns early if none. CC-imported proposals are created without drink plans; without this guard every `paymentIntent.succeeded` retransmission and every reschedule cascade would re-fan a nudge into the dispatcher. Pre-existing tests now seed a `drink_plans` row before invoking the helper.

2. **`createDrinkPlan` post-insert hook fires `scheduleDrinkPlanNudge` best-effort** (`server/utils/eventCreation.js:77-86`). Whenever a drink plan is created (admin route OR the Stripe deposit webhook flow), the helper is invoked in a try/catch that reports to Sentry on failure. The drink plan persists either way — scheduling is not on the durability boundary. This is the re-enroll path for cc-imported proposals whose drink plan is created post-import.

3. **`accruePayoutsForProposal` skips when any participant is a legacy CC stub** (`server/utils/payrollAccrual.js:71-78`, helper in `server/utils/payrollGuards.js`). Returns `{ skipped: true, reason: 'legacy_cc_stub_participant' }` BEFORE any DB writes — we never want to INSERT into `payouts` referencing a stub user (we cannot pay them through Stripe Connect anyway). The check is per-proposal because the whole event's accrual is structurally atomic. After a stub participant is replaced with a real user (the v1 `/admin/cc-import/review` page was deleted 2026-07-07; stubs are resolved directly on the user record now), the admin can re-fire via `POST /admin/proposals/:id/reaccrue-payout`.

4. **`rollForwardLateTip` + `clawbackTip` filter legacy CC stub bartenders out of the per-bartender split** (`server/utils/payrollLateTip.js:41-68`, `server/utils/payrollClawback.js:44-75`). The bartender query JOINs `users` and tags `(u.cc_id LIKE 'legacy_cc:%') AS is_stub`; stubs are excluded from the denominator. On a mixed shift, real bartenders absorb the stub's share. When ALL approved bartenders on the shift are stubs, the entire operation is skipped under `{ skipped: true, reason: 'all_bartenders_are_legacy_cc_stubs' }` BEFORE any DB writes — `rolled_forward_at` / `refunded_amount_cents` stay untouched so a future de-stub can replay. When ZERO bartenders are on the shift, the tip is marked rolled-forward / advanced (permanent — nothing to retry).

5. **`SKIP_REANCHOR_TYPES` in `server/utils/rescheduleProposal.js:15`** — defense-in-depth set of cc-import-injected scheduled-message types that must NOT be re-anchored when an imported proposal's `event_date` moves. Currently `Set(['post_event_wrap_up_email'])`. The wrap-up handler already registers with `offsetFromEventDate: null` (which short-circuits via the `if (!newScheduledFor) continue;` branch in `reanchorPendingMessages`), but the explicit skip set guarantees a future handler-meta change cannot silently start re-anchoring wrap-up rows.

## Third-Party Integrations

### Stripe (Payments)
- **Flow**: Admin creates proposal → Client views → Client signs contract + pays (deposit or full) on a single screen → Stripe PaymentIntent confirmed → Webhook updates status → Event shift auto-created
- **Payment options**: Pay $100 deposit (default) or pay in full. Deposit option includes autopay checkbox.
- **"Is the client overpaid?" is not `amount_paid - total_price`.** Syrup-only `Drink Plan Extras` and free-text manual invoices (Damage Fee, Travel) roll into `proposals.amount_paid` and never into `total_price`, so the raw difference counts money the client still owes for goods DRB is still buying. `invoiceExtras.sumOffContractPaidCents()` is the one answer: it nets collected money whose label is outside `IN_TOTAL_PRICE_LABELS` (Deposit / Balance / Full Payment / **Additional Services** / **Enhancement Lab** — the last two ARE contract money, minted from or folded into `total_price`). `Drink Plan Extras` cannot be classified by label at all, so it is decided by persisted line items via `extrasLinesAreFolded()`: any add-on or bar-rental line means the submit transaction folded the whole invoice into `total_price`; syrup-only means the fast path folded nothing. Consumed by the cancel-line overpayment computation. Do NOT substitute `CONTRACT_LABELS` here — that is a refund-scope classification, and using it under-refunded a client by $300 in review.
- **Balance-invoice monitor**: `server/utils/balanceInvoiceMonitor.js` watches one invariant hourly and is **alert-only** — it reads `proposals`/`invoices`, writes only `proposal_activity_log`, and never creates, edits, or voids an invoice. It reports both directions: a client billed MORE than `total_price − amount_paid` across their open invoices (never legitimate), and a client owing a balance with no payable invoice (the 2026-07-20 incident, where eight clients were dunned toward a page with no pay control). One Sentry warning plus one `proposal_activity_log` row per offending proposal, and one batched admin email per run, throttled to once per 24h per proposal via `balanceScheduler`'s marker pattern. Gated by `RUN_BALANCE_INVOICE_MONITOR`.

  **The invoice DERIVATION is deliberately unchanged.** `refreshUnlockedInvoices` still derives a Balance from `total_price − external_paid − Σ(locked invoice amount_due)` and still skips every label outside Deposit / Balance / Full Payment. Both are known defects — they over-billed four clients in July 2026 — and a rewrite was built, reviewed, and PULLED on 2026-07-28 after two review rounds found new money bugs in it. The blocker is architectural: `invoices.amount_due` has four writers that do not agree (`refreshUnlockedInvoices`, `invoiceExtras.findOrRefreshExtrasInvoice`, `lineItemCancel.reconcileOpenDeltaInvoices`, and `routes/drinkPlans/lab.js`, which runs its own copy of the discredited locked-sum formula and overwrites the refresh in the same request), and `Drink Plan Extras` carries two different money semantics under one label depending on submit path. Read `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-28-invoice-derivation-and-monitor-design.md` before attempting it again. Until then this monitor is the safety net.

- **Autopay**: When enrolled, Stripe saves the payment method via `setup_future_usage: 'off_session'`. A Stripe Customer is created for the client. Balance is auto-charged on the due date (default: 14 days before event) by the hourly scheduler in `server/utils/balanceScheduler.js`. A durable charge record (`server/utils/autopayDurableCharge.js`) persists each balance PaymentIntent to `stripe_sessions` at charge time and guards a stale-claim re-charge against Stripe's actual intent status, so a >24h webhook outage can't drive a second off-session charge (F1).
- **Off-session charges**: Admin can manually trigger via `POST /api/stripe/charge-balance/:id` or the scheduler runs hourly.
- **Alternative**: Admin generates a reusable Payment Link via `POST /api/stripe/payment-link/:id`. Amount follows the same booking-window policy as the client flow — full total inside the 14-day window, otherwise the $100 deposit — and tags the link with `metadata.payment_type` so the webhook settles it correctly. It carries NO gratuity election metadata, so a link-paid proposal never collects a prepaid gratuity (deliberate consequence of election-at-payment: the election belongs to the client's sign-and-pay moment).
- **Webhook events**: `payment_intent.succeeded` (deposit, full, balance, drink-plan-extras), `checkout.session.completed` (deposit OR full via Payment Link, read from `metadata.payment_type`, AND bartender tips, branched on `metadata.kind`); `payment_intent.processing` (a bank debit confirmed, money not yet moved: `stripe_sessions.status = 'processing'`, and every checkout rail, the balance reminder ladder, the autopay guard and the public payloads read it through `utils/paymentInFlight.js`); `payment_intent.payment_failed` (a decline, or a bank debit that bounced days later: releases the processing state); `refund.created` (THE refund reconciler: a refund issued in the Stripe dashboard lands in the ledger, and an in-app one is backstopped) and `charge.refunded` (the payroll tip clawback only, which reads the version-proof `amount_refunded`). The live endpoint must subscribe to all of these; the processing and failed events were added 2026-09-14, the two refund events 2026-09-15
- **Deposit**: $100 (configurable via `STRIPE_DEPOSIT_AMOUNT` in cents)
- **Tip Payment Links**: Each onboarded bartender has a reusable Stripe Payment Link with `metadata.kind = 'tip'`, `metadata.bartender_user_id`, and `metadata.tip_page_token`, provisioned by `server/utils/tipPaymentLinks.js`. On `checkout.session.completed` the webhook cross-validates the metadata against `payment_profiles.tip_page_token` (DB is source of truth — if metadata's bartender_user_id disagrees, the DB user_id wins) and inserts a row into `tips`. Admin can regenerate the Stripe link via `POST /api/admin/contractors/:userId/tip-page/regenerate-stripe`, or rotate the tip token entirely via `POST /api/admin/contractors/:userId/tip-page/rotate-token` — the emergency break-glass route used when a printed QR card or URL is compromised. Rotation issues a fresh UUID, deactivates the old Stripe Payment Link, creates a new one, and invalidates in-flight checkouts on the old link (the webhook drops sessions whose `metadata.tip_page_token` no longer matches the DB).
- **Partial refunds**: Admin-issued via `POST /api/stripe/refund/:id`, which takes a `total_scope` of `'contract'` (default) or `'overpayment'`. `planRefund` (in `server/utils/refundHelpers.js`) auto-targets the largest refundable charge (no spanning); under overpayment scope it prefers the charge carrying the most UNCREDITED headroom, but only among charges that can cover the amount, so the no-spanning rejection still names the true maximum. The refund ORCHESTRATION (pending row → `stripe.refunds.create` → `applyRefundReconciliation` in a tx → redundant-pending-row cleanup) lives in `server/utils/refundExecute.js`, shared by this route and the cancel-event refund endpoint so no caller ever calls `stripe.refunds.create` directly. `applyRefundReconciliation` (in `refundHelpers.js`) holds a row-lock on the proposals row, applies Approach-A label-conditional `total_price` correction (Deposit/Balance/Full Payment invoices adjust the contract total; extra-scope invoices leave it intact), nets the aggregate invoice reversal, and writes to the activity log. The synchronous route does the Stripe API call then reconciliation; an idempotent `refund.created` webhook handler is the backstop that self-heals a failed post-Stripe write AND records out-of-band Stripe-dashboard refunds. The partial unique index on `proposal_refunds.stripe_refund_id` (WHERE NOT NULL) is the shared idempotency anchor.

  **Overpayment scope (spec 2026-09-15).** A refund that returns money held beyond the contract must not lower `total_price`, or the proposal stays overpaid by the same figure and the contract shrinks on every attempt. The admin chooses on the payment panel; the default and the cap come from `overpaymentCents` (`amount_paid - total_price - sumOffContractPaidCents`, the same netting cancel-line uses, exposed on `GET /api/proposals/:id` as `overpayment_cents`). The cap is asserted inside the transaction that writes the pending row, holding the proposals row lock, so two concurrent submits cannot both spend it. Reconciliation consumes the payment's UNCREDITED headroom first (`linkPaymentToInvoice` caps an invoice credit at its remaining due while the webhook rolls the whole intent into `amount_paid`, so an overpaying payment is part-credited): those cents are on no invoice, so refunding them reverses no invoice. On the dashboard path the scope is `'overpayment'` when Stripe's reason is `duplicate` OR the netted excess covers the refund, never from the reason string alone.

  **Refund line (spec 2026-10-16 section 6).** A contract refund lowered `total_price` and nothing else, so the next re-price (an editor save, a drink-plan fold, cancel-line) rebuilt the total from lines that never knew about the refund and billed it again. Now, in the same transaction and under the same row lock, right after the money UPDATE, `applyRefundReconciliation` calls `appendRefundLine` (`server/utils/refundLine.js`), which writes the contract portion as a visible discount line, `{ type: 'discount', label: 'Refund', amount: contractCents / 100, visible: true, source: 'refund', refund_id }`. `refund_id` is the succeeded `proposal_refunds.id` exactly as pg returns it, an integer and never a string: `validateAdjustments` refuses any other shape, and the editor sends the lines back on every save. The writer runs the line through `validateAdjustments` before storing it, so it can never store a line the editor refuses. The line goes into `proposals.adjustments` and `pricing_snapshot` together: appended to `snapshot.adjustments`, its row placed by `insertAdjustmentBreakdownRow` (just before a last client `Gratuity` row) when the snapshot has a breakdown, and `snapshot.total` lowered by the same cents with the 0 floor `total_price` has when it has a total. The writer never creates a breakdown or a total a snapshot lacks: the admin views draw any breakdown that exists, and one holding only the Refund row would read as the whole booking. So a snapshot with no breakdown (the `{}` column default, or an older stored shape) gains the line in `snapshot.adjustments` only, and the next re-price rebuilds the rest with the line netted. Neither total is re-derived from the other, so a booking whose snapshot already drifted from `total_price` keeps that drift. Only a contract portion above zero writes a line: overpayment-scope and extra-scope refunds (Additional Services, extensions, extras, off-ledger labels) leave `total_price` alone and write none, and a refund that walks both kinds of invoice writes the contract part only, the cents `total_price` is lowered by before its 0 floor. One refund, one line: the call sits after the already-applied check, so no real path reaches the writer twice, and a line that already names the refund (by `refund_id`, any source, a planted one included) stops the write and is reported, never silently. Every refund path shares it: the payment panel and Cancel event through `refundExecute`, the stale-pending sweep, and the `refund.created` webhook (cancel-line's own refunds are overpayment scope, so they write none). A legal-hold booking (`isLegalHold` on the id of the row the writer loaded) gets no line while its money reconciles unchanged, and a hold check that throws (the list moved or broke) counts as held. The writer never throws on the row's data or on that check, because a throw would roll back the money reconciliation of a refund Stripe already made and every retry would fail the same way: it skips, and every skip but the two expected ones (no contract amount, a held booking) goes to Sentry, a stored shape it cannot read left alone; database errors still roll the transaction back. Cancel-line refuses to remove the line (`PROTECTED_LINE`). Until the override is retired the money UPDATE also lowers `total_price_override` by the same cents, so an override booking gets both. Refunds reconciled before the line existed have none; the conversion backfills them (spec 8.4).
- **Cancellation refunds** (P6/fix #7): `POST /api/proposals/:id/cancel/refund` loops `refundExecute` per refundable charge largest-first (a booked event has deposit + balance charges, which `planRefund`'s single-charge model can't span), until the `cancellationMath` target is met. Each refund row is stamped with its `gratuity_cents` attribution. Refunded money is not income — the standard reconciliation nets it out of collected/income; the original contract total is preserved in the cancellation audit note. The money-out target is capped at a lifetime ceiling (the cancel-time `refund_owed_cents` activity-log snapshot plus post-cancel succeeded-payment headroom) so a retry after a mid-loop failure cannot over-refund via the retainer feedback loop. The retainer itself (`assembleContext` in `cancel.js`) is the paid amount on a `Deposit` invoice, plus, for a Deposit that `upgradeDepositInvoiceToFull` or the backfill relabelled `Full Payment`, the deposit amount recorded in that row's `invoice_upgraded_to_full` / `invoice_backfilled_to_full` breadcrumb capped at the row's `amount_paid`; a native Full Payment invoice carries no retainer. Decided 2026-08-28: a deposit-terms client who pays in full still forfeits the retainer (agreement 2.1 defines it by the amount the proposal stated).
- **Cancel line item** (2026-07-24): `POST /api/proposals/:id/cancel-line` removes one client-visible priced line and settles the money in a single act. `server/utils/lineItemCancel.js` holds the per-kind target registry (add-on / bar / syrup / extra-bartender / adjustment / gratuity; the package line hands off to the cancel-event flow), mutates, then reprices through `foldExtrasIntoProposal` so a negotiated `total_price_override` moves by the catalog delta. The removal COMMITS first; refunds fire post-commit, sequentially per charge, with `total_scope='overpayment'` so reconciliation never re-lowers the already-folded total. `POST .../cancel-line/preview` runs the identical core inside a rolled-back transaction, so the confirm button restates exactly what execute will do, guarded by an `updated_at`/`amount_paid` fingerprint (409 `STALE_PREVIEW`). Money paid outside Stripe surfaces as `manual_return_cents` (returned by hand, never auto-refunded). Two notice modules ride the flow: `server/utils/gratuityStaffNotice.js` sends the MANDATORY staff "you can set out a tip jar" email whenever a gratuity is removed or lowered under the $50 no-jar floor (approved non-dropped roster, email only), and `server/utils/lineItemRemovedNotify.js` sends the notify-gated client notice when a removal owed no refund. That notice renders only `applyLineItemCancel`'s `clientNotice`, decided from the locked row: `{ kind: 'removed', label }` for a shown line or any other kind ("We removed <label> from your event at your request."; a shown line stored with a label of only spaces takes the engine's type name, "Discount" / "Surcharge"), and `{ kind: 'package_adjusted', amountCents }` for a hidden adjustment ("We adjusted your package price by $X at your request.", wording approved 2026-10-17), where X is the change in the booking's total between the locked row and the fresh snapshot in the same transaction, so the engine's zero clamp is honored and the hidden line's label and own amount never reach the client. `removedLabel` stays on the admin preview, toast and audit row. A call without a well-formed descriptor sends nothing, so the admin label cannot reach a client by accident.
- **Service extensions (on-site added bar time, 2026-08-03)**: a staff request mints a `Service Extension` invoice (off-ledger label, see `OFF_LEDGER_INVOICE_LABELS` under Database Schema > Invoices) and the client pays it through the normal InvoicePage rails, gated on a server-recorded terms acceptance in `create-intent-for-invoice`. The webhook discriminates an extension payment by the `service_extensions.invoice_id` lookup, skips the `amount_paid` roll-up, and runs the settle tail post-commit (`settleExtension` moves `event_duration_hours` and the shift end time, payroll hours re-seed, staff get the greenlight, `finalizeExtension` stamps completion). Extension payments are excluded from the refund planner's candidate list, so the admin refund cannot land on them; refunding one is a manual Stripe-dashboard act by design (procedure: `docs/ops-runbook.md` > "Service Extension refunds"), with the `refund.created` webhook adopting the dashboard refund without touching contract money (the off-ledger label zeroes both the contract portion and the `amount_paid` drop, whichever scope it lands in). The 60-second expiry sweep (`RUN_SERVICE_EXTENSION_SWEEP_SCHEDULER`, `serviceExtensionSweep.js`) enforces the hard stop and heals crash-stranded settles. Full route table under API Routes > Service Extensions; schema under Database Schema > Service Extensions.
- **Stale-pending refund recovery**: a `proposal_refunds` row is written `pending` before `stripe.refunds.create`; ambiguous Stripe errors (connection/API) now leave it `pending` rather than `failed` (a false `failed` re-opens refund headroom and enables a double-refund). `refundSweepScheduler.js` (`RUN_REFUND_PENDING_SWEEP_SCHEDULER`, every 15 min) reconciles rows stuck `pending` >30 min against `stripe.refunds.list` — adopting the real refund via `applyRefundReconciliation` (matched by the `proposal_refund_row_id` metadata anchor, else a unique amount) or marking it `failed` only when Stripe confirms no refund exists.
- **Refund-to-zero reaping** (M-1): a full refund that zeroes `amount_paid` demotes the proposal to `accepted`, which is still active everywhere. The admin refund UI therefore prompts to archive; `POST /:id/archive` now reaps like the cancel flow (via the shared `shiftReap.js`): soft-cancels shifts, denies open shift_requests, deletes pending proposal-level scheduled_messages, and voids unpaid invoices, so a refunded booking no longer lingers on the staff open-shifts feed or the dunning ladder. Partial refunds never auto-kill. The public invoice and drink-plan intent routes reject payment on an archived proposal (409 `EVENT_CANCELLED`), and the settlement webhooks alert on any payment that still lands on an archived proposal.
- **Payout mirror (read-side)**: `payout.paid`/`payout.failed` webhook branches + a daily sweep (`RUN_STRIPE_PAYOUT_SWEEP_SCHEDULER`) and a one-off backfill (`server/scripts/backfillStripePayouts.js`) all converge on `server/utils/stripePayoutSync.js`, which mirrors payouts + balance-transaction lines into `stripe_payouts` / `stripe_payout_lines` and reconciles each line back to payments/tips/refunds/disputes. Surfaced as the Financials → Stripe Payouts tab. Ingest is livemode-only; a failed payout fires a one-time admin email (`stripe_payout_failed` category). No existing table is written.
- **Important**: Stripe webhook route (`/api/stripe/webhook`) must receive raw body — registered before `express.json()` in `server/index.js`

### Resend (Email)
- **Wrapper**: `server/utils/email.js` (single send + batch)
- **From**: `Dr. Bartender <no-reply@drbartender.com>`
- **Used for**: Transactional notifications (proposals, OTPs, shifts, application status, balance reminders, shopping list) + email marketing (blasts, drip sequences)
- **Marketing**: `server/routes/emailMarketing/` — leads, campaigns, sequences, conversations, analytics, behind a composition router (`index.js`). Split from a single 987-line file in lane mkt-e; paths, middleware, and mount order are unchanged. Mount order in `index.js` IS behavior: `designer.js` and `sequences.js` register paths more specific than `campaigns.js`'s `/campaigns/:id`, and Express matches in registration order. Verify any change with `node scripts/route-inventory.js server/routes/emailMarketing` before and after, and diff.
- **Webhooks**: `server/routes/emailMarketingWebhook.js` — receives tracking events (sent/delivered/opened/clicked/bounced/complained), verified via `svix`
- **Scheduler**: `server/utils/emailSequenceScheduler.js` — processes drip sequence steps every 15 minutes
- **Templates**: `server/utils/emailTemplates.js` — `wrapEmail()` for transactional, `wrapMarketingEmail()` for marketing (includes unsubscribe link). Application status progression sends `applicationInterviewInvite`, `applicationHired`, `applicationRejected`, `applicationDeactivated` (admin's optional personal note is `esc()`-d into a styled block; user-supplied names are also `esc()`-d in HTML bodies). `applicationReceivedConfirmation` is sent only at submission time, not on admin status reverts.
- **Greetings**: `server/utils/firstName.js` — `firstNameOf()` is the single source for the name in every "Hi ..." line across all 10 template files (client- and staff-facing). Title-aware (shares the `TITLES` set with `staffDisplayName.js`, so "Dr. Monica Donnely" greets as "Monica") and idempotent, so it is safe at a site whose input may already be a first name. Apply it AT the greeting, never at the `const name =` above it: several of those variables also feed admin-facing copy that must keep the full name. **Couple-aware**: a name joined by `&`, `+`, or `and` with whitespace on both sides is split, narrowed per side, and rejoined with the joiner the data used, so "Aubrey Smith & Dominic Jones" greets as "Aubrey & Dominic" instead of dropping the partner. Nine client rows are stored this way and nearly all are weddings; requiring whitespace on both sides of the joiner is what keeps "Alexander" and "Andrea" intact.

### Twilio (SMS)
- **Wrapper**: `server/utils/sms.js` (includes `normalizePhone()` for E.164 formatting): `sendSMS({ to, body, meta, from, statusCallback })` (a line key `from`; Twilio 21610 writes `sms_optouts`), `smsStatusCallbackUrl()`, `twilioErrorText(err)` (the stored failure text, `Twilio <code> (failed)`), `sendAndLogSms`
- **Shared helpers**: `server/utils/xmlEscape.js` (TwiML `& < >` escaper, shared by the SMS and voice TwiML routes) and `server/utils/usPhone.js` (`toUsE164`/`isUsE164`: `normalizePhone` + strict `^\+1[2-9]\d{9}$` NANP gate, rejecting international numbers and 900/976 premium codes — the VA-calling toll-fraud control).
- **Telegram side-channel** (VA calling): `server/utils/telegram.js` is a raw-`fetch` Bot API wrapper (no new dep, Node 18 global `fetch`): `sendTelegramMessage` / `sendTelegramAudio`/`setTelegramWebhook`/`getTelegramWebhookInfo`, plus `verifyTelegramSecret` (constant-time `secret_token` header check over SHA-256 digests) and `isNewUpdate` (de-dupes Telegram retries via `telegram_update.update_id` `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`). Sends gate on `notificationsEnabled()` exactly like `sendSMS`.
- **Used for**: Admin-initiated SMS to staff (general messages, shift invitations), shift approval notifications
- **Consent (staff)**: Collected during agreement signing (`agreements.sms_consent`) — only consented staff appear as eligible recipients (`server/routes/messages.js` gates on it). Separate from client consent, which lives in `clients.communication_preferences` + `sms_consent_log`; the two are deliberately never conflated.
- **Event-eve is a PAIR**: `event_eve` (sms) and `event_eve_email` (email) are scheduled together at the same T-24h instant, both registered `multiChannel: true` so the substitution step never turns one into a duplicate of the other. Whichever channel is unhealthy suppresses and its twin delivers. The email half exists because `sms_enabled: false` now also means "declined the quote-wizard consent box", the expected default for a new client; before the twin, that silently dropped the day-before message carrying the bartender's name, phone, and arrival window.
- **Logging**: All inbound + outbound messages logged to `sms_messages` table with delivery status tracking
- **Lines (spec 2026-10-06)**: `server/utils/smsLines.js` maps the three DRB lines. The 888 is `TWILIO_PHONE_NUMBER`; the 1922 (`+12242221922`) and 0082 (`+12242220082`) are fixed constants, deliberately not `CONSULT_CALLER_ID`/`VOICE_CALLER_ID`, which fall back to each other. `sendSMS({ from })` takes a line key and refuses an unknown one; omitted, it sends from the 888 exactly as before. A 224 send always names its line, never the Messaging Service pool. Human replies default to the line of the person's latest human-involved text (`lastHumanLineFromRows`, one rule for the Messages reply and Inbox; a relay text counts as their 888 text), else the sender's own line (users 1 and 12 the 1922, user 2 the 0082, anyone else the 888), limited to `INBOX_TEXT_LINES`; staff and Thumbtack proxy numbers get the 888 only.
- **One opt-out for every line**: Twilio keeps opt-outs per sending number (the 888 and the 224 Messaging Service are separate domains), so `sms_optouts` records the phone once: any STOP-set word to any line from any sender, and Twilio 21610 at send or in the status callback. `textability()` (`smsOptOut.js`) is the rule the Messages reply and Inbox ask: an active row, `sms_enabled=false`, or for staff no `agreements.sms_consent`, is opted out; `phone_status='bad'` cannot be texted. The admin group send (`POST /api/messages/send`) is a human send that does not consult it: it checks agreement consent and a usable phone only. Automated sends do not consult it yet (fix list).
- **Delivery status**: Messages-page replies (and Inbox sends) register `POST /api/sms/status`, which flips the row to `failed` on `failed`/`undelivered` and stores nothing else. A failure that beats its row is kept in `sms_status_orphans`: the Messages reply folds it into its own row, and the Inbox reader left-joins it.
- **Shift commands (decision 17)**: CONFIRM/CANT and the automated staff replies run only on the 888, and only when DRB's latest outbound text to that staffer was automated (`latestDrbTextWasAutomated` in `smsShiftCommands.js`: matched by `recipient_id` or the last-10 phone, `sender_id` NULL is automated, failed rows skipped, no text at all is not automated). Only a text DRB sent them as staff counts: admin alerts (`admin_*`), dead-letter alerts (`critical_path_dead_letter_alert`) and automated texts on a client's thread (`client_id` set) also have no sender and are skipped, so an alert landing after a human text can never reopen shift commands. The shift approval and auto-assign notices go out through `sendSMS` directly and write no `sms_messages` row, so this check cannot see them. A CONFIRM or CANT turned into conversation is emailed (routine, no SMS) with a subject saying no shift was changed.

### Zul VA Calling (Telegram trigger + Twilio Voice bridge)

Lets our Philippines-based VA (Zul) place and receive US calls that work over a poor mobile-data connection. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-01-zul-va-calling-design.md`. Operator runbook: `docs/va-calling-runbook.md`.

- **Core constraint**: real-time voice never rides Zul's internet. It rides her **cellular** network via a Twilio callback bridge — Twilio calls her cell, she answers, Twilio bridges the second leg to the target. Only a tiny few-KB Telegram trigger message uses data.
- **Outbound flow**: Zul texts a target number to the bot → `POST /api/telegram/<secret>` authenticates (secret path + `secret_token` header + `user_id` allowlist), validates the target US-only (`toUsE164`: `normalizePhone` then `^\+1[2-9]\d{9}$`, 900/976 rejected — the primary toll-fraud control), upserts a short-TTL `pending_call` and replies "Reply YES to call …". On `YES` a conditional `UPDATE … WHERE status='awaiting_confirm' RETURNING` claims the row (claim-then-call: `calls.create` is an external HTTP call, never in a DB txn), then `placeBridgedCall({ to: VA_CELL, callerId: VOICE_CALLER_ID, url: /api/voice/bridge, statusCallback: /api/voice/status, timeLimit })`. When Zul answers, `/bridge` looks up the target by `CallSid` (never a request param) and dials it with `answerOnBridge="true"` showing the 224.
- **Inbound flow (two lines since Phase 1a, spec 2026-07-26)**: the phone system is TWO-LINE. The 0082 is Zul's: `POST /api/voice/inbound` forwards to `VA_CELL` and stamps `?line=zul` on its `<Dial action>`. The 1922 is the primary business number: `POST /api/voice/inbound/primary` dials `VM_PRIMARY_DIAL_TARGET` (Dallas's 312 GV, env-only strict E.164) with a deliberately short `VM_PRIMARY_RING_SEC` so a re-enabled GV voicemail can never intercept, stamping `?line=primary`. One SHARED missed handler serves both, keyed on the HMAC-covered `line`: it picks the greeting, the escalation target, and the delivery channel (zul → Telegram audio; primary → SMS to `VM_TEXT_DESTINATION` carrying a listen link, recording retained until 1b). Carrier voicemail is OFF on `VA_CELL` (owner-confirmed 2026-07-22), which is what makes the miss detectable: the dial rings out, ends `no-answer`, and Twilio requests `POST /api/voice/inbound/missed`. VERIFIED 2026-07-24 by a live test call: a CALLER mid-ring hang-up (well before the 20s timeout) DOES cause Twilio to request the action URL with a `MISSED_STATUSES` value, so it inserts a `voicemail_delivery` row and pings Zul, the same as a full ring-out. Twilio's docs never documented this; the live call settled it. `VOICEMAIL_ENABLED` was flipped on in Render on 2026-07-24 and the full flow (ping → recorded → delivered → Twilio recording deleted) was confirmed against prod. An instant answer on the primary line is logged by the interception canary — LOG-ONLY since 2026-08-14, no Sentry page (the detector reads connected time, not time-to-answer, so it fired on fast humans and stayed silent for real interceptions; check the `[voice/missed] ... dialSec` console line, not Sentry).
- **Press-1 escalation (spec 2026-07-26 section 3, ships dark behind `VM_ESCALATION_ENABLED`)**: the missed DAY greeting is wrapped in a `<Gather>` whenever a digit could arrive — escalation on, or the greeting already saying "press one" (`dayGreetingOffersPress1`, 2026-08-20). A caller who presses 1 reaches `/api/voice/escalate`, which tries the OTHER person before voicemail, or goes straight to the `escalate_failed` recording when the switch is off. Never inviting a digit nobody is listening for is the invariant; the switch's job is the DIAL, and it still stops every one of them. The billed leg (international when the target is `VA_CELL`) is guarded four ways: the kill switch, a single-use claim (`escalated_at IS NULL` on the row's PK), a HARD rolling daily cap (`claimEscalationUnderCap` serializes cap-check+claim behind `pg_advisory_xact_lock(74209,0)` with a 3s lock_timeout, pinned to READ COMMITTED), and a per-target quiet window. The dialed leg gets a `<Number url>` whisper requiring a KEYPRESS to bridge, so an unattended carrier voicemail is screened out; `escalate/done` branches on the recorded acceptance state, never on `DialCallStatus`. No keypress at the greeting still falls through to `<Record>` with no extra route.
- **Caller communication (spec 2026-08-19)**: the flow narrates itself, because escalation shipped with two silent gaps. Pressing 1 now plays an acknowledgment (`escalate_ack`) BEFORE the `<Dial>`; previously the route answered the `<Gather>` with a bare `<Dial>`, so the caller tapped a key and got dead air for up to 20 seconds and often pressed again into a document that had already left the `<Gather>`. Every return to voicemail plays `escalate_failed` before the beep, which covers seven of the eight branches of `voiceEscalate.js` with one edit (the rate-limiter handler carries the same message inline, because no `line` binding exists that early); previously that was a bare `<Record playBeep>`, and an unexplained tone after 20 seconds of ringing reads as a dropped call rather than an invitation. Inside `VM_NIGHT_WINDOW` (default `21:00-09:00` `America/Chicago`, the literal `none` disables night entirely, evaluated by `isNight()` in `voicemailLine.js`) the missed document drops the `<Gather>` entirely and the greeting promises the morning. That is deliberately NOT a business-hours rule: Zul keeps Chicago-aligned hours, so Chicago night is her off time too and a night press-1 rings a sleeping phone in the Philippines for a predictable failure. No copy claims we are open or closed, so nothing here can contradict the published client-facing hours. `isNight` shares the window machinery with the quiet hours (`parseWindow`, `minutesInZone`, and one `inWindow` half-open comparison, so a wrap-around fix lands in one place) and fails OPEN to day, so a config typo produces a rung phone rather than a caller silently denied a human. Four message slots per line (`greeting_day`, `greeting_night`, `escalate_ack`, `escalate_failed`) resolve through `messageVerb()` on the `VM_GREETING_URL` contract: unset takes the default, `say` forces synthetic (the kill switch for a bad recording), a URL is `<Play>`ed. Both lines default to bundled recordings served by `voiceAssets.js`: Dallas's four and Zul's four, all recorded 2026-08-19. Her 2026-07-24 mp3 is still served at its original `/api/voice/greeting.mp3` path for anything referencing that URL directly, but it is no longer any slot's default. The press-1 offer now lives INSIDE the day greeting, so `needsAppendedOffer()` trusts only the KNOWN defaults, and since 2026-08-19 both lines' defaults say the offer themselves, so nothing is appended by default. An override URL has UNKNOWN content, so it gets the offer appended on EITHER line. That direction is deliberate: a doubled offer is an obvious stutter, while a missing one loses the feature invisibly behind a silent four-second `<Gather>`. Set `VM_ESCALATION_PROMPT=none` to suppress the append after wiring a recording that already says it. The old global `VM_ESCALATION_PROMPT` could not be set correctly once the lines diverged: off hid the option from her callers, on made his line say it twice.
- **Public phone constants (client, mirrored server-side in `server/utils/companyPhone.js` for client emails and SMS; change both together)**: voice and text are two DIFFERENT numbers since Phase 1a: `COMPANY_PHONE`/`COMPANY_PHONE_TEL` = the 1922 for calls, `COMPANY_TEXT_PHONE`/`COMPANY_TEXT_PHONE_TEL` = the 888 for texts (the 224 numbers have no approved A2P SMS campaign until Phase 2, when the pairs reunite on the 1922). Do not collapse them: repointing texting at the 1922 sends client texts to a number that silently does not deliver.
- **Voicemail (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-22-voicemail-224-telegram-design.md`)**: `/inbound/missed` checks `VM_DAILY_CAP` FIRST (a rejected call must not write a row that extends the window), claims a `voicemail_delivery` row (the ping dedup, stamping `line`), returns the per-line greeting + `<Record>`, and only THEN pings Zul on Telegram — zul-line misses only; a primary miss shows natively on the GV dial target, so it pings nobody (response first, so a hung Bot API can never leave a live caller in dead air). The ping is two messages when the caller is NANP: prose, then the bare number alone, because the bot's own trigger runs `toUsE164` over the whole message text. `<Record>` carries no `action` attribute on purpose (Twilio skips it when the caller hangs up, which is the normal case); delivery hangs off `recordingStatusCallback` → `POST /api/voice/inbound/voicemail`, which claims delivery, fetches the mp3 from a URL CONSTRUCTED from the account SID + a `^RE[0-9a-f]{32}$`-validated `RecordingSid` (the body's `RecordingUrl` is never read), uploads it to the same chat, and deletes the Twilio recording **only** on an affirmative `ok === true`. A gated `skipped` send and a `failed` send both keep the recording. Both voicemail webhooks fail CLOSED on signature in every environment. Master switch `VOICEMAIL_ENABLED`, default off. **Listen link (spec 2026-08-10):** every row carries a `listen_token` UUID, and the primary line's alert SMS ends with `<API_URL>/api/voice/vm/<token>`, which streams the retained recording so the message can be heard from the text instead of the Twilio console. The token is the only auth, acceptable ONLY because the alert goes to `VM_TEXT_DESTINATION` (the operator, who can already hear every voicemail in the console) — explicitly NOT the proposal/invoice precedent, since the audio belongs to a third party. Two-sided kill switch `VM_LISTEN_LINK_ENABLED` (default on) closes the route AND drops the line from the text so no dead URL is ever sent; revoke a single link with `UPDATE voicemail_delivery SET listen_token = gen_random_uuid() WHERE call_sid = $1`. Links expire at 30 days, enforced in the route's own SQL rather than borrowed from the prune's status allowlist.
- **Status feedback**: `POST /api/voice/status`; a failed/unanswered leg messages Zul via Telegram so she always learns the outcome.
- **Toll-fraud guards** (this webhook dials billed international calls on an auto-refill account from external input): (1) Telegram `secret_token` header + unguessable secret URL path, (2) numeric `user_id` allowlist layered on top, (3) US-only NANP validation in code, (4) confirm-before-dial with a TTL'd pending record (a new target replaces it), (5) claim-then-call idempotency + `telegram_update` `update_id` dedupe, (6) DB-backed spend caps (5 triggers/min, 40 calls/day counted from `call_audit`) + a 1800s per-call `timeLimit` on both legs, (7) shared `xmlEscape` on every TwiML interpolation, (8) `notificationsEnabled()` gate so a dev server never dials the live account, (9) a daily webhook heartbeat (`getWebhookInfo` → re-`setWebhook` + admin email on failure), (10) last-4 log redaction and a retention purge of the PII-bearing `call_audit`, (11) `VM_DAILY_CAP` on the voicemail path (the inbound analog of `VA_CALL_DAILY_CAP`: a missed call used to cost nothing after ring timeout and now costs greeting + up to `VM_MAX_LENGTH_SEC` of billed recording) plus a per-`CallSid` limiter and fail-closed signature checks on both voicemail webhooks, (12) the press-1 escalation's own stack: `VM_ESCALATION_ENABLED` kill switch (default off), the single-use `escalated_at` claim, the HARD `VM_ESCALATION_DAILY_CAP` (cap-check+claim serialized on an advisory lock so concurrency cannot overshoot it), the quiet windows, and the keypress whisper screen; every escalation dial target is env-only strict E.164, (13) signature-BEFORE-limiter ordering on both forward routes so an unsigned flood can never drain the constant-key buckets and busy-out real callers.
- **Helper modules**: `server/utils/telegram.js` (Bot API, raw fetch, no SDK), `server/utils/pendingCall.js` (claim-then-call DB helpers + audit + prune), `server/utils/usPhone.js` (`toUsE164`/`isUsE164`), `server/utils/xmlEscape.js` (shared TwiML escape), `server/utils/vaCallingScheduler.js` (prune + line-aware undelivered-voicemail redelivery sweep + webhook heartbeat), `server/utils/voicemail.js` (voicemail ledger, per-line delivery, line-aware `alertOperator`, constructed-URL Twilio media fetch/delete), `server/utils/voicemailLine.js` (per-line routing policy: `resolveLine`, `E164_RE`, escalation targets, quiet windows, `isNight` for the caller-facing night window, ring clamp, interception canary), `server/utils/voicemailTwiml.js` (shared TwiML fragments so the two emitting routers cannot drift; owns the four message slots and `messageVerb`), `server/utils/voicemailEscalation.js` (escalation claim/cap/acceptance/outcome ledger), `server/utils/sms.js#placeBridgedCall` (Twilio `calls.create`, gated identically to `sendSMS`).
- **Tables**: `pending_call` (confirm-before-dial state, one row per user), `call_audit` (spend/abuse audit + daily-cap source of truth), `telegram_update` (`update_id` retry dedupe), `voicemail_delivery` (one row per missed inbound call: ping dedup claim + `VM_DAILY_CAP` window + delivery ledger). Pruned by the `RUN_VA_CALLING_SCHEDULER` job, which also runs the undelivered-voicemail redelivery sweep.
- **Bootstrap**: deploy with `TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_USER_ID` unset; the webhook echoes each sender's id and dials nothing until the id is set + redeployed.
- **Account setup (owner, console-only)**: Twilio PH voice geo (low-risk enabled, high-risk off), auto-refill on with headroom, and a low-balance + monthly-spend billing alert (a dry balance takes SMS down with it).

### Web Push (Staff notifications and the admin phone)
- **Library**: `web-push` (VAPID). Server keys `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` / `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` / `VAPID_CONTACT_EMAIL`; the public key is also exposed to the client as `REACT_APP_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` (staff portal only; the admin phone reads the key from `GET /api/me/notification-preferences`, so admin push needs nothing in Vercel).
- **Sender**: `server/utils/pushSender.js` — `sendPush()` configures VAPID once at module load inside a try/catch, so a missing or malformed key fails closed (`vapid_unset`) and never crashes boot. Maps `410`/`404` → `{ gone: true }` so the dispatcher prunes dead subscriptions; all other errors are transient. Gated by `SEND_NOTIFICATIONS` like email and SMS (`notificationsEnabled()`, checked per call): gated off, it logs the push service's host and returns `{ ok: false, error: 'notifications_off' }`. Each send carries a 10 s timeout; a stall counts as failed and the subscription is kept. Callers: `adminPush.sendPushToUser`, which `pushDispatch.dispatchPushRow` (the dispatcher's push-channel branch), the admin channel and the test send all ride, and `serviceExtensionNotify.js`, which sends to staff directly. `dispatchPushRow` records a failed subscription read as `failed` with `push_read_failed: <msg>`, never as suppressed.
- **Subscriptions**: stored per-user in `users.staff_notification_preferences.push_subscriptions[]` (JSONB array, 10-cap, evict-oldest), written via `POST` / `DELETE /api/me/push-subscriptions`. Each entry carries a `surface`: `'admin'` from the admin phone (the POST stores it only for exactly `'admin'`), absent = staff. Each channel sends to its own (`sendPushToUser({ surface })`: the admin channel and the test send pass `'admin'`, the staff dispatcher `'staff'`), so one user subscribed on both never gets an admin banner on the staff device; the prune and a passkey revoke still work on the whole array.
- **Client**: service worker `client/public/staff-sw.js` (`push` → `showNotification`, `notificationclick` → focus/open), registered by `client/src/utils/pushSubscribe.js` (`permissionState` / `subscribePush` / `unsubscribePush`). iOS only delivers push to a home-screen-installed PWA, so a staff-scoped manifest (`client/public/staff-manifest.json`) + Apple standalone meta tags are injected on the `staff.` host by `client/src/utils/installStaffPwaMeta.js`. The Notifications settings drive the flow (`PushPermissionBanner` permission states + `IOSCoachmark` install walkthrough).
- **Admin channel (lane ma-c-push, spec 2026-08-13-mobile-admin section 7)**: `server/utils/adminPush.js` holds the seven kinds (`booking_signed`, `booking_signed_paid`, `payment_landed`, `booking_last_minute`, `drink_plan_submitted`, `staffing_drop`, `staffing_cover_swap`), which are the dispatch allowlist (a `push` naming anything else is dropped and logged), and `pushCopy`, the banner builders: client display name, event kind, date and amount only, because the banner shows on a locked screen; the client name is clamped to 60 characters and the event kind to 40, since both can be typed on the public quote form. `notifyAdminCategory({ ..., push })` (`server/utils/adminNotifications.js`) pushes behind three gates: the category subscription (the recipient list itself), the per-kind switch in `users.admin_push_preferences` (absent = on), and at least one stored subscription. The push leg starts BEFORE the email and SMS loop and is fire-and-forget: never awaited by the caller, never thrown into it, reported only as `pushQueued`. `pushAdminKind` is the push-only door (the drink-plan submit, whose email keeps its own throttle). The pushes come from the client signature, the Stripe payment notices and a bank payment starting, the last-minute booking alert, the drink-plan submit, and the staff drop, cover and swap paths (web and the SMS CANT); the website quote submit does not push. A passkey revoke empties the user's stored subscriptions in the revoke's own transaction (a global logout; the remaining phones re-enable with one tap). `SEND_NOTIFICATIONS` gates every admin push like any other send.
- **Admin channel, the phone**: More > Notifications (`client/src/pages/mobile/NotificationsPhone.js`, `/more/notifications`, copy and state rules in the pure `client/src/utils/notificationsView.js`) shows this device's real state, Android's permission and then the admin worker's subscription (`client/src/utils/adminPush.js`: `readDevicePushState`, `enableAdminPush`, `disableAdminPush`, every entry a no-op off the admin host), never a stored flag. It subscribes through `pushSubscribe.js` against `/admin-sw.js` with the key the API served, into the same `POST`/`DELETE /api/me/push-subscriptions`. Every session purge (logout, a revoked token on `/auth/me`, a stale hydration, a failed refresh) goes through AuthContext's `purgePhoneSession`, which unsubscribes this device locally only: the token is gone before a server DELETE could go out, so the endpoint dies at the push service and the server prunes it on the next send's 410. The admin worker's click handler focuses a window already on the banner's path, else focuses an open admin window and navigates it there, and opens a new window only when none is open; it navigates and opens same-origin paths only.
