- **Channel routing**: push is one of three channels (push / sms / email) selectable per notification category; `notificationChannelResolver.js` keeps an email fallback for critical categories so a push-only preference never silently drops a critical message.
- **Public opt-in form**: `POST /api/sms/opt-in` (`server/routes/smsOptIn.js`, no auth, `publicLimiter`) is the submit for the standalone `/sms` page, built after two Twilio A2P 10DLC rejections (30909, then 30896) whose cause was that the quote wizard's consent checkbox sits on step 2 of `/quote` where a carrier reviewer never sees it. **The checkbox is OPTIONAL, and the number is required only when it is ticked** — a third rejection ("Forced Consent Violation", 2026-08-03) landed because requiring both meant a consumer could not decline texts and still use the form. An un-ticked box is therefore a valid submit, and it takes a **completely separate path that never touches `clients`**: it upserts `email_leads` (the real marketing list) and returns. Any number supplied is ignored and stored nowhere. It never stamps `sms_opt_out_at` either, because not ticking a box is not a STOP and that field blocks every later opt-in as `prior_opt_out`; the no-consent path never calls `recordSmsConsent` at all. **Both halves of that design are load-bearing and were paid for.** The first version created a `clients` row with `sms_enabled` forced false, and the review fleet found two defects in it: a client row here is a row the submit "owns", so a LATER ticked submit from the same address resolved as `existing_client`, recorded nothing and still showed a success screen (declining once meant never being able to opt in through this form again); and because that branch skipped `recordSmsConsent` it bypassed the phone-scoped STOP guard, so posting a stranger's stopped number under a different name committed a second row carrying it, which `smsInbound`'s newest-row-wins `lookupSender` would then resolve STOP/START against instead of their real row. Writing to `email_leads` removes both by construction: no client row to own, no column to hold a number. `status` is deliberately excluded from the upsert's `DO UPDATE` list so a public form cannot resurrect an unsubscribed lead. Consent semantics on the ticked path are entirely `utils/smsConsent.js` — the ownership gate (`subjectIsNew`), the row- and phone-scoped STOP guards, and the `sms_consent_log` audit row (`source_form='sms_page'`). Where the wizard has to keep a client row whose consent did not record and force `sms_enabled=false`, this route's **ticked** path rolls the whole submit back instead, because nothing depends on the row. Every consent outcome returns the same generic 200 so the form cannot be used to probe which emails are clients or which numbers have opted out.
- **Inbound webhook**: `POST /api/sms/inbound` (`server/routes/sms.js`) receives Twilio inbound messages, signature-verified via `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN` (`twilio.validateRequest` through the shared `server/utils/twilioSignature.js` check, no JWT), and passes `To` and the picture media through. `server/utils/smsInbound.js` orchestrates each message: looks up the sender (client or staff); records the row with `metadata.to`, `metadata.media` and, when it settles, `metadata.outcome`; handles STOP/START (the preference for a known sender, `sms_optouts` for every sender); answers HELP; runs staff CONFIRM/CANT through `smsShiftCommands.js` only on the 888 after an automated text (decision 17); and routes client texts to the admin Messages thread UI.

### Admin PWA (Mobile Admin, spec 2026-08-13)
- **Surface**: the admin host is installable (spec section 7). `client/public/admin-manifest.json` ("DrB OS", `start_url /events`, standalone) + placeholder icon set (`client/public/admin-icon-*.png`, regenerated by `scripts/make-admin-icon.js`; the design fit-back lane replaces the art, filenames are stable). Metas injected at runtime by `client/src/utils/installAdminPwaMeta.js` behind the admin host gate (`admin.*` / bare localhost, mirroring `getSiteContext`); it also mutates the existing `theme-color` meta in place and amends the viewport with `viewport-fit=cover`. The staff injector/manifest/SW are untouched siblings.
- **Service worker**: `client/public/admin-sw.js` (registered by `client/src/utils/adminSw.js` from AdminLayout). Push display + notification-click deep links (staff-sw pattern; since v11 a tap navigates an open admin window rather than opening a second one, see Web Push above). Offline: at install the SW fetches `/asset-manifest.json` and precaches ONLY its `entrypoints` (Chrome's memory cache sits above the SW, so the entry bundle can bypass the fetch handler on a controlled reload; lazy chunks runtime-cache organically on cold-session navigations, and a full-manifest sweep was rejected in review as 22.6MB per SW update); network-first navigations with cached `index.html` fallback (only an `ok` document may become the shell); cache-first ONLY for content-hashed `/static/` assets (the dev server's stable bundle names are deliberately excluded so a dev box can never pin a frozen bundle); allowlisted phone-surface GET `/api/` reads cached per-user (`admin-api-<ver>-u<id>`; the worker version is `admin-sw-2026-10-14-v11`), served ONLY on transport failure or a 4s timeout race, stamped `x-sw-cached-at` (surfaced as `res.staleAt` by `utils/api.js` `markStaleFromHeaders`, rendered via `utils/staleTime.js`). The allowlist is exact paths (`/api/shifts`, `/api/proposals`, `/api/admin/badge-counts`, `/api/admin/search`, `/api/admin/active-staff`, `/api/me/notification-preferences`, `/api/auth/me`) plus anchored numeric-id patterns (`/api/proposals/:id`, `/api/shifts/by-proposal/:id`, `/api/shifts/detail/:id`, `/api/invoices/proposal/:id`), and `/api/drink-plans/by-proposal/:id` ONLY with the exact query `?fields=day_of_contact`. Storing and stale-serving are OPT-IN per request: the worker handles a read only when the request carries `X-Offline-Ok: 1` (sent by `client/src/utils/offlineRead.js` `offlineGet`) or is the identity read `/api/auth/me`; every other request on an allowlisted path goes to the network untouched and is never stored, so desktop screens are never shown a stored copy. Pinned by `client/src/utils/adminSwAllowlist.test.js`, which runs the real worker file. Two phone-chrome reads do NOT opt in and so are never stored: the tab badge counts (`AdminLayout`) and the command palette search. On a cold offline launch the badges are empty. `offlineGet`'s importers are bounded by the class-contract test: `client/src/utils/mobileClassContract.test.js` ("only phone screens import offlineRead") fails if a file outside `pages/mobile/` or `components/mobile/` imports it. Today they are `pages/mobile/EventDetailPhone.js`, `pages/mobile/EventsListPhone.js`, `pages/mobile/ProposalsListPhone.js`, `pages/mobile/NotificationsPhone.js`, `components/mobile/AssignmentSheet.js` and `components/mobile/useEditSheet.js`. Non-GET is never intercepted; writes never queue. A server-answered non-2xx is never answered from cache. `AuthContext.logout` posts a purge (`purgeMobileAdminState`) clearing all admin caches + the phone's localStorage. Rollback: a self-unregistering stub recipe is documented at the top of the SW file; the kill path is exercised in the lane's browser pass.
- **Install row**: `client/src/utils/installPrompt.js` captures `beforeinstallprompt` at module import; More renders an explicit "Install app" row when available and not already standalone.
- **Passkey lock (spec section 8)**: `MobileLockScreen` has TWO mounts and they cover different failures. A **gate** inside `ProtectedRoute` (`client/src/App.js`) handles the expired-JWT cold launch: no user, but a phone with an enrolled passkey and a token in storage gets the lock instead of a redirect to `/login`, so the URL survives re-entry. An **overlay** inside `AdminLayout` arms the 30-minute background lock and claims phone 401s. Both render null unless armed, where armed means a passkey enrolled on THIS device AND a phone viewport (`phoneUnlockArmed()`), so desktop admin and the staff portal keep today's behavior untouched. `POST /api/auth/webauthn/assert-verify` (see "WebAuthn phone unlock" in the route table) is the ONLY site that mints the 12h device JWT. Revoking a credential (`DELETE /api/auth/webauthn/credentials/:id`) bumps `users.token_version`, which is a deliberate GLOBAL logout: it is the lost-phone kill switch, not a per-device unenroll. The RP ID is pinned to the admin host (`WEBAUTHN_RP_ID`, defaulting to `admin.drbartender.com` in prod) because one bundle serves four hosts, and a bare-domain RP ID would let an admin passkey assert from the public, hiring, and staff origins. Rows live in `webauthn_credentials` + `webauthn_challenges` (Database Schema above); both are `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` in `schema.sql`, which `server/db/index.js` re-executes on every boot, so the DDL is self-applying and there is no separate migration step.
- **Lock-model utils**: `client/src/utils/mobileLock.js` owns the decision model, deliberately kept as pure functions plus a tiny registry so the UI holds no policy: the 30-minute threshold (`LOCK_AFTER_MS`, measuring BACKGROUNDED time, stamped on background and cleared in the foreground, never inactivity), the expired-JWT cold-launch decision (`shouldLock` reading the token's own `exp`), the `adminPasskeyEnrolled` / `adminPasskeyNudgeDismissed` phone-local flags, the one `REVOCATION_CODES` list plus the 401-claim handler registry that `SessionExpiryHandler` consults before logging a phone out, and the key set `purgeMobileAdminState` (`utils/adminSw.js`) wipes on logout. `client/src/utils/webauthnClient.js` is the thin `@simplewebauthn/browser` wrapper: `registerPasskey` / `unlockWithPasskey` / `isPasskeySupported`. Every URL it calls sits under `/auth/`, inside `utils/api.js`'s 401-exclusion prefix, so a failed unlock can never fire the session-expired dispatch; `registerPasskey` treats an already-registered authenticator as success, because logout purges the device flag while the server row and the OS resident passkey both survive.
- **Phone Events list**: `client/src/pages/mobile/EventsListPhone.js` is mounted by a route-level fork inside `client/src/pages/admin/EventsDashboard.js`: same URL and route table, `useMobileView()` renders the phone list at phone width (decided when the page opens: `MobileViewContext` latches `isPhone` per pathname, so a resize never remounts the open page and takes effect on the next route) unless the `events-list` screen is pinned to Desktop view (the pattern ma-f1 repeats for Proposals). It reads the scoped, event-paged admin feed (`GET /shifts?scope=upcoming|past`, route table above) and renders one card per event, with Upcoming / Past, the Needs staff chip, Show more paging, and for a manual shift the phone assignment sheet, `client/src/components/mobile/AssignmentSheet.js`, opened with push history (below), so Android Back closes it and stays on the list. The row-to-card grouping is a separate pure module, `client/src/utils/eventCards.js` (`groupShiftRows`, `eventKeyOf`, `railParts`): no React, no fetch, no clock unless the caller passes one, so the card shape is unit-testable. The assignment sheet's head reuses it (`groupShiftRows`), and the detail's when line and shift heads reuse `railParts`; the detail's header is `headerOf` in `eventDetailView.js`. It folds every shift of an event into one card carrying the client name, the event kind, the date rail, the time range, the venue, the guest count, the slots/filled/open/pending counts (staffing counted BY ROLE through `roleFill` / `rowRoleFill` in `client/src/components/adminos/shifts.js`, the same rule the phone staffing card uses, so the list and the detail give one event the same fraction; a cancelled shift of a live event leaves the sum), the cancelled flag and the tap target, taking the card's date from its FIRST row (which is why the feed orders rows by date inside an event). The list's staleness line comes from `client/src/utils/staleTime.js` (`formatStaleTime`): "as of" on a live load, and "offline copy · as of" plus a dot when the service worker answered the read from cache. This is the first render of the `.m-stale` element the SW's `x-sw-cached-at` chain was built for.
- **Phone Proposals list**: `client/src/pages/mobile/ProposalsListPhone.js`, mounted by the same route-level fork inside `client/src/pages/admin/ProposalsDashboard.js` (`desktopView('proposals-list')`). Reads `GET /proposals?envelope=1` (50 a page; `sort=created_at&dir=desc` for Newest lead, `sort=event_date&dir=asc` for Event date; `unviewed=1` for the chip), renders the ma-e1 card family with the pipeline chip from `utils/proposalStatusMap.js` and the meta line per stage from the pure `utils/proposalCards.js`, remembers scroll per order and chip in sessionStorage, and degrades per the offline law (the worker already allowlists `/api/proposals` by exact path; each query variant is its own cache entry).
- **Phone search**: `client/src/pages/mobile/SearchPhone.js` at `/search` (phone only; desktop width redirects to `/dashboard`), opened by the phone header's magnifier; `AdminLayout` renders it alone in the shell, with no phone header and no tab bar. Reads `GET /admin/search` through `api.get`, never `offlineGet`, so the worker stores no result (its allowlist entry for the path is inert under the opt-in rule); the palette's 200 ms debounce, a 10 s timeout, a superseded-answer guard. The model is the pure `client/src/utils/searchView.js`. The term rides in the `/search` history entry's state so Back from a result returns to it, and `utils/routeRestore.js` never records `/search`. Client and staff rows and every full group's More row open their desktop page for that visit only (`setDesktopForVisit` in `client/src/context/MobileViewContext.js`: held in memory, never stored; the page search opened and the pages opened from it, until the user backs out past it, arrives at the screen afresh, or taps Phone view); the header's own escape stays sticky.
- **Phone event detail**: `client/src/pages/mobile/EventDetailPhone.js` is mounted by a route-level fork inside `client/src/pages/admin/EventDetailPage.js` (same URL, `useMobileView()`, screen key `event-detail`). The fork mounts it KEYED by the event id (`<EventDetailPhone key={id} />`), so another event is a new instance and nothing of the previous event can show on the next. It makes four reads with `offlineGet` (`GET /proposals/:id`, `GET /shifts/by-proposal/:id`, `GET /drink-plans/by-proposal/:id?fields=day_of_contact`, `GET /invoices/proposal/:id`), each allowed to fail alone; of the four, only a dead `GET /proposals/:id` (404 or 403) dispatches `mobile-route-dead`. An id that is not an integer from 1 to 2147483647 is a dead route: it dispatches the same event at once and nothing is fetched. Roster reads are numbered, and an answer goes on screen only when it is newer than what is already there (not merely the newest asked: a newer read that fails must not discard an older one that succeeded). The reload after a sheet write never takes the phone's stored copy, which is the roster from before the write: if it fails, or only a stored copy answers, the roster on screen stays and says it may be out of date, with a Retry that keeps the roster while it reads. The notice stays until an answer asked for AFTER the failed one lands. The assignment sheet is mounted only for a shift that is one of this event's own, once the roster has loaded (a roster is judged only for the event it was read for); a sheet link for any other id is dropped from the URL. The page tells the sheet when it already knows the shift is open (`assignable`), and the Events list does the same for a manual shift, so the staff list is read at once and not a round behind the sheet's own roster. The chrome subscribes to `mobile-route-dead` in a layout effect, so the event a page dispatches from its first commit (a cold load of `/events/abc`) is heard. React takes a layout effect down while a suspended boundary hides the chrome, so the screen also renders its own way back ("This event isn't available", Back to Events) for an event nobody heard. The staleness line reports the oldest cached-at stamp of the four reads, and on a stored copy it offers Refresh, which reads the event again. Two rows follow Financials (lane ma-e3; `NoteRow` and `EditRow` in `EventDetailSections.js`). The Note row shows the first line of the internal booking note, or "Add a note", on every event, past and archived included, and opens the note sheet. The Edit details row (absent on an archived event) reads "date · time · guests" and opens the edit sheet on an upcoming, live event (`editableEvent` in `client/src/utils/editSheetView.js`: not archived or `completed`, and with a live shift not yet `finished` when a roster is on screen, otherwise dated Chicago's today or later), and reads "desktop view" and opens the Desktop view of this screen on a past one. On a cache-served read both rows read "needs connection" and do nothing. Both rows take no tap while held (marked `aria-busy`, their note dimmed), and a `?drawer=edit` or `?drawer=note` deep link opens nothing, until every read the gate depends on has settled (the roster says whether the event has finished, and a drink plan or invoices answer served from the cache after a sheet opened would close it on the edits), so a held tap cannot stack a second history entry; once open, the note sheet latches, so a read that comes back stale later never closes it (it reads and re-reads fresh on its own); an edit or note parameter that cannot open here (another event's id, a past event, a stored copy) is then dropped from the URL. Both sheets push a history entry (`useDrawerParam` with `{ push: true, kinds }`), so Android Back closes them, and their closers act only while the live URL still shows that sheet: a save that settles after Back closed it does not navigate a second time and leave the event. After an edit save the page re-reads the event, the invoices and the roster, never from the stored copy, numbered as the roster reads are, so an older answer or failure that lands late changes nothing; when the re-read fails the page says "Saved. The event below could not be refreshed and may be out of date." with Retry. The sheets themselves are under Edit sheet below. Its view-model is the pure `client/src/utils/eventDetailView.js`, which follows the desktop payment panel (`ProposalDetailPaymentPanel.js`) figure for figure: once the invoices read has landed, the Payments list opens with an "Off-platform" row when `proposals.external_paid` is positive; "No payments yet." is said only when nothing was paid; when the rows add up to less than `amount_paid`, or the invoices read is unavailable, a "Paid to date" row states the total; "Overpaid" comes from the server's `overpayment_cents`, never from paid minus total, and its row says "refund it from desktop view" only when `max_overpayment_refundable_cents` is positive (otherwise "return it by hand", as the desktop panel does); an archived event reads "Cancelled" for `client_cancelled`, `we_cancelled` and a row with no reason, and "Archived" for every other `archive_reason` (`closedWord`), except that an archived event holding an overpayment puts "Overpaid" on the Financials chip (a bank debit in flight still outranks Overpaid on a live event); with the invoices read unavailable, a balance owed gets no chip and reads "Balance", never "Balance due". Its presentational Contacts and Financials sections are `client/src/pages/mobile/EventDetailSections.js`. The date line carries a "No tip jar" chip when the client paid to skip the jar (`tip_jar === false`) and the event is not cancelled. It hands the chrome its rich header (client, kind, guests, venue as a map link) through the outlet context (`setHeaderDetail`). `client/src/hooks/usePhoneHeader.js` holds that header WITH the path it was set for and the chrome draws it only on that path, so one event's header is never on screen over another's; nothing is reset in an effect (a layout-effect reset ran again whenever a suspended boundary hid and revealed the chrome, and wiped the header). Desktop only in phase 1, reached through the Desktop view of this screen: the activity feed, invite to portal, re-enroll nudges, cancel event, cancel line, the Out-of-Area Bonus knob, the drink plan card (the shopping-list Approve), payment actions, the message log, the menu print block, BEO confirmations, the service-extension panel, the "Last-minute: verify staffing" badge, the links to the client page, the proposal page and each staffer's profile, the client's source, the syrups line and package details, "View event details" (the staff BEO view), the plan logo upload, the house menu image, and the waitlist and requests-on-file counts. In the desktop drawer only: equipment and supply-run edits, over-filling a role, and editing a past or cancelled roster.
- **Assignment sheet**: `client/src/components/mobile/AssignmentSheet.js` is the phone ShiftDrawer for one shift, mounted only while open, by the detail (a staffing row or "Assign staff") and by the Events list (a manual shift). It writes through the desktop drawer's endpoints, unchanged: `POST /shifts/:id/assign { user_id, position }` for approve and assign, `PUT /shifts/requests/:id { status: 'denied' }`, `DELETE /shifts/requests/:id`. Its role resolution is the pure `client/src/utils/staffingSheet.js` (`roleStep` answers blocked, direct or pick; `defaultAssignRole` is not imported), and its law is that the phone never over-fills (with no open role, Approve is disabled and the Assign section is absent), never defaults a role, and re-reads the shift, from the network and never from the cache, before EVERY write. The writes and their rules live in `client/src/components/mobile/useSheetWrites.js`. From that re-read: an Approve is sent only while the same request is still pending (not withdrawn, not denied by someone else), and a plain Approve only while that request still resolves to the same single role; an Assign is sent only while the person has no pending request and no place on the roster (a denied request, or an approved one that was dropped, does not block it); a Deny is never sent to a request that is no longer pending; a Remove is never sent for a person who is no longer on the roster (a DELETE would erase a drop record); a person found already on the roster in the role being written means the earlier write landed, so nothing is sent; a request that is gone while its person is back under a new request is refused as a roster change. A cache-served roster, a finished shift and a cancelled event make the sheet read-only. A cache-served STAFF LIST locks the picker only, and says so under the Assign heading: the roster's Approve, Deny and Remove stay live, because none of them reads the staff list. The staff list is read once the roster says a role is open (or at mount, when the owner said so), and not again after a write. Failed saves are held one per row: each is shown under the row it came from with what it was, its own Retry, which repeats exactly that write, and a Dismiss. A failed save leaves the screen in three ways only: its Retry, a new WRITE on the same row, or Dismiss. Opening or closing a row, a confirm, the search field and anything done to another row never remove it. A failure whose row is not on screen (the picker closed, the person left the roster) is shown at the top of the sheet, and a person in the picker who holds one stays in the list whatever the search field says. While a write is in flight the row being written says "Saving", every action is disabled, and the scrim and Escape do not close the sheet; that ends when the WRITE settles, not when the roster has been read again. After a save, and after a refusal (which means the roster moved), the owner is told at once; the roster is then re-read, and if the read after a save fails or only a stored copy answers, the sheet says the save landed and the roster may be out of date, until a fresh roster is on screen by any path. A person the sheet has just assigned is not offered in the picker again, even when that read failed. A pending row that carries both a role and a ranked list that resolves to a different role never takes one tap: it picks. Cover claims: `claim-cover` writes the claimer's own role on the request and writes no ranked list of its own; the sheet and the detail's staffing card read such a row by that role and label it `Covering <name>` (both reads carry `replaced_by_request_id`); it approves through `POST /shifts/:id/assign` like the desktop drawer, which does NOT run the cover cascade, so a swap on the phone is two steps (Remove the original, Approve the claimer). `events_worked` on all three staffing reads comes from `server/utils/staffingMeta.js` (`loadEventsWorked`, pinned to `GET /admin/users/:id/seniority` by test), and so does the picker's WHOLE-mile `home_distance_miles` (`candidateMeta`); an applicant's distance is computed in `server/routes/shifts.js` to the tenth, and the phone rounds every distance it shows to the mile. Both are plain meta: nothing sorts by them, and the picker is alphabetical. The sheet's URL state is `client/src/hooks/useDrawerParam.js`, which replaces history by default; `{ push: true, kinds }` makes a phone sheet push one entry, so Android Back closes it and stays on the page (a sheet reached by deep link or cold route restore gets an entry seeded behind it). `close()` acts once per history entry: a pop lands a moment after it is asked for, and a second call in that moment went back two entries. Its keyboard half is `client/src/hooks/useSheetFocus.js`.
- **Edit sheet**: `client/src/components/mobile/EditSheet.js` is the phone edit sheet for one event (Date, Start with the setup under it, Duration, Guests), mounted only while open, by the detail's Edit details row or a `?drawer=edit` link. It writes through the desktop editor's endpoints, unchanged: `POST /proposals/calculate`, `POST /proposals/:id/notify-preflight`, `PATCH /proposals/:id`. It draws; its reads and writes live in `client/src/components/mobile/useEditSheet.js`, and its view-model is the pure `client/src/utils/editSheetView.js`. Its reads are fresh (`GET /proposals/:id`, `/proposals/packages` and `/proposals/addons` through plain `api.get`, which the admin service worker neither stores nor answers), so a money sheet never opens on a stored copy; every read, the preview and the notify preflight carry a 10 second timeout (`READ_TIMEOUT_MS` in `editSheetView.js`) and no other config, so a request that hangs on weak signal fails into the existing failure line, while the PATCH carries none (a write that timed out can still commit); an event that read finds archived or `completed` is locked ("Use desktop view"). From them it builds the desktop editor's own form state (`initialFormFromProposal`, `recoverAddonQuantities`, and the bartender override and stored gratuity from `editorCore.js`) and sends the desktop editor's complete payload (`buildProposalPatchBody`) with its four fields replaced, because the PATCH reads a missing add-on list as "delete every add-on" and a missing override as "drop it"; `includeVenue: false` leaves out the five venue keys (the sheet never edits location, and sending them can recompose `event_location` on a legacy row and read as a location change), and it never sends the gratuity mandate or writes the client contact. Duration steps in half hours from 1 to 12 and Guests in fives from 1 to 1000, and a stepper never moves a value the wrong way from outside its range (a stored 13 hours after an on-site extension disables "+", where a clamp would lower it). Start is the phone's picker, clamped to 06:00 through 23:30, and picking the time the event already has keeps the stored value as stored, so an unchanged time never reads as a reschedule. The new total comes from the desktop preview's own `POST /proposals/calculate` body (`buildCalculateBody`), asked again as a priced field moves and numbered so a late answer never describes a form it no longer matches. It shows only once a field changed (an untouched sheet says "Done" and closes without a request), and Confirm stays disabled while a figure is on its way or failed: the phone never confirms an unknown total. Its layout is the design pass of 2026-10-06 ("readout above, controls pinned", `docs/design-artifacts/2026-10-06-edit-sheet-layout/`): one readout between the head and the rows. Its copy (the total and its balance line, the reprice lines, the extension hint, the multi-shift note) scrolls past the max height, under a fade while it overflows; under the copy sit the notices ("Couldn't price the change.", a save's line, the changed-meanwhile notice, the curfew confirm, in that order), in a strip that scrolls inside itself only when they alone outgrow the room the rows and footer leave, the curfew confirm's button row then brought into view; the notices, the rows and the footer sit at the bottom of the screen, so when a notice leaves, copy slides into its place and no button moves. The sheet is as tall as its content, at most the screen less 12px; the readout keeps its two top lines (a 58px floor), so the whole sheet scrolls only on a screen too short for the head, those two lines, the notices, the rows and the footer (a phone in landscape in a browser tab). Untouched, the readout shows the stored total, what is paid and the balance (or the server's netted overpaid figure) from the event read ("Total"); after a change, "New total $old → $new" and "balance due becomes $X" in the same two lines, so the first tap changes the height of nothing; while a newer figure is on its way the last one stays, dimmed, under PRICING (before any figure describes the change, three dots, which a screen reader hears as "pending"; the two lines are a polite live region). A changed field shows what it was under its label ("was 3 hr"). The notify step fills the max height, so a channel's message showing or hiding moves nothing. When the detail's invoices read shows a bank debit in flight, the detail passes `inFlight` and the untouched readout reads "paid $X · bank payment in flight". As the sheet closes, however it closes, `holdTaps` (`client/src/utils/tapGuard.js`) lays an invisible layer over the whole screen for the half second, so a double tap's second tap lands on nothing beneath; a strip of scrim thinner than a thumb (44px) above the sheet, as at its max height (12px), does nothing in the edit view (Cancel, Back and Escape still close). With more than one shift on the event (cancelled ones included, as the server's shift sync counts them; it moves a shift only at exactly one), a note says a date or time change does not move them. On a booked event "balance due becomes $X" follows, then the desktop's own reprice lines (`buildRepriceSummary`), the same words on both surfaces. Confirm asks the notify preflight; when it returns a notice, a notify step in the same sheet mirrors the desktop popup through `client/src/components/comms/notifyDrafts.js` (the reasons and the contact on file, any autopay notice, Email and Text ticked as the desktop ticks them, the standard message read-only, "Notify assigned staff" off by default), with Cancel and "Send the update" on the footer's first row and the main button, "Don't send", across its second (last in reading order), so no label wraps on a narrow phone. Each time the sheet swaps the view under the finger (the sheet opening, every change of phase (loading, ready, failed, locked), the notify step opening, and the step going back to the form by Cancel, Escape, the scrim, or a send refused for the curfew or a moved event), the new view's controls are disabled, and the scrim and Escape ignored, for half a second (`armDelayMs`, default 500), because the second tap of a double tap lands on whatever replaced the first tap's target. The sheet being as tall as its content, a phase change moves its top edge, which is why the opening and the phases arm too; after a reload, or a failed load's Retry, whatever it lands on, the sheet takes focus. A change inside a view (a figure landing, a notice or an error line coming or going, a step that withdraws the curfew confirm) is not a swap and arms nothing. One write runs at a time (a ref, so a double tap sends one request), and every PATCH, the notify step's included, re-reads `GET /proposals/:id` first and refuses when its `updated_at` moved since the sheet opened ("This event changed since you opened it.", with Reload, which discards the edits; a read with no `updated_at` on either side counts as moved, so the guard fails closed): an on-site extension that settled while the sheet was open stops the save instead of being written back over (a read before the write, not a lock; the desktop's twin is on the fix list). A past-curfew refusal (`fieldErrors.past_curfew`) opens an inline confirm with the server's reason, "Keep editing" and "Book it anyway", which resends the held body with `acknowledge_past_curfew: true` (when that body carries a client notice or a staff send, a line under the reason says the update goes out with it); any edit while that confirm is open withdraws it, so "Book it anyway" can never send values the sheet no longer shows. Any other refusal shows the server's field text; a transport failure before the PATCH goes out (the re-read, the preflight) reads "No connection, didn't save.", and a PATCH that got no answer reads "No connection. It may not have saved; reopen the event to check.", because it may have landed. What goes wrong after Confirm is scrolled into view and focus moves to it: to the curfew confirm's Keep editing, to the stale notice's Reload, and to a failed save's line, which in the notify step is said above "Notify the client?". The curfew confirm and the stale notice are scrolled into view once, as they appear, and their button takes focus once the view is armed (a held button cannot take focus); the arm's end moves focus only, never the scroll, so a scroll made inside the half second stays put. The notify heading takes focus as the step opens, and Confirm takes it back, once armed, on Cancel, Escape or the scrim. Escape and the scrim in the notify step step back to the edit view with the edits kept, as the desktop popup's Escape and backdrop do, and in the edit view close the sheet. While a save is in flight the button reads "Saving", everything is disabled, and the scrim and Escape do nothing. A save toasts "Event updated." and the per-channel notice outcomes; the page then closes the sheet and re-reads (see Phone event detail). The note sheet, `client/src/components/mobile/NoteSheet.js`, edits the internal booking note (`proposals.admin_notes`: never shown to staff or clients, no money) through `PATCH /proposals/:id/notes`, reading it fresh at open. Every save, "Save mine" included, re-reads the note first and compares it with the note being overwritten; a note that moved shows the changed-meanwhile choice ("This note changed since you opened it.", the note as it now reads, "Discard mine" and "Save mine"), scrolled into view with focus on its copy, keeps the text written, and sends nothing; so does a re-read with no `admin_notes` at all (the compare fails closed). Its reads carry the same timeout, and a PATCH with no answer reads the same "may not have saved" line. Back or the scrim keeps an unsaved draft with the page as `{ text, base }`, `base` being the note it was written over, so a draft reopened after the note moved opens that choice at once; a save clears the draft, and Cancel discards it only once the note has loaded and shown it. Both sheets take their keyboard half from `useSheetFocus`.
- **Inbox (spec 2026-10-06)**: `client/src/pages/admin/Inbox.js` is ONE route, `/inbox/:personKey?`, so the page (its 60 s list poll and the drafts it holds) stays mounted between the list and an opened item. It forks like Events: `InboxPhone` (`client/src/pages/mobile/`) at phone width unless the screen key (`inbox`, or `inbox-item` for an opened person) is pinned to Desktop view, else three panes (people waiting, the thread, the context). Seen is written ONLY for a deliberate open, meaning the URL names the person; the desktop's own pick of the longest wait never writes the URL, so it never marks anyone seen. Reads are always live (`api.get`, never `offlineGet`): a stored copy could hide someone waiting, and a failed list load shows the error card with the last good load (memory plus `localStorage`), never an empty or stale list. The item read resolves a 404 so it can follow `INBOX_MOVED`. Drafts and send ids are per person (`useInboxDrafts`): an id is kept while the server has not settled that send (no answer, 409 `INBOX_SEND_IN_PROGRESS`, or an answer with no code from a limiter or proxy in front of the route) and replaced once it answered anything else, `INBOX_SEND_UNRECORDED` included; Send also ignores a second tap while one is in flight. The FROM hint names `reply.their_line`, the line of their latest inbound text. Undo toasts ride `ToastContext`'s optional `{ action, duration }`; while Inbox is open, and only then, the toast sits under the phone header and is square on House Lights (`:has(.ib-page)` and `:has(.ib-phone)` in `index.css`, pinned by `client/src/utils/toastCss.test.js`). The sidebar and the Inbox tab (first on the phone) draw "!" when `inbox_waiting` is null, and AdminLayout sets it null when the badge fetch fails.

### WebAuthn (Phone admin passkey unlock)
- **Libraries**: `@simplewebauthn/server` (server-side option generation + attestation/assertion verification) and `@simplewebauthn/browser` (client). There is no third-party service here: the "provider" is the phone's own platform authenticator, so there are no keys to rotate and no vendor outage mode.
- **Config**: `WEBAUTHN_RP_ID` and `WEBAUTHN_ORIGIN`, both optional with prod/dev defaults in `server/routes/webauthn.js`. Both are deliberately host-scoped to the admin origin, never the bare domain (see Admin PWA above).
- **Routes**: `server/routes/webauthn.js`, mounted at `/api/auth/webauthn` (full table under "WebAuthn phone unlock"). Six routes, all on `webauthnLimiter`. Challenges are server-issued, DB-stored, single-use, 5-minute TTL.
- **Storage**: `webauthn_credentials` (one row per enrolled device: credential id, public key, signature counter, label, `last_used_at`) and `webauthn_challenges`. No secret material of ours is stored; the private key never leaves the phone.
- **Failure mode**: a passkey that cannot assert is not a lockout. Password login is untouched and still mints the 7d token, so the escape from a broken or lost authenticator is to log in normally and revoke the credential from Settings > Security.

### Thumbtack (Lead Generation)
- **Integration type**: Custom endpoint webhooks (V4 format with legacy fallback)
- **Endpoints**: `server/routes/thumbtack.js` — receives lead, message, and review webhooks
- **Auth**: Shared secret via Basic Auth header or `X-Thumbtack-Secret` custom header (`THUMBTACK_WEBHOOK_SECRET`)
- **Lead flow**: Thumbtack sends lead → webhook creates/matches client (by phone) with `source='thumbtack'` → stores in `thumbtack_leads` → auto-creates an inert draft proposal → emails admin notification
- **Auto-draft**: after lead capture commits, the webhook best-effort calls `createDraftProposalFromLead` (`server/utils/thumbtackProposalDraft.js`), which prices a Core Reaction (BYOB) draft via the shared `server/utils/proposalInsert.js` row builder and links it from `thumbtack_leads.proposal_id`. The draft is inert (`status='draft'`, no invoice, no send), so the admin opens it, adds the email, and clicks Send. A draft failure never rolls back lead capture or 500s the webhook (it is logged, plus Sentry when configured). The admin notification deep-links straight to the draft. `proposals.source` (`'thumbtack'` vs NULL = manual/direct) drives the Proposals dashboard source filter and badge.
- **Important**: Thumbtack does NOT include customer email in webhooks. Admin must grab email manually from Thumbtack (lead → three-dot menu → create estimate/invoice) and add it to the client record.
- **Messages**: Customer messages are persisted in `thumbtack_messages`. The `/api/thumbtack/messages` webhook no longer sends an admin email (Thumbtack notifies the pro directly via app push, SMS, and email through their per-lead proxy-number system rolled out 2026-06-08). Lead and review webhook emails are unchanged.
- **Proxy-relay suppression (inbound SMS)**: Inbound SMS from a phone number that matches a post-rollout `thumbtack_leads.customer_phone` proxy is recorded in `sms_messages` tagged `metadata.thumbtack_relay = true`, fires no admin alerts, and is excluded from the admin Messages endpoints (`GET /api/sms/conversations` and `GET /api/sms/conversations/:clientId`, including their unread counts and threads). Detection fails open: a lookup error logs and routes the message through the normal client path rather than dropping it.
- **Reviews**: Stored in `thumbtack_reviews`, admin notified via email
- **Custom domain**: `api.drbartender.com` CNAME → Render, so Thumbtack endpoints are permanent regardless of hosting changes

### Cal.com

Self-hostable open-source scheduling platform. drb-os receives Cal.com webhooks for consult bookings.

- **Hosting**: V1 uses Cal.com's hosted SaaS. V2 plan migrates to self-hosted Docker on the always-on office box, sharing the same `CAL_*` env vars (only secret + URL change at cutover). Cal.com's open-source codebase means the webhook payload shape is identical between hosted and self-hosted.
- **Endpoint**: `POST /api/calcom/webhook`. Mounted at `server/routes/calcom.js`. Bare HTTP semantics (no AppError JSON envelopes) matching the Stripe and Resend webhook patterns.
- **Signature scheme**: HMAC-SHA256 over the raw body, secret = `CAL_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, header `x-cal-signature-256`. Fails closed: handler returns 503 if secret unset, 400 on missing or invalid signature.
- **Replay protection**: SHA-256 of the raw signed body recorded in the `webhook_events` table. Same body delivered twice (legitimate Cal.com retry on a 5xx, OR attacker replay) returns 200 'Already processed' without side effects. One exception, the strand-heal: an identical CREATE or RESCHEDULE whose uid no consult has ever carried (current or prior) drops the dedupe row and is reprocessed, because that shape means the first delivery died before writing its consult.
- **Events handled**: `BOOKING_CREATED` (auto-creates a `clients` row if booker email doesn't match an existing client, links to most recent non-terminal proposal if any), `BOOKING_CANCELLED` (defensive upsert), `BOOKING_RESCHEDULED` (in-place update with fallback to fresh-create), `BOOKING_NO_SHOW_UPDATED` (mirrors Cal.com's manual no-show marking). Other event types are logged + 200 OK so Cal.com does not retry.
- **Side effects**: the booking-filing path sends NO admin SMS on any booking event, and no admin email either. Cal.com itself owns booking notification (it emails the organizer and syncs the event into the organizer's Google Calendar), so drb-os silently files the booking into the `consults` table for status tracking, suppression queries (drink-plan nudge), and audit. The exception is the consult call bridge below, which is a FAULT path rather than a booking path: its tail can send a single admin email when the booker's number is undialable (bounded to one per rolling 24 hours), or when an unresolvable reschedule stopped one or more of the booker's other upcoming consults (bounded to one per booker email per Chicago day). It never sends SMS, because `notifyAdminCategory` texts only when a caller passes `smsBody` and `sendChainEmail` never does.
- **Booker phone capture** (spec 2026-08-25): `extractPhone` reads the booker's typed number from the four places Cal.com puts it (`attendees[0].phoneNumber` / `.phone`, `responses.phone`, `customInputs.phone`, unwrapping the `{ value, label }` form-field shape), `normalizeBooker` trims it and caps it at `MAX_PHONE_LEN` 50, and `BOOKING_CREATED` stores that in `consults.booker_phone` without any format normalization. It is the ONLY number the consult call bridge ever dials, and it is attacker-supplied, because the booking page is public: nothing dials it until `toUsE164` accepts it, at chain open and again at press-1. `BOOKING_RESCHEDULED` writes it `COALESCE($6, booker_phone)`, so a reschedule payload that carries no number keeps what the booker gave originally, and the tail then reads every field off the RETURNING row rather than the payload.
- **Release before the tail**: `handleCreated` holds a pooled client for its transaction and calls `client.release()` immediately after `COMMIT`, before `consultCallTail` runs. The tail uses bare `pool.query`, so holding the client across it would be one request occupying a connection while waiting for a second one, the pool-deadlock pattern that has bitten this codebase twice. A `released` flag keeps the `catch` from issuing a `ROLLBACK` on a client the pool may already have handed to someone else, and the tail runs inside a swallow-everything wrapper so the webhook's 200/503 semantics never change. Only a WON insert (`rowCount === 1`) reaches the tail: losing the `ON CONFLICT` race means the winner's own tail covers that consult.
- **What the tail files**: `consultCallTail` deliberately opens NO chain (a consult booked three weeks out would leave a pending row sitting for three weeks; the sweep opens it five minutes before the slot). It does only the three things that only the webhook knows. When a reschedule named an old booking uid we could not resolve, it marks the booker's OTHER future scheduled consults `skipped_cancelled` / `rescheduled_unresolved` and sends the one `unresolved reschedule` email (at most one per booker email per Chicago day), and that runs BEFORE the phone check (ruling R3) so a moved slot is stopped even when the new number turns out to be undialable. When `toUsE164` refuses the number it files the terminal `skipped_invalid_phone` row (`invalid_phone` vs `no_phone`), emailing at most once per rolling 24h. And on a reschedule it DELETEs any `skipped%` row standing at the consult's CURRENT slot, derived in SQL, so a re-taken slot is not blocked by a stale skip; rows at the old slot carry a different `scheduled_at` and survive as history.
- **Prior uids**: a reschedule renames `calcom_event_id`, so the webhook keeps the outgoing uid in `consults.calcom_prior_event_ids`. The strand-heal reprocesses only a uid no consult has ever carried; `handleCreated` answers `Already filed` for any known uid; `handleRescheduled` answers `Already rescheduled` when its NEW uid is a prior one, or the current one at the same slot (a same-uid move to a new time still goes through), and resolves its OLD uid against the prior lists too; `handleCancelled` answers `Booking already moved` for a uid that is only a prior one. A reschedule whose old uid resolves nowhere files a fresh consult that records the unmatched old uid(s) as prior, so their late CREATE is `Already filed` too. The `Already rescheduled` and `Booking already moved` answers each log the uid.
- **Completion**: the linked consults row flips to `'completed'` when admin submits the existing consult form in `server/routes/drinkPlanConsult.js`. Side effect of the existing user action; no UI change.
- **Deferred to V2 (when self-hosted)**: writing the drb-os event URL directly into Cal.com's `booking.description` via direct DB access, so the link appears in the organizer's Google Calendar entry. Today admin opens drb-os manually after seeing Cal.com's notification.

### Cloudflare R2 (File Storage)
- **Wrapper**: `server/utils/storage.js`
- **Upload flow**: Validate file (magic bytes) → Upload to R2 bucket → Store key in DB → Generate signed URL (15-min expiry) for downloads
- **Two validators, deliberately different widths** (`server/utils/fileValidation.js`):
  - `isValidUpload` — PDF, JPEG, PNG, WebP. The narrow default, used by the W-9 (`payment.js`), blog images, staff-portal uploads, and BOTH headshot fields (a headshot must stay a renderable image).
  - `isValidOnboardingDocument` — additionally accepts HEIC/HEIF and DOC/DOCX. Used ONLY for the resume and alcohol certification on the two onboarding forms. These files are stored in R2 and opened by an admin through a signed URL, never rendered publicly, so an iPhone photo or a Word doc is legitimate; rejecting them stranded a recruit (2026-07-23). Container formats (DOC/DOCX) are accepted only when the magic bytes AND the filename extension agree, so PK magic alone never admits an arbitrary `.zip`.
  - The client mirrors these two sets as `UPLOAD_KINDS.narrow` / `UPLOAD_KINDS.document` in `client/src/utils/uploadLimits.js`, checked at pick time. The client must never accept more than its destination validator, or the user waits through an upload and is rejected at the end.
- **Size limit**: `MAX_FILE_SIZE` (default 10MB) enforced by `express-fileupload` in `server/index.js` with `abortOnLimit: true`, plus a `limitHandler` that reports `upload_limit_exceeded` to Sentry. Installing `limitHandler` means express-fileupload answers through it and its own `closeConnection` early-returns on `res.headersSent`, so the socket is not reset and the JSON 413 arrives readable (the stock config reset the stream and the browser reported a bare "network error"). The remaining body drains rather than being cut off, which matches what `express.json` has always done for an over-limit body and is bounded by Node's 300s `requestTimeout`. The primary defence is still the client-side check, which rejects before a byte is sent; images over ~1.5MB are downscaled in the browser first (`client/src/utils/downscaleImage.js`) and rarely reach the limit at all.
- **Files stored**: W-9, resume, headshot, alcohol certification, BASSET certification, bartender tip-page photos, menu art layers and references (`menu-art/<proposalId>/`)
- **Admin access**: `GET /api/files/:filename` redirects to signed URL (admin/manager only)
- **Server-side reads**: `storage.readFile(key)` returns an object's `{ buffer, contentType }` through a signed URL, bounded at 8 s and 25 MB (a 404 is a `NotFoundError`, anything else an `ExternalServiceError`). The menu art job reads references and backgrounds with it before sending them to a provider, and `server/utils/r2Proxy.js` (`sendR2Object`) serves the menu art image routes from it.

### Google Places (Venue Search)
- **Wrapper**: `server/utils/googlePlaces.js` — server-mediated proxy over the Google Places (New) API powering the proposal venue-name typeahead (autocomplete + place details → structured venue address). Fails soft (returns `[]`/`null`, never throws) so the venue-name field degrades to a plain text input when `GOOGLE_PLACES_API_KEY` is unset or Google is unreachable. The pure `mapPlaceToVenue` mapper drops out-of-area addresses, keeping only `VENUE_STATES` matches.

### Anthropic (Inbox AI read)
- Described with the Inbox, under its route table: the one client (`server/utils/anthropicClient.js`, `@anthropic-ai/sdk` 0.131.0), what is redacted before anything is sent, the read job and its caps, and the status. Env: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `INBOX_AI_MODEL`, `INBOX_AI_DAILY_CAP`, `RUN_INBOX_READ_SCHEDULER`.

### Gmail (Inbox email feed, spec 2026-10-14)
- **Client**: `server/utils/gmailClient.js` is the only module that talks to Google for the feed. No SDK: it signs a service-account JWT with `node:crypto` (`sub` = the business mailbox, a code constant; scope `gmail.readonly` only), exchanges it at the pinned token host and calls the pinned Gmail REST host with `fetch`, 20 s per call, no retries (the caller's next tick retries). One client per key, so the access token is reused until a minute before expiry. Every method resolves `{ data }` or `{ error }`; an error carries a class (`GmailAuthError`, `GmailHistoryGoneError`, `GmailNotFoundError` for one message gone before it was fetched, `GmailUnavailableError`, `GmailRequestError`), the HTTP status (0 when no HTTP response came back) and the stage that failed (`token` or `api`), never a response body. Concurrent callers share one token request. Under `NODE_ENV=test` it refuses to build a real client; suites inject a stub.
- **Credential**: `GMAIL_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY` (base64 JSON). The service account holds Workspace domain-wide delegation, so the key can read every mailbox in the Workspace; which mailbox it reads is therefore fixed in code, never env. Leak response: delete the key in Google Cloud and remove the delegation row in the Workspace admin console.
- **Probe**: `scripts/gmail-probe.js`, run once by hand with the key in that command's environment, prints the address it authenticated as, the label count and whether the Primary category label exists, and whether the key is ready for Render (exit 0 only when it reads the business mailbox and the Primary label exists); on failure it names the refusing stage with a one-line hint. The only Gmail call allowed from the dev box. Keep the downloaded JSON in `~/.secrets/` (chmod 600) and delete it once the value is in Render.
- **Caller**: the `gmail_sync` scheduler (`server/utils/inbox/gmailSync.js`, every 60 s, `RUN_GMAIL_SYNC_SCHEDULER`), described with the Inbox under its route table, is the one caller. `INBOX_OWN_EMAILS` adds addresses that are ours (the older Gmail address of the business) to the mailbox, its aliases and every operator's address; with the mailbox and its personal alias it is also what the Zola gate accepts as addressed to us, which an operator's own address is not; and with the mailbox, the no-reply address and `ADMIN_EMAIL` it is the business addresses whose presence in To or Cc makes mail at the personal alias business (`people.businessEmails`). Nothing in the OS sends through Gmail.

### OpenAI (menu art words and images, spec 2026-10-01)
- **Client**: `server/utils/openaiClient.js` is the only module that requires `openai` (pinned 7.34.0). The key is passed explicitly and the API host pinned (`OPENAI_BASE_URL` cannot redirect client images); organization, project, admin key and webhook secret are nulled so no env var can scope or bill the call elsewhere; SDK logging is off; `maxRetries: 0` and explicit per-call timeouts (`MENU_ART_IMAGE_TIMEOUT_MS`, default 180000; text 60000). It refuses every call while `OPENAI_CUSTOM_HEADERS` is set, and under `NODE_ENV=test` unless a test injected a stub (`__setTestStub`). Exports `isConfigured`, `draftMenuCopy` (Responses API, strict JSON schema, `store: false`), `generateImage` and `editImage`, each returning the payload plus OpenAI's request id.
- **Callers**: the menu art job's words step (always OpenAI, `MENU_ART_TEXT_MODEL`) and `server/utils/menuImageProvider.js` for OpenAI pieces (`MENU_ART_IMAGE_MODEL`: the background at `MENU_ART_BACKGROUND_SIZE`, high quality, JPEG; a drink at 1024x1024, transparent PNG). The route table, the job and the cap are described under "Menu art drafts".
- **Payload rule**: only the event type label, the three brief fields, drink names, catalog descriptions and ingredients, client-typed drink names, the art direction and re-roll notes, the reference image and the generated background. Never the client's name, email, phone, venue, address or event date.
- **Errors**: only a sanitized descriptor (class, HTTP status, request id, the API's error code) reaches Sentry, `menu_draft_pieces.error`, `menu_art_calls.error` or `words_error`, never the SDK message, which can quote the request. A moderation refusal fails the piece with a readable reason.
- **Credential and spend**: `OPENAI_API_KEY`; unset means the feature is off (503 `MENU_ART_NOT_CONFIGURED`). A full eight-drink menu is estimated at about $0.55 to $0.65 and the 60-image cap bounds a worst day at about $15; a monthly budget on the OpenAI project is the provider-side backstop. Dev and prod share one key, so they share OpenAI's per-minute limit; each database keeps its own cap.

### Google Gemini (optional menu art image model, spec 2026-10-01)
- **Client**: `server/utils/geminiClient.js` is the only module that requires `@google/genai` (pinned 2.30.0). One client with `GEMINI_API_KEY` passed explicitly, the Gemini API host pinned and the Vertex backend off; the Interactions API (`ai.interactions.create`) with `store: false`, no `response_format.delivery` (the live API rejects any delivery value on image output; the image comes back inline without it), and `{ maxRetries: 0, timeout }` on each call. With `store: false` the API returns no response id, so Gemini ledger rows carry a null `provider_request_id`. It refuses every call while `GOOGLE_GENAI_DEBUG` (the SDK would log the key and the images) or `GOOGLE_GENAI_USER_PROJECT` (it would move the billing) is set, and under `NODE_ENV=test` without an injected stub. Exports `isConfigured` and `generateImage`, which accepts exactly one output image; a blocked prompt, a 200 with status `failed` or `incomplete`, or an answer with no image becomes one `GeminiRefusal` with a readable reason.
- **Caller**: `server/utils/menuImageProvider.js` only, for pieces stamped `gemini` (`MENU_ART_GEMINI_IMAGE_MODEL`, default `gemini-3-pro-image`, the only supported id): a background at 4:5 / 4K with the references as style inputs, downscaled with `sharp` to 2560x3200 JPEG; a drink at 1:1 / 1K on flat `#FF00FF`, keyed to a transparent PNG by `server/utils/menuKeyOut.js` (too little key color fails the piece: "Gemini didn't leave a clean background. Re-roll it."). The words step never uses Gemini.
- **Credential and spend**: `GEMINI_API_KEY`; unset leaves the toggle's Gemini option disabled (503 `MENU_ART_GEMINI_NOT_CONFIGURED`) and OpenAI working. In Render it must come from a billing-enabled AI Studio project (paid tier) with a hard ceiling, preferably separate from the second-opinion reviewer's project, since Gemini's limits are per project. A full eight-drink menu is about $1.31; the shared 60-image cap bounds a worst day at about $14.40. BLAST RADIUS: the key can draw images billed to that project. Leak response: delete the key in AI Studio and unset it in Render; OpenAI keeps working.

### QR Code Rendering (`qrcode.react`)
- Client-only dependency used by `client/src/pages/staff/tipCard/SignLayout.jsx`, `BizCardLayout.jsx` and `PhoneSignLayout.jsx` to render the bartender's tip-page URL as a QR code on the downloadable bar sign, the hand-out card and the full-screen phone display, and by `TipCardPage.js` for the on-screen preview. These use `QRCodeCanvas`, not `QRCodeSVG`, deliberately: html2canvas is far more reliable capturing a canvas, and the print renders become 300-DPI files a bartender prints. Three settings are load-bearing: `level="Q"` (signage-grade error correction for an object that gets handled and set down in wet places), `marginSize={0}`, and a 2x `size` displayed at 1x via `style` (qrcode.react sizes the canvas backing store to `size * devicePixelRatio`, so a 1x QR on a 1x display is a bitmap html2canvas then upscales, putting 150 DPI of real information inside a 300 DPI file). No server side.
- **The quiet zone is the plate band, not `marginSize`.** qrcode.react defaults `marginSize` to ZERO, so the redesign moved the clear space out of the SVG and into the cream plate that surrounds the code: `SIGN_QR` / `CARD_QR` in `sizes.js` size the `plate`/`pad` pair, and `pad` must never fall below 4 modules. Setting a non-zero `marginSize` on top of that does not add safety, it doubles the quiet zone and shrinks the code inside a fixed plate. The two numbers in each `*_QR` constant are a pair and move together; `sizes.js` carries the arithmetic for the current 37x37-module tip URL.

## Deployment Architecture

```
┌──────────────┐     push to main      ┌─────────────────────────┐        ┌────────────────────┐
│   GitHub     │ ────────────────────► │  Render (Backend)       │ ─────► │  Neon (PostgreSQL) │
│   main       │                       │  - Express server       │        └────────────────────┘
│              │                       │  - Auto-deploy on push  │
│              │     push to main      │                         │
│              │ ────────────────────► ├─────────────────────────┤
└──────────────┘                       │  Vercel (Frontend)      │
                                       │  - React SPA            │
                                       │  - Auto-deploy on push  │
                                       └──────────┬──────────────┘
                                                   │
                                       ┌───────────▼──────────────┐
                                       │  External Services       │
                                       │  - Stripe (payments)     │
                                       │  - Resend (email)        │
                                       │  - Twilio (SMS)          │
                                       │  - Cloudflare R2 (files) │
                                       └──────────────────────────┘
```

- **Backend**: Render web service (`os`), auto-deploys from `main`; created in the dashboard, not from a Blueprint (settings in `docs/dev.md`). Schema auto-runs on startup.
- **Frontend**: Vercel static site. `vercel.json` rewrites all paths to `index.html` for client-side routing. `REACT_APP_API_URL` set at build time.
- **Database**: Neon PostgreSQL (the project and its branches are in `docs/dev.md`). Schema initialization is idempotent: `schema.sql` uses `IF NOT EXISTS` and `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` throughout.
- **No manual deploy step**: push to `main` triggers both Render and Vercel builds automatically.

## Dev Tooling & Code Quality

### ESLint + Security Plugin
- **Config**: `eslint.config.mjs` (ESLint v10 flat config)
- **Plugin**: `eslint-plugin-security` — flags SQL injection patterns, unsafe regex, object injection sinks, eval usage
- **Rules**: `eqeqeq` (strict equality), `no-eval`, `no-implied-eval`, `no-new-func`, `prefer-const`, `require-await`
- **Run**: `npm run lint` (check) or `npm run lint:fix` (auto-fix)

### Pre-Commit Hooks (Husky + lint-staged)
- **Hook**: `.husky/pre-commit` runs six steps in order; the docs check and the CLAUDE.md check read the index git is committing from (the temporary index under a pathspec commit, the private copy under a lane merge), never the working tree, and the CLAUDE.md check's manifest comes from HEAD and from that same index, while the check script itself, the CSS check's class harvest and lint-staged read the working tree as before; every step but the fourth refuses the commit:
  1. `node scripts/gen-docs.js --check --staged`: renders the six generated sections of `README.md` and `ARCHITECTURE.md` from the index and compares them with the staged docs, and checks the header rule over the staged added and modified source files. It prints one line per problem (`marker:`, `stale:`, `unparsed:`, `no header:`) and one `run:` line naming what to do, usually `npm run docs -- --staged`; `docs/dev.md`'s Docs section says what each line means.
  2. When `.claude/CLAUDE.md` is staged (`git diff --cached --name-only --no-renames`): its staged blob (`git show :.claude/CLAUDE.md`) is written to a temp file and checked by `bash scripts/check-claudemd-invariants.sh <tmp> --additive-against HEAD` against `scripts/claudemd-invariants.txt`; a missing invariant, a manifest in the committing index that removes or changes a key HEAD's manifest has (a commit that stages `CLAUDE.md` may only add keys; a pattern change lands in its own commit), or the file staged for deletion, refuses. The temp file is removed either way.
  3. `node scripts/check-file-size.js --staged`: the file-size ratchet (soft cap 700, hard cap 1000; blocks only a commit that grows an over-cap file).
  4. `node scripts/check-css-palette-scope.js --staged`: the CSS palette-scope checker, a no-op unless `client/src/index.css` is staged. **Warn-only on purpose** (`|| true`, 2026-08-14) while the parser burns in: it is a brand-new 600-line CSS parser and `index.css` is the largest file in the repo, so a false positive here would cost the author a `--no-verify` that ALSO skips the file-size ratchet, lint-staged, and `guard-os-main`. It prints, it does not veto. The same script exits non-zero under `npm run check:css-scope`, so CI can gate on it and the hook can be promoted to blocking later.
  5. `npx lint-staged`: ESLint on staged `server/**/*.js` only (fast, not the full codebase); blocks on errors, allows warnings.
  6. `bash scripts/guard-os-main.sh`: the os-stays-on-main guard; blocks any commit from the primary `os` worktree while it is off `main`, and any `docs/superpowers/specs|plans/` doc committed off `main`.
- **Workflow tooling** (think-on-main / build-in-lanes, not all hook-invoked): `scripts/guard-os-main.sh` (above), `scripts/merge-lane.sh` (flock'd squash-merge wrapper, run only in `os`), `scripts/lane-status.js` (open-lane + stale-lane detection, `npm run lane:status`), `scripts/board-write.sh` (atomic `docs/build-board.md` writer with a PII/Stripe-id denylist; its push is **guarded** and skipped entirely unless the board commit is the only unpushed commit on `main`, because `git push origin main` is a branch push and would otherwise turn a board line into an uncued production deploy of unrelated work — it nearly shipped 49 commits that way on 2026-08-14), `scripts/sensitive-paths.txt` + `scripts/sensitive-match.js` (the one sensitive-path list and its matcher, the single trigger for review-scaling, conflict-escalation, and auto-pull disqualification), and `scripts/check-claudemd-invariants.sh` + `scripts/claudemd-invariants.txt` (paired keyword/regex coverage check over `CLAUDE.md`). Most carry a co-located `*.test.js` run by `npm test` (`node --test`).

### Mobile Verification Harness
- **Run**: `npm run mobile:check` (`scripts/mobile-capture.js` + `scripts/mobile-capture.manifest.json`)
- Dev-only phone-viewport (390x844) screenshot + horizontal-overflow probe over every client-facing surface via headless system Chrome (`playwright-core`); refuses to run against production (`NODE_ENV`/DB-host gate) and writes to the gitignored `/mobile-audit/`. Merge gate for the mobile-fixes lanes (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-02-mobile-fixes-design.md`).

### Claude Code agents, commands and skills

Generated by `npm run docs` from the front matter of `.claude/agents/*.md`, `.claude/commands/*.md` and `.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md`.

<!-- gen:agents -->
Generated by npm run docs; do not edit between the markers.

| Agent | Model | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `access-egress` | opus | Access and egress lens. Fires on a router handler, an auth or role check, a limiter, a log or Sentry call, a send or AI client call, a response, a webhook, a public token route, an export, or any path under server/routes or server/middleware. |
| `business-time` | opus | Business-time lens. Fires on CURRENT_DATE or NOW, new Date, a date cast or INTERVAL, AT TIME ZONE, a date helper or formatter, or a threshold in days. |
| `client` | opus | Client lens. Fires on any path under client/src and on the client tier floor, and judges the captures at 320 and 390 wide that the main session provides. |
| `concurrency` | opus | Concurrency lens. Fires on FOR UPDATE, pool.connect, a transaction statement, ON CONFLICT, Promise.all, rowCount, a retry, a timer, a status write, a provider call that moves money or sends, or a scheduler file. |
| `lifecycle` | opus | Lifecycle lens. Fires on a status write, a status constant, an archive reason, a pay-period predicate, a setInterval, or a scheduler, sweep or reaper file. |
| `money` | opus | Money lens. Fires on an UPDATE proposals SET, a money column, a Stripe money call or a pricing or refund helper in the diff, and on any path in the money section of the sensitive list. |
| `outcomes-providers` | opus | Outcomes and providers lens. Fires on a catch, an ok flag, a skipped or failed status, a process.env read, TwiML, a webhook, a COMMIT, a provider SDK require, a post or fetch, a page-driving step, or the Thumbtack agent paths. |
| `performance-review` | opus | Performance review, explicit-only. Runs on request when a public page or a list endpoint changes, never by the review skill, and reads queries, payload size and client fetch patterns. |
| `plan-decomposition` | opus | Design-stage reviewer. Verifies an implementation plan (a lane map without code, spec D12) is decomposed into lanes that can each be built, reviewed, tested and reverted on their own (one logical feature per lane, the eight lane-map fields declared, runnable acceptance checks, no forward or circular dependencies). |
| `plan-fidelity` | opus | Design-stage reviewer. Verifies an implementation plan (a lane map without code, spec D12) implements its spec with no scope creep and no scope drop. Every spec requirement maps to a lane; every lane traces back to the spec; every lane declares the eight lane-map fields. |
| `schema-data` | opus | Schema and data lens. Fires on DDL, a catalog insert, or a path in schema.sql, db/index.js, server/scripts, scripts, or anything named seed. |
| `seams` | opus | Seams lens. Runs on every non-docs tier floor and has no trigger of its own; reads a changed field, response shape, enumeration, entrance, a helper with copies, or client-facing copy. |
| `spec-gaps` | opus | Design-stage reviewer. Surfaces flows the spec glosses over (loading / empty / error UI, null handling, refund / cancellation, race conditions, migration of existing rows, backwards-compat, server vs client validation parity, observability). |
| `spec-grounding` | opus | Design-stage reviewer. Verifies a spec is grounded in the actual current codebase (named files, functions, routes, columns exist; "we'll mirror X" patterns hold; CLAUDE.md invariants respected; cross-cutting consumers enumerated). |
| `spec-risk` | opus | Design-stage reviewer. Pre-flights the design through the high-stakes lenses: money math, auth and access control, data integrity, side effects, webhook safety, secrets, PII, graceful degradation. |
| `verify` | fable | Verify pass on a tier A review. An adversarial read of each fix and of the money math in the diff, returning findings only, never a verdict table. |

| Command | Description |
|---|---|
| `/full-audit` | Quarterly whole-codebase audit, explicit-only. Three lenses (seams, access-egress, schema-data), a sensitive-list check and a lens coverage re-run over the defects fixed since the last audit, a Sentry cross-reference, and no external pass. Report-only, no fixes. |
| `/review-plan` | Design-stage review of an implementation plan (a lane map without code) via 2 specialized Claude agents (plan-fidelity, plan-decomposition) in parallel. |
| `/review-spec` | Design-stage review of a spec via 3 specialized Claude agents (spec-grounding, spec-gaps, spec-risk) in parallel. |

| Skill | Description |
|---|---|
| `review` | The per-lane review that writes a receipt (spec 2026-10-19 section 3). Run it before every lane merge, on a staged quick fix before its commit, and on a commit already on main with no receipt. Trigger words review, review the lane, run the review, per-lane review, review receipt, quick-fix review, review --commit, properly reviewed. |

<!-- /gen:agents -->

See `CLAUDE.md` > Review model (when the fleet runs, the iron rule, chunk-and-retry) and `CLAUDE.md` > Design-stage review fleet.

## Operational Practices

### Weekly dispute-email-bailout sweep

The dispute-won email-retry bailout (see `server/utils/payrollDisputeNotify.js`) writes `tips.dispute_email_failed_at = NOW()` when it permanently abandons a notification after three send failures. The Sentry alert accompanying the bailout is best-effort: a process crash between commit and the Sentry call, or a Sentry transport failure, can lose the alert silently while the DB row carries the canonical marker.

Run this query weekly to catch any abandonments that did not reach Sentry:

```sql
SELECT id, dispute_email_failed_at, amount_cents, shift_id, target_user_id
  FROM tips
 WHERE dispute_email_failed_at IS NOT NULL
 ORDER BY dispute_email_failed_at DESC;
```

For each row, follow the manual recovery runbook in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-25-dispute-email-retry-bailout-design.md`. Before posting any adjustment, search `proposal_activity_log` by `tip_id` to avoid double-paying bartenders (the `Promise.race` timeout in `notifyDisputeWon` aborts the awaiter but does not cancel the in-flight Resend request, so the email may have actually delivered server-side even when the function treated it as a failure).
